\section{Structure of the proof}
\label{ch:proof-structure}

This chapter records the main transformations of~\cite{JNVWY20} at headline level: for
each, what it does, what it relies on, and one theorem statement so that the dependency
graph reflects the architecture. The conditional assembly, its parameter arithmetic
and the ambient introspection construction for seven-level inputs are formalized.
Supplying answer reduction remains a main task of the project. These soundness
analyses use the bipartite framework.
The paper factors these transformations through \emph{typed} verifiers and detypes at
specified boundaries (Remark~\ref{rem:typed-detyping}); the ambient Lean hypotheses
also require the runtime adaptation of Remark~\ref{rem:runtime-normalization}.

Following Lin~\cite{Lin25}, every soundness statement below is in \emph{value form}: a
transformation preserves the property ``value at most $1 - \eps$'' up to a quantitative
loss, and compression preserves ``value at most $\frac12$''. The three inner
transformations, repetition and compression all carry that property in $\valstar$, the
bipartite tensor-product value: it is the class the compressibility criterion is
instantiated with and the form Definition~\ref{def:mipstar} requires
(Remark~\ref{rem:compression-abstract}), and no passage between the two values occurs
anywhere on the path. The synchronous value is not used, and neither is the
almost-synchronous transport of Theorem~\ref{thm:almost-sync}; see
Remark~\ref{rem:sync-invariant} for why that is the better arrangement and
Remark~\ref{rem:bipartite-route} for the evidence. No entanglement lower bound is tracked. Remark~\ref{rem:direct-vs-anchored} explains why none is needed,
Section~\ref{sec:rr-compression} states the abstract lemma (Lin's compressibility
criterion) that turns value-form compression into the halting reduction, and the
entanglement clauses of~\cite{JNVWY20} are recorded for reference in
Remark~\ref{rem:entanglement-form}.

\subsection{Conditionally linear functions and samplers}
\label{sec:ps-cl}

Question distributions must be structured enough that players can sample them
\emph{on their own} in a certifiable way (introspection), yet expressive enough to
implement all the verifiers in the pipeline. The right class is that of conditionally
linear distributions: linear maps applied in stages, where each stage may depend on
the output of the previous ones.

\begin{definition}[Conditionally linear function]
\label{def:cl-function}
\ledgernode{1.1.2}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.CLFun, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.eval, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.SupportedOn, MIPRE.CL.IsCLFun,
  MIPRE.CL.clDist}
\leanok
Let $V = \F^n$ for a finite field $\F$ and $n \geq 0$. A \emph{register subspace} of $V$ is
the span of a subset of the standard basis; for a register subspace $W$ and $x \in V$, $x^W$
denotes the projection of $x$ onto $W$ along the complementary register subspace. For
$\ell \geq 0$, the \emph{$\ell$-level conditionally linear (CL) functions} on $V$ are defined
inductively: the only $0$-level CL function is the zero function on $V$; for $\ell \geq 1$, an
$\ell$-level CL function is a function $L : V \to V$ of the form
\begin{equation*}
  L(x) = L_1\bigl(x^{V_1}\bigr) + L_{>1,\, L_1(x^{V_1})}\bigl(x^{V_{>1}}\bigr)
\end{equation*}
for complementary register subspaces $V = V_1 \oplus V_{>1}$, a linear map $L_1 : V_1 \to
V_1$, and, for each $v$ in the range of $L_1$, an $(\ell-1)$-level CL function $L_{>1,v}$ on
$V_{>1}$. A \emph{CL distribution} is the distribution of $(L^\alice(x), L^\bob(x))$ for
uniformly random $x \in V$ and CL functions $L^\alice, L^\bob$ on $V$.
\end{definition}

\paragraph{Comments.} This is the paper's definition (\texttt{linear.tex},
\texttt{def:cl-func}); the paraphrase this blueprint carried before started at level $1$ and
let $V_1 \oplus V_{>1}$ be any direct sum decomposition, both departures from the paper. Two
things about the formalization. Every consumer of the definition --- the structure lemma
below, the query interface of a sampler, the hiding tests of introspection --- works with the
decomposition data $(V_1, L_1, L_{>1,\cdot})$, which the function does not determine; so the
Lean object is a \emph{presentation}, \texttt{CLFun}, an inductive type indexed by the level,
with \texttt{CLFun.eval} the function it presents and \texttt{IsCLFun} the paper's notion
(some presentation on $V$ evaluates to the function). And presentations live on one ambient
space $\F^\iota$ whose register subspaces are sets of coordinates; ``a presentation on $V_T$''
is the predicate \texttt{CLFun.SupportedOn}, under which the paper's $V_{>1}$ is the
complement of $V_1$ inside $V_T$. The continuations $L_{>1,v}$ are indexed by all $v$ rather
than by the range of $L_1$; the extra ones are never evaluated. The distribution is
\texttt{clDist}, in the real-valued format of Definition~\ref{def:game}.

\begin{definition}[Orthogonal and canonical complements]
\label{def:cl-canonical}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.coordSub, MIPRE.CL.dotForm, MIPRE.CL.perp, MIPRE.CL.mem_perp,
  MIPRE.CL.tail, MIPRE.CL.dimTail, MIPRE.CL.pivots, MIPRE.CL.mem_pivots,
  MIPRE.CL.canonCompl, MIPRE.CL.canonLin, MIPRE.CL.lperp, MIPRE.CL.sub_canonLin_mem}
\leanok
Let $V = \F^n$ with its standard basis. Two complements of a subspace $S \leq V$ are needed,
and they are different.

The \emph{orthogonal complement} $S^\perp = \{u : u \cdot v = 0 \text{ for all } v \in S\}$
for the standard dot product. Over a finite field it need \emph{not} be a complement of $S$:
$\mathrm{span}\{(1,1)\}$ over $\F_2$ is orthogonal to itself.

The \emph{canonical complement}, which is one. Write $u_j = \dim\bigl(S \cap
\mathrm{span}\{\hat e_i : i \geq j\}\bigr)$; the \emph{pivot set} of $S$ is
$J = \{j : u_{j+1} < u_j\}$, and the canonical complement of $S$ is
$\mathrm{span}\{\hat e_j : j \notin J\}$. The \emph{canonical linear map with kernel $S$}
is the projector onto the canonical complement parallel to $S$. Finally, for a linear map $L$,
$L^\perp$ is the canonical linear map with kernel $(\ker L)^\perp$.
\end{definition}

\paragraph{Source.} \texttt{preliminaries.tex}: the orthogonal complement and
\texttt{lem:perp\_perp}; \texttt{def:canonical-complement} with \texttt{rk:cancomp};
\texttt{def:cl-canonical}; \texttt{def:Lperp} with \texttt{lem:L\_perp\_perp}. Among the
facts ledger node $1.1.6$ lists as ``subfield/trace facts as used''.

\paragraph{Comments.} The paper defines the canonical complement through a canonical Gaussian
elimination: reduce the matrix whose rows are a basis of $S$ to reduced row echelon form and
take the standard basis vectors off the pivot columns. The formalization uses instead the
equivalent characterization \texttt{rk:cancomp} credits to a referee --- the dimension
sequence $u_j$ above --- and that choice is what makes the construction usable. It needs no
echelon form, and, more importantly, it is manifestly a function of the \emph{subspace} $S$:
the paper has to argue that the echelon form does not depend on the chosen basis or on the
order of its vectors, which is exactly what \texttt{rk:cancomp} spends its length on, whereas
here there is nothing to argue. That the two agree is \texttt{card\_pivots}: the pivot set
has $\dim S$ elements.

This vocabulary is needed for the \emph{lines} of the low-degree test, not for CL functions as
such: a line $\ell(u_0, v)$ is presented by the canonical representative
$u_0 = L^{\mathrm{Ln}}_v(u)$ of Definition~\ref{def:lidt-cl}, $L^{\mathrm{Ln}}_v$ being the
canonical linear map with kernel $\mathrm{span}\{v\}$.

\begin{lemma}[The two complements]
\label{lem:cl-canonical}
\uses{def:cl-canonical}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.finrank_perp, MIPRE.CL.perp_perp, MIPRE.CL.isCompl_canonCompl,
  MIPRE.CL.card_pivots, MIPRE.CL.ker_canonLin, MIPRE.CL.range_canonLin,
  MIPRE.CL.ker_lperp}
\leanok
With the notation of Definition~\ref{def:cl-canonical}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item ($\texttt{lem:perp\_perp}$) $\dim S + \dim S^\perp = \dim V$ and
  $(S^\perp)^\perp = S$, over every field;
\item ($\texttt{lem:canonical-complement}$) $S$ and its canonical complement are
  complementary subspaces, and the pivot set has $\dim S$ elements;
\item the canonical linear map with kernel $S$ has kernel exactly $S$ and range the canonical
  complement;
\item ($\texttt{lem:L\_perp\_perp}$) $\ker(L^\perp) = (\ker L)^\perp$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{def:cl-canonical}
Item 1 is Mathlib's, for a nondegenerate reflexive bilinear form, once the dot product is shown
to be one. For item 2, let $0 \neq x \in S$ lie in the canonical complement and let $j$ be the
least index with $x_j \neq 0$; then $x$ witnesses $S \cap \mathrm{span}\{\hat e_i : i \geq
j+1\} < S \cap \mathrm{span}\{\hat e_i : i \geq j\}$, so $j$ is a pivot, so $x_j = 0$ --- a
contradiction. The pivot count is an induction on $j$ along $u_j + \#\{\text{pivots} < j\} =
\dim S$, using that $u_j \leq u_{j+1} + 1$ because the $j$-th coordinate is a linear functional
on $S \cap \mathrm{span}\{\hat e_i : i \geq j\}$ with kernel the next term. Codisjointness
then follows by counting dimensions, the complement having $n - \#J$. Items 3 and 4 are then
Mathlib's projector along a direct sum decomposition.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Structure of a CL function]
\label{lem:cl-structure}
\uses{def:cl-function}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.CLFun.truncate, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.factorAt, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.mapAt,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.factorOfPrefix, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.mapOfPrefix, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.ExactlyOn,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.SupportedOn.eval_truncate_eq_sum, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.SupportedOn.disjoint_factorAt,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.SupportedOn.factorAt_subset, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.ExactlyOn.biUnion_factorAt,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.SupportedOn.factorOfPrefix_eval, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.SupportedOn.mapOfPrefix_eval}
\leanok
Let $L$ be an $\ell$-level CL function on $V$, given with its decomposition data. For $k \in
\{1, \ldots, \ell\}$ there are a $k$-level CL function $L_{\leq k} : V \to V$ on $V$, the
\emph{$k$-th marginal} (with $L_{\le 0} = 0$ and $L_{<k} = L_{\le k-1}$), and, for every
prefix $u \in L_{<k}(V)$, a register subspace $V_{k,u}$ of $V$, the \emph{$k$-th factor space},
and a linear map $L_{k,u} : V_{k,u} \to V_{k,u}$, the \emph{$k$-th linear map}, such that for
all $x \in V$, writing $u_i = L_{<i}(x)$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $L_{\leq k}(x) = \sum_{i=1}^{k} L_{i,u_i}\bigl(x^{V_{i,u_i}}\bigr)$, and
  $L = L_{\le \ell}$;
\item the factor spaces $V_{1,u_1}, \ldots, V_{\ell,u_\ell}$ are pairwise disjoint register
  subspaces of $V$, and $V = \bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell} V_{i,u_i}$ when the data are
  \emph{exact}, that is, when the last level of every branch leaves no coordinate over.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
This is \texttt{lem:cl-kth} of \texttt{linear.tex}, by induction on the level. In Lean the
marginal is \texttt{CLFun.truncate}; the factor space and the linear map of a stage are
computed both along a run (\texttt{factorAt}, \texttt{mapAt}) and from the prefix alone
(\texttt{factorOfPrefix}, \texttt{mapOfPrefix}), and the two agree
(\texttt{factorOfPrefix\_eval}, \texttt{mapOfPrefix\_eval}): that is what makes the paper's
indexing by prefixes well defined, and it is the fact the queries of a sampler rest on.
Exactness is \texttt{CLFun.ExactlyOn}. The paper states the lemma as an equivalence, with the
data normalized so that the factor spaces always exhaust $V$; the formalization keeps the
paper's own inductive definition, under which a level-$0$ remainder is allowed, and records
exactness as the separate predicate that samplers are required to satisfy.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Closure of CL functions]
\label{lem:cl-closure}
\uses{def:cl-function,lem:cl-structure}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.CLFun.concat, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.directSum, MIPRE.CL.IsCLFun.succ,
  MIPRE.CL.IsCLFun.concat, MIPRE.CL.IsCLFun.directSum, MIPRE.CL.IsCLFun.directSum'}
\leanok
Let $V = U \oplus W$ be a decomposition of $V$ into complementary register subspaces.
\begin{enumerate}
\item (Levels.) Every $\ell$-level CL function is $(\ell+1)$-level.
\item (Concatenation.) If $L$ is a $k$-level CL function on $U$ and $R_u$ an $\ell$-level CL
  function on $W$ for each $u \in L(U)$, then $x \mapsto L(x^U) + R_{L(x^U)}(x^W)$ is a
  $(k+\ell)$-level CL function on $V$.
\item (Direct sums.) If $L$ is a $k$-level CL function on $U$ and $R$ an $\ell$-level CL
  function on $W$, then $x \mapsto L(x^U) + R(x^W)$ is a $\max(k,\ell)$-level CL function on
  $V$.
\end{enumerate}
Concatenation and direct sums preserve exactness in the sense of
Lemma~\ref{lem:cl-structure}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\texttt{rk:higher-level}, \texttt{lem:cl-concat} and \texttt{lem:cl-func-prod} of
\texttt{linear.tex}, the last for two summands, which iterates. The Lean constructions are on
presentations and carry no hypotheses; that the supports are disjoint enters in the evaluation
lemmas identifying the function presented.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Direct sums of families, and their distributions]
\label{lem:cl-famsum}
\uses{lem:cl-closure,lem:cl-structure}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.CLFun.famSum, MIPRE.CL.IsCLFun.famSum, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.exactlyOn_famSum,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.eval_famSum, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.eval_truncate_famSum,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.factorOfPrefix_famSum, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.mapOfPrefix_famSum,
  MIPRE.CL.clDist_famSum}
\leanok
Let $V = \bigoplus_{i=1}^{k} V^{(i)}$ with every $V^{(i)}$ a copy of $F^s$, and let $L^{(i)}$
be an $\ell$-level CL function on $V^{(i)}$ for each $i$. Then $x \mapsto (L^{(i)}(x^{(i)}))_i$
is an $\ell$-level CL function on $V$, exact if every $L^{(i)}$ is, whose marginals, factor
spaces and stage maps read off a prefix are those of the $L^{(i)}$ on the blocks. For two such
families $(L^{(i)})$, $(R^{(i)})$, the conditionally linear distribution of the pair of direct
sums is the product of the distributions of the pairs $(L^{(i)}, R^{(i)})$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Item (3) of Lemma~\ref{lem:cl-closure} iterated over the $k$ summands, done in one induction
on the level with the $k$ presentations side by side: at every stage the register subspace is
the union of the blocks' and the map their direct sum, and the continuations are selected
blockwise. The Lean presentation lives on any $F^\kappa$ with $\kappa \cong \{1, \ldots, k\}
\times \{1, \ldots, s\}$, so that $\kappa = \{1, \ldots, ks\}$ needs no reindexing. For the
distribution: a uniform vector of $V$ is a $k$-tuple of independent uniform vectors of the
blocks, and the event that the direct sums take prescribed values is the conjunction of the
blockwise events.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Downsizing]
\label{lem:cl-downsize}
\uses{def:cl-function,lem:cl-structure}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.downsizeEquiv, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.downsize, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.eval_downsize,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.eval_truncate_downsize, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.factorOfPrefix_downsize,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.mapOfPrefix_downsize, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.ExactlyOn.downsize,
  MIPRE.CL.IsCLFun.downsize, MIPRE.CL.clDist_downsize, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.reindex,
  MIPRE.CL.IsCLFun.reindex}
\leanok
Let $\F$ be a finite field, $\mathbb{K}$ a subfield, and $e_1, \ldots, e_t$ a basis of $\F$
over $\mathbb{K}$; let $\downsize : \F^n \to \mathbb{K}^{nt}$ be the $\mathbb{K}$-linear
bijection reading off the coordinates of each entry in this basis. If $L$ is an $\ell$-level CL
function on $V = \F^n$, then $L^\downsize = \downsize \circ L \circ \downsize^{-1}$ is an
$\ell$-level CL function on $\mathbb{K}^{nt}$, whose decomposition data are the downsized data
of $L$: for $j \in \{1, \ldots, \ell\}$ and $u \in L_{<j}(V)$,
\begin{equation*}
  L^\downsize_{\le j} = \downsize \circ L_{\le j} \circ \downsize^{-1}, \qquad
  V^\downsize_{j,\, \downsize(u)} = \downsize(V_{j,u}), \qquad
  L^\downsize_{j,\, \downsize(u)} = \downsize \circ L_{j,u} \circ \downsize^{-1},
\end{equation*}
where $\downsize(V_{j,u})$ is the register subspace of $\mathbb{K}^{nt}$ on the coordinates
$(i, k)$ with $i$ a coordinate of $V_{j,u}$. Exactness is preserved, and the CL distribution of
$(L^\downsize, R^\downsize)$ is the image under $\downsize$ of that of $(L, R)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\texttt{lem:cl-downsize} and \texttt{lem:downsize-cl-dist} of \texttt{linear.tex}, by
induction on the level; the one fact used is that $\downsize$ commutes with the coordinate
projections onto register subspaces (\texttt{lem:downsize\_subspace} of
\texttt{preliminaries.tex}, in the register case).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The paper states the lemma for admissible $q = 2^t$, $t$ odd, with the
self-dual normal basis of Lemma~\ref{lem:self-dual-basis}; the formalization proves it for any
basis of any finite field over any subfield, the coordinates of $\mathbb{K}^{nt}$ being indexed
by pairs $(i, k)$ and identified with $\{1, \ldots, nt\}$ by a reindexing
(\texttt{CLFun.reindex}, the transport along any bijection of coordinates, which preserves
everything above). The self-dual normal basis matters elsewhere: for the arithmetic of
$\F_{2^t}$ to be computable from $\F_2$-data in polynomial time by the samplers and deciders
that use it, a matter for the efficient-computability toolkit rather than for this lemma. The
paper's downsized \emph{sampler} (\texttt{def:downsize\_sampler},
\texttt{lem:downsize\_sampler}), a Turing machine wrapping a sampler over $\F_{2^t}$, has no
counterpart here: samplers are over $\F_2$ by definition (Definition~\ref{def:sampler}), and a
construction native to $\F_{2^t}$, such as the introspection sampler, is to present the
downsized CL functions directly, this lemma supplying their decomposition data.

\begin{remark}[Why the pipeline is bipartite throughout]
\label{rem:bipartite-route}
\uses{def:q-value,def:sync-value,thm:almost-sync,rem:almost-sync-hypothesis,rem:sync-invariant,lem:sync-le-valstar}
Every soundness clause of this chapter is stated in $\valstar$ and none in $\synval$. The
choice follows the source transformation contracts and the direct repetition theorem
(Theorem~\ref{thm:direct-repetition-q}), all of which already concern $\valstar$.
No scalar transport from a synchronous value bound is needed. In particular, neither
the synchronization statement of Theorem~\ref{thm:almost-sync} nor the additional
hypotheses discussed in Remark~\ref{rem:almost-sync-hypothesis} enter the composition.
The source rigidity analyses can therefore be formalized in their bipartite setting,
without the synchronization invariant of Remark~\ref{rem:sync-invariant}.
Lemma~\ref{lem:sync-le-valstar} is used only to read a $\valstar$ conclusion in $\synval$ when
a synchronous statement is wanted (Theorem~\ref{thm:halting}, Theorem~\ref{thm:main}),
never in the other direction.

What this costs in turn is that the rigidity and approximate-measurement lemmas the inner
analyses consume must be stated bipartitely, as their sources state them, rather than
tracially. That is the arrangement of~\cite{JNVWY20} and of the companion repository's
paper, so it is the arrangement with the least to prove.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Question reduction: introspection}
\label{sec:ps-introspection}

Introspection removes the verifier's cost of sampling questions: instead of receiving
questions for $\verifier_{2^n}$, the players \emph{sample the questions themselves} by
measuring shared EPR pairs, and the new verifier uses the Pauli basis test
(Theorem~\ref{thm:qld}) to force honest sampling. The CL structure of the sampler is
what makes commitment to each stage of the sampled question enforceable (the ``hiding''
directions of the CL decomposition). The result is a verifier whose questions are
exponentially smaller, at the price of a fixed number of levels.

\begin{remark}[Typed verifiers and the exterior transformation boundaries]
\label{rem:typed-detyping}
\ledgernode{1.1.4}
\uses{lem:detype-compiler}
Introspection is detyped at its exterior boundary. Oracularization stays typed
inside answer reduction; the final answer-reduced verifier is then detyped.
The exterior levels are $5\to7\to7$ for introspection, answer reduction and
repetition. Detyping adds two levels and gives
$\mathrm{failure}_{\mathrm{typed}}\leq16^{|T|}
\mathrm{failure}_{\mathrm{detyped}}$ via a same-state strategy conversion.
This is a bound on failure, not a multiplicative comparison of values.
The graph must have an edge. The following interfaces retain the effective
and timeout hypotheses of the current source~\cite{AuditSync26}.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[Typed sampler and its distribution]
\label{def:typed-sampler-interface}
\ledgernode{1.1.4.1}
\uses{def:normal-verifier}
Let $G=(T,E)$ be a finite undirected type graph with $m_G\geq1$ edges and
$k_G$ loops. Sample an ordered adjacent type pair uniformly from its
$2m_G-k_G$ possibilities, and independently a common uniform
$z\in\F_{q(n)}^{s(n)}$. Output
$((t,L_t^\alice(z)),(u,L_u^\bob(z)))$.
A typed sampler exposes dimension, marginal, level-linear and factor-support
queries for each type. It computes the prescribed bounds before parsing
auxiliary inputs, inspects at most the bound plus one symbol per component,
and halts with a fixed default on malformed input. Computing the bounds is
charged in its runtime. Linear and factor-query correctness is required only
on valid prefix images; image membership is not a parsing obligation.
No field-size query is implicit in this interface.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Typed decider and normal verifier]
\label{def:typed-normal-interface}
\ledgernode{1.1.4.2}
\uses{def:typed-sampler-interface,def:normal-verifier}
A typed decider receives $(n,t,x,u,y,a,b)$; a timeout rejects regardless of
the output tape. A typed normal verifier is over $\F_2$ and first uses the
sampler's dimension query to check $t,u\in T$ and $|x|=|y|=s(n)$.
In timeout-counter form, its bound dominates this preliminary computation.
For finite decider runtime the answer alphabet consists of strings of length
at most $\TIME_D(n)$, and the decision predicate is total.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Effective typed downsizing]
\label{lem:typed-downsize}
\ledgernode{1.1.4.3}
\uses{def:typed-sampler-interface,lem:self-dual-basis}
Given an admissible-field typed sampler $S$ and a supplied total machine
$R_{\mathrm{field}}(n)=q(n)$, one can construct its coordinate downsizing
over $\F_2$, with dimension $s(n)\log_2q(n)$ and unchanged levels and types.
Runtime is polynomial in $\TIME_S(n),T_{\mathrm{field}}(n),s(n),\log q(n),|T|$;
description size and compilation cost are polynomial in
$|S|,|R_{\mathrm{field}}|,|T|$.
The wrapper calls the field routine and dimension query before bounded-prefix
parsing. A factor-support bit expands to a whole block of ones or zeros,
not to the coordinate representation of a field element.
There is no assertion that $q(n)$ can be recovered from arbitrary sampler
code. The introspection and PCP parameter routines separately supply this
premise within their budgets; the binary case uses the constant routine.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Graph rejection sampling]
\label{lem:detype-graph}
\ledgernode{1.1.4.4}
\uses{def:typed-sampler-interface}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.Graph.presentation,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.presentation_exactlyOn,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.presentation_eval,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.localValid_output_iff,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.localValid_both_iff,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.card_validSeeds,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.mem_validSeeds_iff,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.invalid_seed_locally_detected,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.opposite_output_zero_of_invalid,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.card_seeds,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.validProbability_eq,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.inv_pow_le_validProbability,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.conditional_average}
\leanok
Put $t=|T|$. Two independent $2t$-bit blocks encode a vertex indicator and
its neighbor indicator. A two-level CL graph sampler has a valid-edge event
$E_G$ with
$p=\Pr(E_G)=(2m_G-k_G)/16^t\geq16^{-t}>0$.
Each ordered adjacent pair has exactly one seed preimage; conditionally on
$E_G$ the types have the specified graph law, with loops counted once.
On its support, an invalid edge exposes a zero opposite-neighbor block to
at least one player, enabling a trivial local measurement on that branch.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The first CL factor reads the player's two indicator blocks. The second
factor is the whole complementary pair of blocks, with a linear map keeping
only the opposite neighbor bit selected by a successful first-block decode.
The one-hot indicator makes vertex encodings injective. Thus each ordered
edge has exactly one valid seed, out of $16^{|T|}$ seeds. Reindexing the
valid-seed sum by ordered edges proves the conditional law. The sampled
question preserves the own block and the selected bit; a failed local
validity test has zero opposite block. This proves the semantic sampler
and its law. The ambient program is the separate obligation of
Lemma~\ref{lem:detype-sampler}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Detyped conditionally linear functions]
\label{lem:detype-cl}
\ledgernode{1.1.4.5}
\uses{lem:detype-graph,def:typed-normal-interface}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.Detyping.presentation,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.presentation_exactlyOn,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.presentation_eval,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.card_coord,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.select_eq_some_iff,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.select_output,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.select_eq_none_iff,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.graph_output_of_select,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.presentation_eval_valid,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.presentation_eval_invalid,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.sum_valid_questions,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.conditional_average}
\leanok
For $\ell\geq1$, concatenate the two graph levels with the selected type's $\ell$ levels on
an independent binary seed block. On an unselected branch use the zero CL
function. The result has $\ell+2$ levels and dimension $4|T|+s(n)$.
Conditionally on $E_G$, the contents are exactly the typed distribution
generated by one shared seed; the two question contents need not be independent.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Embed the graph and content presentations into the two summands of the
ambient register and concatenate them. The zero fallback consumes the whole
content register at its first level and uses empty factors thereafter, so
exactness also holds on rejected branches. On an ordered edge each player's
graph output is the fixed view of its own type. Reindexing the valid graph
seeds by ordered edges proves the joint conditional law with one common
content seed. The positive-level hypothesis covers the three-level typed
sampler used by introspection.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Register embeddings and detyped query routing]
\label{lem:detype-queries}
\uses{lem:detype-cl,lem:cl-closure}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.CLFun.embed,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.ExactlyOn.embed,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.eval_embed,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.eval_truncate_embed,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.factorOfPrefix_embed,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.mapOfPrefix_embed,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.zeroOn_exactlyOn,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.eval_truncate_zeroOn,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.mapOfPrefix_zeroOn,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.factorOfPrefix_zeroOn,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.marginal_graph,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.marginal_content,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.factor_first,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.factor_second,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.factor_content,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.linear_first,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.linear_second,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.linear_content}
\leanok
Embedding a CL presentation along a coordinate injection preserves its
marginal, factor and linear-map queries. In the detyped presentation the
first two levels route to the graph sampler. Later levels route to the
selected typed presentation using the content prefix. On an unselected
branch all marginal and linear outputs are zero; the first content factor
is the whole content register and the later factors are empty.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Extend each register-linear map by zero along the coordinate injection and
restrict each continuation's prefix to the original register. Induction
on the presentation proves all four query identities. After the two graph
levels, the accumulated graph prefix is the original graph part of the
query prefix, and its content part is unchanged. These identities give the
specified dispatch rules, including the zero fallback. They are the
semantic correctness targets for the ambient sampler implementation.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Executable fixed CL samplers]
\label{lem:fixed-cl-sampler}
\uses{def:sampler,lem:detype-graph}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.FixedSampler.sampler,
  MIPRE.CL.FixedSampler.sampler_timeBound,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.sampler,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.sampler_dim,
  MIPRE.CL.Graph.sampler_timeBound,
  MIPRE.CL.TypedSampler.specialize,
  MIPRE.CL.TypedSampler.specialize_timeBoundAt}
\leanok
Every fixed finite CL presentation with a full register partition has an actual
ambient sampler program, with all marginal, linear and factor query clauses and
halting on malformed inputs. In particular the fixed graph presentation has a
two-level sampler of dimension $4|T|$. Its runtime is polynomial in the raw query
size, uniformly in the index. A single executable typed sampler can also be
specialized to any fixed type, with explicit runtime transfer.
These fixed-table bounds may depend on the whole finite presentation; they do
not assert polynomial compilation time in a variable graph's size.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Compile a finite table of all legal queries and use a total fallback elsewhere.
For type specialization, a total raw-data adapter inserts the fixed type tag
before calling the original program; the original halting theorem applies to
every adapted raw query. The program evaluations and cost bounds are checked.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite-game detyping PCC completeness]
\label{lem:detype-game-completeness}
\uses{lem:detype-game-soundness,def:pcc}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.Detyping.decodeView_view,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.decodeQuestion_output,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.complete,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.complete_state,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.complete_player_independent,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.complete_isPCC,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.complete_value,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.exists_perfectPCC}
\leanok
For positive-level partitioned typed CL presentations and a symmetric nonempty
graph, every perfect PCC witness for the doubled typed finite game gives a
perfect PCC witness for the doubled detyped finite game. The construction keeps
the local dimension and tracial state and uses one player-independent measurement
rule. This is a construction inside the ambient PCC representation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Decode the Alice-view pattern, then the disjoint Bob-view pattern, and use a
fixed deterministic answer otherwise. On a common seed, either both decoded
views identify an actual ordered edge or one side uses the deterministic
measurement and the predicate accepts. On an edge the original measurements
and predicate are recovered. Positive support transfers commutation and zero
failure from the original perfect PCC witness.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Executable detyping sampler]
\label{lem:detype-functional-sampler}
\uses{lem:fixed-cl-sampler,lem:detype-cl}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.Detyping.sampler,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.sampler_dim,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.samplerProg_closed,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.samplerProg_halts,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.samplerProg_dimension,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.samplerProg_marginal,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.samplerProg_linear,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.samplerProg_factor,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.sampler_haltsWithin,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.sampler_timeBoundAt_uniform_degree,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.sampler_timeBound}
\leanok
An actual ambient typed sampler with $\ell>0$ levels and a fixed finite type
graph gives an actual ambient sampler with $\ell+2$ levels and dimension
$4|T|+s(n)$. Its closed program answers the dimension, marginal, linear and
factor queries of the detyped presentation, and halts on every malformed
query. A polynomial runtime bound transfers from the given typed sampler,
with an explicit coefficient and a query degree independent of the index.
The constants depend on the fixed graph; uniform polynomial compilation time
and description size in a variable graph are not asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Parse the raw query totally. The first two levels use the graph sampler;
later levels either return the zero branch or forward one correctly typed
query. The graph prefix supplies the selected type. A context-preserving
one-call program reattaches the graph result. The proved query identities
identify every returned bit string with the constructed CL presentation.
The program's evaluation cost is bounded by a polynomial combining routing,
the forwarded call, the returned data size and postprocessing. Bounding that
polynomial's degree supplies the uniform query exponent.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Detyping with answer coarse-graining]
\label{lem:detype-game-answers}
\uses{lem:detype-game-soundness}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.Detyping.restrictAnswers_state,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.restrictAnswers_failAt_le,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.restrictAnswers_failure_le,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.restrictAnswers_value_ge}
\leanok
Allow different typed and detyped answer alphabets, with question-dependent
coarse-graining maps. If acceptance on every valid edge implies acceptance
after those maps, restriction and coarse-graining preserve the state and give
failure at most $16^{|T|}$ times the detyped failure. Thus a decider may reject
additional overlong answers. The program-level cutoff must still establish
the stated acceptance implication.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Adapt the strategy's questions and coarse-grain its answer measurements.
The acceptance implication bounds conditional failure on each edge. Sum those
bounds and apply the already proved detyping conditional-distribution identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Implementing the detyped sampler]
\label{lem:detype-sampler}
\ledgernode{1.1.4.6}
\uses{lem:detype-cl,lem:detype-queries}
For $\ell\geq1$, the concatenation is implemented with runtime
$\poly(|T|,\TIME_S(n))$, retaining bounded-prefix checks and valid-prefix
query semantics. On a zero-tail branch its first factor receives the whole
remaining seed block and later factors are zero, so factors still partition
the ambient space. Valid factor output gives $s(n)=O(\TIME_S(n))$.
The implementation depends only on $S,G$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Executable detyping decider with two answer cuts]
\label{lem:detype-executable-decider}
\uses{lem:detype-functional-sampler,def:normal-verifier}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.prog_closed,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.prog_halts,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.decider,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.raw_accepts_iff,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.accepts_iff,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.verifier,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.verifier_rejectsLong,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.bounded_acceptance,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.bounded_acceptance_edge,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.verifier_game_D}
\leanok
Given an actual typed sampler, an all-input terminating typed decider and an
executable index routine returning inner and outer answer cuts, construct an
ambient detyped verifier. It rejects malformed raw encodings, wrong question
lengths and answers beyond the outer cut. Within that cut, a nonedge accepts;
a genuine edge additionally enforces the source typed-answer cut and calls
the typed decider on the unchanged answers and content questions. On the
outer finite answer alphabet its predicate is exactly the finite detyping
predicate with the inner rejection included. The inner cut bounds the whole
typed answer; an introspection implementation must separately check the
original answer component before calling the original decider.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Use actual dimension and cutoff calls followed by a total finite-graph router.
Only the canonical Boolean true result accepts. Evaluation determinism gives
the exact acceptance equivalence, and the coordinate-numbering identities
give the finite predicate equality. These statements prove no runtime bound
for the supplied cutoff routine and assume no implicit inequality between
the two cuts. Such bounds and the appropriate cut comparison remain explicit
requirements when constructing a bounded compiler and transferring completeness.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Strategy transfer for the executable detyping verifier]
\label{lem:detype-program-strategies}
\uses{lem:detype-executable-decider,lem:detype-game-completeness,lem:detype-game-soundness}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.numbered_clDist,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.verifier_game_mu,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.toFinite_state,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.toFinite_value,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.restrictAmbient_state,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.restrictAmbient_failure_le,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.restrictAmbient_value_ge,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.fromFiniteSync_isPCC,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.exists_ambientPerfectPCC,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.DeciderProgram.verifier_hasPerfectPCC}
\leanok
The actual program-defined detyped verifier has exactly the finite detyping
distribution and predicate under the explicit coordinate-numbering equivalence.
Every strategy for it restricts to a typed-game strategy on the same state
with failure at most $16^{|T|}$ times its failure. Conversely, a perfect PCC
strategy for the typed game gives an actual ambient perfect PCC strategy
with the same local dimension. Both sides use the outer answer alphabet and
the typed predicate includes rejection beyond the source typed-answer cut;
there is no implicit comparison of two different answer alphabets.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Transport the common seed and questions along the register equivalence.
Finite-sum reindexing proves the distribution identity and exact strategy
value preservation. Apply finite-game restriction and completeness, then
transport the resulting strategies, including the doubling tags and PCC
commutation clauses, to the actual verifier.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Total clocking of arbitrary typed decision programs]
\label{lem:detype-total-clock}
\uses{lem:universal-tm,lem:detype-executable-decider}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.constant,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.ofPolyTimeFun,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.clockedResult_eq_iff,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.wrapProg_halts,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.wrapProg_accepts_iff,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.wrap,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.wrap_total,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.wrap_accepts_iff_of_bounded,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.decider,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.decider_total,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.decider_accepts_iff,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.ClockProgram.wrapProg_haltsWithin}
\leanok
An executable index routine producing a unary cost budget gives a total
typed decider from arbitrary source code, even if that code does not halt.
The wrapper accepts exactly when the source returns the canonical Boolean
true within the supplied budget. It preserves the acceptance predicate on
inputs whose source evaluation is known to fit. The default construction uses
the proved clocked self-interpreter. An explicit wrapper-cost bound combines
the actual index-clock cost, interpreter polynomial and routing overhead.
This supplies the totality mechanism;
instantiating the appropriate growing clock and proving the final compiler's
time, size and answer-format bounds remain separate obligations.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Run the index clock, invoke the clocked universal interpreter on the complete
original input, and inspect its success tag and Boolean result. The interpreter
halts for every source program. Evaluation determinism identifies its tagged
result with precisely an in-budget source evaluation, proving both directions
of the acceptance law without a source-termination assumption.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Implementing the detyped decider]
\label{lem:detype-decider}
\ledgernode{1.1.4.7}
\uses{def:typed-normal-interface,lem:detype-graph}
Given the full verifier $(S,D)$ and $G$, compile the dimension query from
$S$ and identify $D$'s timeout counter once at construction time.
Let $B=B_D(n)$ and choose a universal $c$ with $K=(cB)^c\geq\TIME_D(n)$.
After bounded question checks, accept a non-edge view without scanning its
answers. On an edge, inspect at most $K+1$ symbols of each answer, reject
overflow or malformed strings, then simulate the typed decider.
An outer timeout $(C(|T|+1)B)^C$ for a sufficiently large universal $C$
dominates every branch. Its binary coefficients are computed from the
original counter; no runtime supremum is computed.
All original legal answers have length at most $K$. A valid edge and a
length-$K+1$ answer force that scan before rejection, ensuring the new game's
runtime-defined alphabet contains all original legal answers.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Detyping PCC completeness]
\label{lem:detype-completeness}
\ledgernode{1.1.4.8}
\uses{lem:detype-sampler,lem:detype-decider,def:pcc}
A perfect PCC typed witness lifts on the same state to a perfect PCC detyped
witness. Both players use the same combined rule: on an Alice-view pattern
use the original Alice operator, on a Bob-view pattern use the original Bob
operator, and otherwise use the identity measurement. The view patterns are
disjoint. On valid edges this reproduces the typed strategy; on invalid
edges one local measurement is trivial. Projectivity, consistency and the
required commutation follow, as does the identical-operator property.
This does not assert symmetry of the detyped question distribution.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Finite-game detyping soundness]
\label{lem:detype-game-soundness}
\uses{lem:detype-cl,def:tensor-strategy}
\lean{MIPRE.SampledGame.game,
  MIPRE.SampledGame.sum_dist_mul,
  MIPRE.SampledGame.one_sub_value,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.typedGame,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.game,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.game_mu_eq_clDist,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.view_disjoint,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.restrict,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.restrict_state,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.restrict_failAt,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.valid_average_le,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.restrict_failure_eq,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.restrict_failure_le,
  MIPRE.CL.Detyping.restrict_value_ge}
\leanok
Fix positive-level typed CL presentations, a symmetric type graph with at
least one edge, finite answer alphabets, and a decision predicate. Construct
the typed finite game by sampling a uniform ordered edge and a common seed.
Construct the detyped finite game using the detyped presentations and the
same answer alphabets, accepting outside valid edge views. Any strategy $S$
for the latter restricts to a strategy $S'$ for the former on literally
the same state and local dimensions, with
\[
  1-\mathrm{val}(S')\leq16^{|T|}(1-\mathrm{val}(S)).
\]
The finite detyped game's distribution equals the CL distribution of the
constructed presentations. The two families of fixed player views are
disjoint.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Restrict the measurements to the fixed graph view of each typed question.
On every ordered edge the decision predicate and conditional failure
probability agree. The typed failure is therefore exactly the detyped
failure conditioned on valid graph seeds. Nonnegativity lets us bound this
subsum by the whole failure sum; there is at least one ordered edge among
$16^{|T|}$ graph seeds. This proves the stated bound and its near-perfect
value form. This finite-game result does not yet implement the machine
wrapper's answer cutoff, truncation, or runtime.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Same-state detyping soundness]
\label{lem:detype-soundness}
\ledgernode{1.1.4.9}
\uses{lem:detype-decider,lem:detype-graph,lem:detype-game-soundness}
Restrict any detyped strategy of success at least $1-\eps$ to the fixed
graph views for typed questions and coarse-grain answers to length
$\TIME_D(n)$. Answer truncation leaves the typed computation unchanged.
Conditional detyped acceptance is no larger than acceptance of this legal
typed strategy on the same state. Hence its failure is at most
$\eps/p\leq16^{|T|}\eps$. Taking suprema uses near-optimal strategies and
does not assume attainment of the value.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Optional entanglement consequence of detyping]
\label{lem:detype-entanglement}
\ledgernode{1.1.4.10}
\uses{lem:detype-soundness,def:ent}
For $0\leq\eps\leq16^{-|T|}$,
$\Ent(\mathrm{detype}(V)_n,1-\eps)\geq
\Ent(V_n,1-16^{|T|}\eps)$, since the conversion keeps the state and its
Schmidt rank. Outside that range use the ordinary value bound; no negative
threshold is supplied to $\Ent$. This clause is separate from the value route.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Effective detyping compiler]
\label{lem:detype-compiler}
\ledgernode{1.1.4.11}
\uses{lem:detype-sampler,lem:detype-decider,lem:detype-completeness,lem:detype-soundness}
The descriptions of $(S,D)$ and the nonempty graph $G$ suffice to construct
the detyped verifier in polynomial description time. It has $\ell+2$ levels,
polynomial sampler and timeout costs, PCC completeness, and same-state
soundness loss $16^{|T|}$. The sampler depends only on $S,G$.
Effective field access for any preceding nonbinary downsizing is the separate
hypothesis of Lemma~\ref{lem:typed-downsize}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{remark}[Turing machine conventions and the ambient cost model]
\label{rem:tm-conventions}
\ledgernode{1.1.5, 1.1.5.1, 1.1.5.2, 1.1.5.3}
\uses{def:decider,def:lambda-bounded,lem:universal-tm}
The paper fixes a concrete Turing machine formalism and a \emph{timeout-counter} normal form
$B(n) = c_0 (c_1 n)^{c_2}$: a machine is wrapped so that it always halts within its bound,
and the wrapped description is canonical enough that the parameters can be read back off
it. The ambient Lean model instead uses input-size-dependent resource budgets
(Section~\ref{sec:rr-computability}). This changes the transformation hypotheses;
it does not establish the source's timeout or answer-truncation properties.
Remark~\ref{rem:runtime-normalization} records the required adaptation, and
Section~\ref{sec:departures} records the departure.

Three things in those nodes are worth keeping even so, because they are the kind of detail a
formalization rediscovers the hard way. The canonical form has to be genuinely injective in
the parameters for the timeout bound to be well defined at all, and the audit had to supply
an explicit parser to establish it --- in the source the parser's existence was asserted
rather than constructed. The overhead polynomial relating a machine's running time to its
bound needs the normalization $c_1 \geq 2$, without which the claim fails outright at
$c_1 = 1$, $n = 1$. And the whole scheme rests on the bound dominating $n$, which holds only
because the constants are positive integers. None of the three is deep; all three are the
sort of thing that stops a formalization dead.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Runtime normalization obligation]
\label{rem:runtime-normalization}
\uses{def:lambda-bounded,def:decider,rem:tm-conventions,rem:runtime-contract-bridge}
The model distinction is recorded in Remark~\ref{rem:runtime-contract-bridge}; here
are its consequences for the transformations.
The source introspection theorem uses an absolute bound $N^\lambda$ at index $N$;
its simulator rejects after that many steps. Definition~\ref{def:lambda-bounded}
instead bounds ambient cost on data $d$ by $N^\lambda(\abs{d}+1)^\lambda$.
Even on questions and answers of length at most $N^\lambda$, this supplies a bound
of order $N^{O(\lambda^2)}$, before simulation overhead, rather than $N^\lambda$.
It also gives no answer-prefix invariance for arbitrary longer strings.

To derive the ambient transformation hypotheses from the source, a normalization
construction must restrict the valid input formats, preserve the original answer cut,
and prove preservation of values and perfect PCC witnesses. It must provide the
absolute simulation budgets and the truncation properties required by the source,
including bounded checks on malformed inputs. If this requires a reparameterization
$\lambda' = p(\lambda)$, its effect on the introspection bound
$\poly(2^{p(\lambda)n},\ell)$ and on the soundness margins must be propagated.
Enlarging the answer alphabet to $N^{\lambda'}$ without rejecting answers beyond the
original cut can change the game. A direct proof in the ambient model is another route.

Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-ambient-runtime} proves the absolute legal-input bound and
its universal-simulator overhead. The ambient introspection and answer-reduction
contracts now allow the polynomial-exponent bounds $2^{(\lambda n+1)^C}$ and
$2^{(\lambda n+1)^\mu}$. Answer reduction also retains the input sampler's
degree in its output budget, as in Lemma~\ref{lem:ar-ambient-sampler-cost}.
The full compression composition is checked with these revised contracts and
still has polynomial output bounds. This is an explicit ambient adaptation;
the paper's source-model
theorem statements below retain their original time bounds.
The absolute PCP clock, normalization construction and format-preservation
clauses remain open.
\texttt{Budget} and \texttt{Verifier.Within} record coefficients, degrees, question
dimensions and explicit rejection bounds; \texttt{GapCompression.output\_rejects\_long}
constrains the output. None of these supplies normalization for arbitrary
$\lambda$-bounded inputs. The introspection, oracularization and answer-reduction
source displays below use the paper's absolute-time convention and its bipartite PCC
notion. Separate ambient contract definitions carry the Lean tags and statement marks;
their inhabitants require the supply targets stated below. In particular, identifying
source PCC witnesses with ambient completeness witnesses requires the two directions
of Remark~\ref{rem:pcc-representation-bridge}. The comparison is with \texttt{introspection.tex},
\texttt{oracularization.tex} and \texttt{ld\_compiler.tex} in the companion paper
pinned by~\cite{AuditSync26}.
\end{remark}


\begin{lemma}[Absolute costs on the original legal answer cut]
\label{lem:intro-ambient-runtime}
\uses{def:lambda-bounded,rem:runtime-normalization}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.legal_query_size_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.legal_query_cost_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.original_decider_haltsWithin,
  MIPRE.Introspection.polynomial_ansBound,
  MIPRE.Introspection.original_simulation_haltsWithin}
\leanok
For an ambient $\lambda$-bounded verifier, $n\geq1$, and questions and answers
of length at most $(2^n)^\lambda$, the actual original decider halts within
$2^{(\lambda n+1)^5}$ cost. Any fixed universal simulator also halts on those
inputs within $2^{(\lambda n+1)^C}$ for a constant $C$ depending only on its
overhead polynomial. The original answer cut is unchanged. Any fixed polynomial
of a polynomial-exponent budget fits another such budget.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The four encoded fields have size at most $16(2^n)^\lambda+7$.
Insert that size into the actual boundedness clause and bound the resulting
exponent by $(\lambda n+1)^5$. Bound program and input sizes by the same
exponential, and absorb the universal machine's polynomial overhead. No
answer-prefix invariance or malformed-input wrapper is asserted here.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Ambient sampler-cost absorption]
\label{lem:ar-ambient-sampler-cost}
\uses{def:answer-reduction-contract}
\lean{MIPRE.AnswerReduction.sampler_cost_le_power}
\leanok
If $L\leq B$ and $s+1\leq B$, then $L(s+1)^\mu\leq B^{\mu+1}$.
Consequently the ambient answer-reduction contract retains the input sampler's
degree in its output budget: a universal base polynomial is raised to
$\mu+1$, and the base output degree is multiplied by $\mu+1$. This arithmetic
bound does not construct the answer-reduction program or its clock.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Bound each factor by its corresponding power of $B$. Compression fixes
$\mu$ before choosing its final bound, so that bound is still polynomial.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The introspective verifier}

\begin{definition}[The introspective verifier]
\label{def:intro-verifier}
\ledgernode{1.2.3}
\uses{def:normal-verifier,def:cl-function,rem:typed-detyping,lem:typed-downsize,thm:qld}
Fix $\ell$ and an $\ell$-level verifier $\verifier$ satisfying the paper's absolute
$\lambda$-bound, as distinguished in Remark~\ref{rem:runtime-normalization}. The
\emph{introspective verifier} $\vint$ is the typed verifier whose players sample the
conditionally linear question distribution of $\verifier_{2^n}$ \emph{themselves}: the
question $y \in V = \F_q^s$ is not sent to them, but obtained by each player measuring its
half of $Q$ shared EPR pairs in a Pauli basis, and reported back. Its decider runs three
kinds of test.
\begin{enumerate}
\item a \textbf{Pauli test}, which is the game of Theorem~\ref{thm:qld} on the same EPR
  pairs, so that the measurements the players use to sample are forced to be genuine Pauli
  measurements in the low-degree-encoded basis;
\item a \textbf{sampling test}, which forces the reported $y$ to be distributed as the
  conditionally linear distribution of $\verifier_{2^n}$ rather than as anything else the
  players might prefer;
\item a \textbf{hiding test}, which forces each player not to learn the parts of the
  question that the conditional sampling should have hidden from it --- the types
  $\mathtt{Hide}_1, \ldots, \mathtt{Hide}_\ell$ exist for this, one per level.
\end{enumerate}
It is a $3$-level \emph{typed} verifier, hence $5$-level once detyped
(Remark~\ref{rem:typed-detyping}), and that $5$ is what the compression pipeline consumes.
\end{definition}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Introspection type graph and typed CL family]
\label{lem:intro-type-presentation}
\uses{def:intro-verifier,lem:detype-game-soundness}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.QuestionType.card,
  MIPRE.Introspection.QuestionType.esize_aux_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypeGraph.symmetric,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypeGraph.adj_pauli,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypeGraph.adj_different_roles_iff,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypeGraph.adj_pauli_aux_iff,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypeGraph.adj_hide_hide_iff,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypeGraph.edges_nonempty,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypeGraph.detyping_factor_of_pauli_card,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPresentation.family_exactlyOn,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPresentation.marginal_aux,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPresentation.linear_aux,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPresentation.factor_aux,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPresentation.joint_aux_zero,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPresentation.mu_cross_introspect,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPresentation.failAt_cross_introspect_le}
\leanok
Given the finite Pauli type graph and its distinguished $X,Z$ vertices, construct
the introspection graph with both hiding chains, the sampling and reading edges,
the cross-introspection edge and all loops. It has $|T_{\mathrm{Pauli}}|+2\ell+6$
vertices, hence $2\ell+32$ when the Pauli graph has $26$ vertices. Its type encoding
uses binary hiding indices. Retain the supplied three-level Pauli CL family;
every auxiliary type has the zero presentation with a full factor partition.
The cross-introspection edge asks the two zero-content questions with probability
exactly the inverse number of ordered edges. Its conditional failure is at most
the edge count times the whole typed game's failure.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The graph is the symmetric closure of the explicit oriented chains and Pauli
edges. Constructor case analysis proves the edge classifications. Summing the
uniform common-seed law over the zero auxiliary presentations gives the exact
edge probability; nonnegativity bounds its contribution to the total failure.
This supplies the graph and CL family. The uniform Pauli sampler and the
introspective decision program are supplied by
Lemmas~\ref{lem:intro-canonical-sampler} and~\ref{lem:intro-quotient-kernel}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The parsed introspection decision predicate]
\label{lem:intro-parsed-predicate}
\uses{lem:intro-type-presentation,def:cl-function}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.CLChecks.prefixRegister_subset,
  MIPRE.Introspection.CLChecks.proj_prefixRegister,
  MIPRE.Introspection.CLChecks.prefixRegister_full,
  MIPRE.Introspection.CLChecks.sampling_prefix,
  MIPRE.Introspection.CLChecks.hidingPauli_coarse,
  MIPRE.Introspection.CLChecks.hidingNext_coarse,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check_formats,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check_consistency,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check_sampling,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check_sampling_prefix,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check_reading,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check_game,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check_game_reversed}
\leanok
Construct the finite predicate on parsed answers, with format and consistency
checks, both orientations of the sampling and hiding tests, and the original
decision predicate on the cross-introspection edge. The original CL family,
original predicate, Pauli predicate and projection of Pauli answers to the
original register are explicit parameters. The Pauli predicate is instantiated
at the actual Pauli question contents. Accepted sampling answers satisfy every
prefix comparison on the whole answer alphabet. Hiding projections use the
registers selected by the later answer, as required by the adaptive construction.
For every claimed output, the prefix is exactly its projection onto the
selected register; at full length, this register is the complete partition.
The cross-introspection check retains the original Alice/Bob argument order
in either orientation. This is the parsed semantic predicate; byte parsing,
global cutoffs and clocked program calls remain separate construction work.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Use a sum of the four answer formats. Dispatch by question constructors and
check both orientations. Compute claimed-output prefixes by coordinate
restriction, and dual readouts in the selected local register. Unfolding the
checks gives the accepted-answer implications; the CL prefix theorem gives
the marginal identity without assuming that a claimed answer is in the image.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The executable five-level introspection sampler]
\label{lem:intro-executable-sampler}
\uses{lem:intro-type-presentation,lem:detype-functional-sampler}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.typedSampler,
  MIPRE.Introspection.typedSampler_dim,
  MIPRE.Introspection.typedSampler_cl,
  MIPRE.Introspection.typedSampler_prog_independent,
  MIPRE.Introspection.detypedSampler,
  MIPRE.Introspection.detypedSampler_dim,
  MIPRE.Introspection.detypedSampler_cl,
  MIPRE.Introspection.typedSampler_haltsWithin,
  MIPRE.Introspection.typedSampler_timeBoundAt,
  MIPRE.Introspection.detypedSampler_timeBound}
\leanok
An actual three-level typed Pauli sampler extends to the complete typed
introspection family and then to an actual five-level sampler. Its dimension
is $4(|T_{\mathrm{Pauli}}|+2\ell+6)+s(n)$. Every marginal, linear and factor
query agrees with the specified CL family, and malformed queries halt.
The typed extension program is independent of $\ell$. Polynomial runtime
transfers with an explicit index-dependent coefficient and an index-independent
query degree. Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-canonical-sampler} supplies the uniform
Pauli sampler and its parameter and size bounds for the actual compiler.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Strip the sum tag and forward Pauli queries. Auxiliary queries produce the
zero vectors or first-factor indicators of the supplied vector length.
Dimension queries call the Pauli sampler. Apply the executable graph detyping
compiler and compose its checked cost bound with the extension's cost bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Complexity of the introspective verifier]
\label{lem:intro-complexity}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.1}
\uses{def:intro-verifier,def:lambda-bounded}
There are Turing machines computing the two halves of $\vint$ separately: one which on input
$(\lambda, \ell)$ outputs the sampler $\sint$ in time $\polylog(\lambda, \ell)$, with
$\TIME_{\sint}(n) = \poly(n, \lambda, \ell)$ and $\sint$ a $3$-level typed sampler; and one
which on input $(\verifier, \lambda, \ell)$ outputs the decider $\dint$ in standard
timeout-counter form, in polynomial time, with $\abs{\dint} = \poly(\lambda, \ell)$ and
timeout bound $B_{\dint}(n) = \poly(2^{\lambda n}, \ell)$. The sampler's description does not
depend on $\verifier$ at all.
\end{lemma}

\begin{remark}[A false step in computing the hidden subspace]
\label{rem:intro-lperp}
\uses{lem:intro-complexity,def:cl-function}
The decider has to compute, for a level $k$ and a prefix $u$, the orthogonal complement
$(L^v_{k+1, u})^\perp$ of the conditionally linear map at that level. The printed procedure
computed it by taking a basis of $\ker(M)$ and forming its canonical complement --- and the
canonical complement of a basis of $\ker(M)$ is \emph{not} a basis of $\ker(M)^\perp$. Over
$\F_2^2$ the map $L(x_1, x_2) = (x_1 + x_2, 0)$ is a counterexample, found independently by
two checkers. Only the computation was wrong: the test itself is defined abstractly through
the complement, so the statement of Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-complexity} and its bound are
unaffected. It is recorded because a formalization must supply a procedure that is actually
correct, and this is the obvious place to get it wrong. The same audit closed a second seam
here, an answer-length bound of $8Q$ that only holds once the honest answer is truncated the
way oracularization and answer reduction already truncate theirs.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Completeness: two identities about Pauli measurements}

The honest strategy measures EPR pairs in the bases the sampled question prescribes, so the
completeness proof is not an approximation argument at all: it is two exact statements about
how Pauli operators interact with measurements of a linear map, and then a check that the
seven tests of the decider all pass with certainty. Both identities are finite-field linear
algebra, and both are plausible early Lean targets.

\effortMedium

\begin{lemma}[Pauli operators against a linear-map measurement]
\label{lem:pauli-linear-fourier}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.2}
\uses{def:cl-function}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.subspaceTrace_eq_zero_iff,
  MIPRE.Introspection.sum_subspace_sign,
  MIPRE.Introspection.linear_measurement_fourier,
  MIPRE.Introspection.linear_measurement_fourier_inverse}
\leanok
Let $\F$ be a finite field of characteristic two, with $\omega=-1$.
Let $L : \F^k \to \F^k$ be linear, let $W \in \{X, Z\}$, and for $a$ in the range of $L$
pick $u_a$ with $L(u_a) = a$. Write $\sigma^W_{[L(\cdot) = a]}$ for the projector onto the
event that a $W$-basis measurement returns a preimage of $a$. Then for every
$v \in \ker(L)^\perp$,
\begin{equation*}
  \sigma^W(v) = \sum_{a \in \F^k} \omega^{\fieldtr(u_a \cdot v)}\, \sigma^W_{[L(\cdot) = a]},
\end{equation*}
and conversely, for every $a$ in the range of $L$,
\begin{equation*}
  \sigma^W_{[L(\cdot) = a]} = \E_{v \sim \ker(L)^\perp}\ \omega^{-\fieldtr(v \cdot u_a)}\,
    \sigma^W(v).
\end{equation*}
The choice of $u_a$ off the range of $L$ is immaterial, since there the projector vanishes.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Each identity is the Fourier expansion of the other over the group $\ker(L)^\perp$: the
character $v \mapsto \omega^{\fieldtr(u_a \cdot v)}$ depends only on $a = L(u_a)$, because two
preimages of $a$ differ by an element of $\ker(L)$, which the trace pairing annihilates
against $\ker(L)^\perp$.
\end{proof}

\effortEasy

\begin{lemma}[A commutation criterion for $X$ and $Z$ measurements]
\label{lem:pauli-commute-criterion}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.2}
\uses{lem:pauli-linear-fourier}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.linear_measurements_commute}
\leanok
Over a finite field of characteristic two, let $L, R : \F^k \to \F^k$ be linear with
$\ker(R)^\perp \subseteq \ker(L)$. Then the
measurements $\{\sigma^Z_{[L(\cdot) = b]}\}_b$ and $\{\sigma^X_{[R(\cdot) = d]}\}_d$ commute.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Expand both families by Lemma~\ref{lem:pauli-linear-fourier}. The $Z$-side expansion runs
over $v \in \ker(L)^\perp$ and the $X$-side over $w \in \ker(R)^\perp$, and
$\sigma^Z(v)$ and $\sigma^X(w)$ commute exactly when $\fieldtr(v \cdot w) = 0$. The
hypothesis gives $w \in \ker(L)$ for every such $w$, and $v \in \ker(L)^\perp$, so the pairing
vanishes term by term.
\end{proof}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Projectivity of sampling and hiding readouts]
\label{lem:intro-linear-readout-projectivity}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.2}
\uses{lem:pauli-linear-fourier,lem:pauli-commute-criterion}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.weyl_projectors_isPVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.linear_measurement_isPVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.joint_measurement_isPVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.sampling_hiding_isPVM}
\leanok
Over a finite characteristic-two field, reading a linear map of either Weyl
eigenbasis outcome is a projective measurement. If
$\ker(R)^\perp\subseteq\ker(L)$, the joint readout with projectors
$\sigma^Z_{[L(\cdot)=a]}\sigma^X_{[R(\cdot)=b]}$ is also projective.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The Weyl spectral projectors are self-adjoint idempotents summing to identity,
and coarse-graining preserves these properties. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:pauli-commute-criterion}, the two coarse-grained families commute.
Their products are therefore self-adjoint idempotents; summing over both
readouts gives the identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Completeness of introspection]
\label{lem:intro-completeness}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.2}
\uses{def:intro-verifier,def:pcc,lem:pauli-linear-fourier,lem:pauli-commute-criterion,lem:intro-honest-core,lem:intro-honest-adaptive,lem:intro-honest-hiding,lem:intro-honest-parsed}
If $\verifier_{2^n}$ has a value-$1$ PCC strategy, then $\vint_n$ has one: the players
measure their EPR halves in the bases the sampled question prescribes and then play the
value-$1$ strategy of $\verifier_{2^n}$ on the question they have obtained. Every test of the
decider accepts with certainty, and the resulting strategy is again projective, consistent
and commuting.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:pauli-linear-fourier,lem:pauli-commute-criterion}
Lemma~\ref{lem:pauli-linear-fourier} identifies the honest sampling measurement with a Pauli
measurement, which is what the Pauli test checks; Lemma~\ref{lem:pauli-commute-criterion}
gives the commutation the hiding test needs, the hypothesis
$\ker(R)^\perp \subseteq \ker(L)$ being exactly the nesting of consecutive conditionally
linear levels. Consistency and projectivity pass through the construction because each
player's measurement is a product of commuting projective measurements.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The soundness toolkit}

Soundness needs three groups of lemmas before the induction can start, and they are
separated here because they are independent of introspection and reusable: a Pauli twirl, a
conversion from sub-measurements to measurements, and a way to condition on part of a
question.

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Exact Pauli twirling]
\label{lem:pauli-twirl}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.3.1}
\uses{lem:pauli-linear-fourier}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.twirlX_wZ, MIPRE.Introspection.twirlZ_wX}
\leanok
Over a finite field of characteristic two, for a subspace $K\leq\F^k$, twirl by the uniform distribution on
$\tau^W(K)$, where $W\in\{X,Z\}$ and $W'$ is the other basis.
The twirl fixes $\tau^{W'}(u)$ if $u\in K^\perp$ and is zero otherwise.
Here the annihilator uses the field-valued dot product. The source proves the
general-characteristic statement; this formalization supplies its characteristic-two
specialization used by the pipeline.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Conjugation by the opposite Pauli family multiplies an operator by the sign of
the trace pairing. Average these signs on $K$. The character is trivial exactly
on $K^\perp$, by Lemma~\ref{lem:pauli-linear-fourier}; all other character sums vanish.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Linear-map blocks of the twirl]
\label{lem:pauli-linear-mixing}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.3.2}
\uses{lem:pauli-twirl}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.linear_twirl_blocks,
  MIPRE.Introspection.averagedBlock_posSemidef,
  MIPRE.Introspection.sum_averagedBlock_eq_one,
  MIPRE.Introspection.blockPOVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.linear_twirl_povm}
\leanok
Over a finite field of characteristic two, for a linear $L:V\to V$, twirling an operator on $\mathbb C^V\otimes H$
first by all $Z$ Paulis and then by $X(\ker L)$ gives
$\sum_{y\in L(V)}P_y^Z\otimes B^y$, where $P_y^Z$ projects onto the
fibre $L(v)=y$. If the original operators form a POVM, the blocks form a
POVM for each nonempty fibre. Empty fibres impose no condition on $B^y$.
The source proves the general-characteristic statement; the Lean theorem supplies
the characteristic-two specialization. It sums over all $y\in V$, with zero
projectors outside the image, and chooses valid POVMs even at empty fibres.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The full $Z$ twirl removes off-diagonal blocks in the sampled register.
The $X(\ker L)$ twirl averages the remaining diagonal blocks on cosets of
$\ker L$. Define $B^y$ by this average at a chosen preimage of $y$ (or at
zero when there is none). Translation permutes each coset, so the block
depends only on $y$. Principal compressions and their averages preserve
positivity, while linearity sends a normalized family to a normalized
family. This constructs the ancillary POVMs directly.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Commutator Parseval identity]
\label{lem:pauli-commutator-transfer}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.3.3}
\uses{lem:pauli-linear-fourier,def:state-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.sum_norm_linearFourier_sq,
  MIPRE.Introspection.linear_measurement_parseval,
  MIPRE.Introspection.linear_measurement_commutator_parseval,
  MIPRE.Introspection.linear_measurement_commutator_parseval_avg}
\leanok
For the fibre projections $P_y^W$ of $L$ and every operator $M$,
\[
 \sum_y\|([M,P_y^W]\otimes\Id)\psi\|^2
 =\mathbb E_{v\in(\ker L)^\perp}
       \|([M,\tau^W(v)]\otimes\Id)\psi\|^2.
\]
The identity remains true after averaging questions and summing measurement
outcomes. It introduces no dimension or alphabet factor.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Distinct nonempty fibres give orthogonal characters on $(\ker L)^\perp$.
Apply Parseval to the complex-linear action that takes a register operator
to its amplified commutator applied to the state. Empty fibre projectors
vanish. Summing outcomes and averaging questions preserves the identity.
The checked field specialization is characteristic two.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Approximate commutation controls unitary mixing]
\label{lem:pauli-unitary-mixing}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.3.4}
\uses{def:state-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.stateSqNorm_mul_of_isometry,
  MIPRE.Introspection.unitaryTwirl_dist_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.unitaryTwirl_outcome_dist_le}
\leanok
Let $M_a^x$ be POVMs, $\psi$ a unit bipartite vector and $\mu_x$ a
conditional unitary distribution. For
$\mathrm T_{\mu_x}(M)=\mathbb E_U UMU^\dagger$, put
$\delta=\mathbb E_x\mathbb E_U\sum_a
\|([M_a^x,U^\dagger]\otimes\Id)\psi\|^2$.
Then $\mathbb E_x\sum_a
\|((\mathrm T_{\mu_x}(M_a^x)-M_a^x)\otimes\Id)\psi\|^2\leq\delta$.
Jensen and left-unitary invariance of the norm suffice; invariance of the
state under $U$ is not a hypothesis.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Write each conjugation error as $U[M,U^\dagger]$. Left multiplication by
$U$ preserves the squared state norm, and convexity bounds the squared
norm of the average by the average of the squared norms. Sum over outcomes
and average questions. The Lean estimate has constant one and holds for
arbitrary operator families and arbitrary state vectors.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Combining two approximate commutations]
\label{lem:pauli-two-sided-commutation}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.3.5}
\uses{def:state-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.commutator_product_bound,
  MIPRE.Introspection.two_sided_commutation,
  MIPRE.Introspection.two_sided_commutation_avg,
  MIPRE.Introspection.unitaryTwirl_comp,
  MIPRE.Introspection.composed_twirl_dist_le}
\leanok
Conditionally on $x$, draw $U_1,U_2$ independently. Suppose both
commutators $[M_a^x,U_i^\dagger]$ have average outcome-summed squared state
norm $O(\eps)$ and
$\mathbb E\|((U_2^\dagger\otimes\Id)-(\Id\otimes U_2))\psi\|^2=O(\eps)$.
Then $[M_a^x,U_1^\dagger U_2^\dagger]$ has the same type of bound.
Transfer $U_2$ across the bipartition and back; $\sum_a(M_a^x)^2\leq\Id$
controls the summed transfer errors without an outcome-count factor.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Transfer the second unitary to its commuting mirror on Bob's side, move
the first unitary past the measurement, and transfer back. The four-link
squared triangle inequality gives coefficients $4,4,8$ for the two
commutator errors and the mirror error. The POVM contraction identity
controls the outcome sum. Independent conditional averages preserve these
coefficients. With an exact mirror, the composed twirl costs at most four
times the sum of the two averaged commutator errors.
\end{proof}

\effortMedium

\begin{lemma}[Completing a nearby submeasurement]
\label{lem:close-submeasurement}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.3.6}
\uses{def:state-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.submeasurement_completion_mass,
  MIPRE.Introspection.submeasurement_completion_dist,
  MIPRE.Introspection.submeasurement_completion_dist_avg}
\leanok
Let $A_a^x$ be a PVM, $B_a^x\geq0$ with $\sum_aB_a^x\leq\Id$, and
$C_a^x$ a POVM satisfying $C_a^x\geq B_a^x$ pointwise. For a unit
bipartite state and an arbitrary question distribution, define
\[
 D_\psi(A,B)=\mathbb E_x\sum_a
       \|((A_a^x-B_a^x)\otimes\Id)\psi\|^2=\delta.
\]
Then $D_\psi(A,C)\leq2\delta+4\sqrt\delta$.
In particular, if $D_\psi(A,B)\leq\eps\leq1$, the conclusion is
$D_\psi(A,C)\leq6\sqrt\eps$. A coefficient-one square-root bound is not
asserted for this literal squared-distance convention.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Using real parts and Cauchy--Schwarz in the outcome-summed state norm,
$\mathbb E_x\langle\Id-\sum_aB_a^x\rangle\leq2\sqrt\delta$;
here $\sum_a(B_a^x)^2\leq\sum_aB_a^x$ and $\sum_a(A_a^x)^2=\Id$.
Since $0\leq C_a^x-B_a^x\leq\Id$,
$(C_a^x-B_a^x)^2\leq C_a^x-B_a^x$, so $D_\psi(B,C)\leq2\sqrt\delta$.
The squared triangle bound gives $D_\psi(A,C)\leq2D_\psi(A,B)+2D_\psi(B,C)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Retained-fibre mixing in coordinate presentations]
\label{lem:pauli-mixing-coordinates}
\uses{lem:pauli-linear-mixing,lem:pauli-commutator-transfer,lem:pauli-two-sided-commutation,lem:close-submeasurement}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.snorm_right_projector_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.retained_fibre_dist,
  MIPRE.Introspection.controlledPOVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.retained_block_le_completion,
  MIPRE.Introspection.block_retention_precompletion,
  MIPRE.Introspection.block_retention_dist_avg,
  MIPRE.Introspection.mirror_expVec,
  MIPRE.Introspection.eprWithAux_norm,
  MIPRE.Introspection.eprWithAux_wX_mirror,
  MIPRE.Introspection.eprWithAux_readout_mirror,
  MIPRE.Introspection.pauli_twirl_dist_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.fine_commutator_parseval,
  MIPRE.Introspection.kernel_commutator_parseval,
  MIPRE.Introspection.pauli_twirl_readout_dist_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_pauli_mixing,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_pauli_mixing_avg,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_pauli_mixing_varying,
  MIPRE.Introspection.mixing_error_sqrt_bound,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_pauli_mixing_sqrt}
\leanok
Over a finite characteristic-two field, write the retained register as
$\F^{n_x}$ and the state in each question's coordinates as
$\mathrm{EPR}_{\F^{n_x}}\otimes\xi_x$, with $\xi_x$ normalized.
Let $e_0,e_Z,e_X$ be the marginal, fine-$Z$ commutator and coarse-$X$
commutator errors of a joint PVM. There are actual normalized POVMs on
the complementary Alice space at every retained fibre, whose averaged
squared distance from the joint PVM is at most
$2\delta+4\sqrt\delta$, where
$\delta=\mathbb E_x(2e_0+8e_Z+8e_X)$.
If the three averaged errors are at most $0\leq\eps\leq1$, the bound is
$56\sqrt\eps$. Register dimensions and auxiliary spaces may vary with $x$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Twirl into ancillary POVM blocks, keep the matching fibre using the exact
EPR mirror, and complete its submeasurement by summing over the discarded
output. Average the pointwise estimates using concavity of the square root.
This includes empty fibres. The ambient coordinate transport is proved in
Lemma~\ref{lem:pauli-mixing}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pauli mixing with a retained classical fibre]
\label{lem:pauli-mixing}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.3,1.2.3.3.7}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.registerPOVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.registerOp_isPVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.stateSqNorm_registerOp,
  MIPRE.Introspection.registerEPR_norm,
  MIPRE.Introspection.registerState_split,
  MIPRE.Introspection.stateSqNorm_registerExtend,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_register_mixing_sqrt,
  MIPRE.Introspection.coordinateSplit,
  MIPRE.Introspection.coordinateLinear_restrict,
  MIPRE.Introspection.coordinateInsert_linear,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_ambient_pauli_mixing}
\leanok
\uses{lem:pauli-linear-mixing,lem:pauli-commutator-transfer,lem:pauli-unitary-mixing,lem:pauli-two-sided-commutation,lem:close-submeasurement,lem:pauli-mixing-coordinates}
Let $U_x\subseteq V_x\subseteq V$ be coordinate registers, $L_x:U_x\to U_x$
linear, and the state be $\mathrm{EPR}_V\otimes\mathrm{aux}$.
Let $M^x_{y,a}$ be a PVM on Alice's $V_x$ register and auxiliary space.
Assume its $y$ marginal is $O(\eps)$-close to $P_y^Z(L_x)$ in the
averaged squared state norm, and that its commutators with the fine $Z$
projectors on $U_x$ and coarse $X$ fibre projectors for a map with kernel
$(\ker L_x)^\perp$ have total outcome-summed squared norm $O(\eps)$.
For $0\leq\eps\leq1$ there are POVMs $N_a^{x,y}$ on the complementary
register outside $U_x$ in $V_x$ and Alice auxiliary space with
\[
 \mathbb E_x\sum_{y,a}
 \|((M^x_{y,a}-P_y^Z\otimes N_a^{x,y})\otimes\Id)\psi\|^2
 \leq C\sqrt\eps
\]
for a universal $C$ (once the hypothesis constants are fixed).
The sums in the commutator hypotheses include both projector and measurement
outcomes. Empty fibres allow any $N$; their left-hand mass is controlled by
consistency, not identically zero.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Split the actual finite coordinate registers by explicit basis equivalences.
The ambient EPR state factors exactly, and adjoining or removing the untouched
complement preserves every squared state norm. Pull back the local operators,
apply Lemma~\ref{lem:pauli-mixing-coordinates}, and transport its actual POVMs
back to the residual register. With each hypothesis bounded by $\eps$, the
conclusion has the explicit constant $56$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.3.7.1,1.2.3.3.7.2,1.2.3.3.7.3}
Twirl to blocks $B^z_{y,a}$, retain the matching blocks $B^y_{y,a}$ using
projectivity and EPR consistency, then complete with
$N_a^{x,y}=\sum_z B^y_{z,a}$. Lemma~\ref{lem:close-submeasurement}
accounts for the square-root loss. Transfers across EPR use matrix transpose,
in particular $X(v)^T=X(-v)$; no odd-characteristic identity $X(v)^T=X(v)$
is presumed.
\end{proof}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Conditioning on part of a question]
\label{lem:conditional-measurement}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.4}
\uses{def:distance,def:inconsistency}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.stateSqNorm_expVec_kron,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_sqNorm,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_commutator,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_sqNorm_sum,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_commutator_sum,
  MIPRE.Introspection.submeasurement_agreement_dist,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_consistency}
\leanok
On a state that factors as a question part times an answer part, a projective measurement of
the question part induces a distribution on it, and consistency or commutation statements
about the unconditioned measurements transfer to the measurements conditioned on the sampled
question. In particular, if
$\sum_{x, y, z} \bra{\psi} A^y_{x, z} \ot B_{x, y, z} \ket{\psi} \geq 1 - \delta$ with
$\{B_{x,y,z}\}$ projective, then
