$\Id \ot B_{x, y, z} \approx_\delta A^y_{x, z} \ot B_{x, y}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
On a product state, the squared norm of a tensor-product operator factors.
The question factor is exactly the probability weight. This gives exact
conditioning and commutator identities, also for outcome-dependent tensor
decompositions. For conditional consistency, the operators
$A^y_{x,z}\ot B_{x,y}$ form a positive submeasurement. Expand its distance
from $\Id\ot B_{x,y,z}$, use projectivity to identify the cross term with
the agreement probability, and bound each diagonal sum by one. The literal
summed squared-distance bound is $2\delta$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Soundness}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Extracting a Pauli strategy]
\label{lem:intro-pauli-extract}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.5}
\uses{def:intro-verifier,thm:qld,lem:pauli-mixing,def:state-distance}
There is $\delta_1(\eps, R) = a\bigl( (\log R)^a \eps^b + (\log R)^{-b} \bigr)$, with $a > 1$
and $0 < b < 1$ depending only on $\ell$, such that any strategy for $\vint_n$ of value
$1 - \eps$ is within $\delta_1$ of a projective strategy whose state is
$\ket{\EPR_2}^{\ot Q} \ot \ket{\aux}$ and whose Pauli-test measurements are the actual Pauli
operators $\sigma^W_x$ on those $Q$ qubits, for both players.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{thm:qld,lem:pauli-mixing}
This is Theorem~\ref{thm:qld} applied to the Pauli-test part of the decider, followed by the
mixing estimate of Lemma~\ref{lem:pauli-mixing} to replace the strategy's own measurements by ones that
commute with the Pauli basis.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The formal route keeps the paper's estimates as distances instead of
replacing the strategy: Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-pauli-extraction} is the application of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qld}, through Corollary~\ref{cor:qld-binary}, that this proof makes, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-valid-isometric-soundness} accounts for the replacement. The statement above,
with its exact state and exact Pauli operators, is not formalized as such.

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Sampling and hiding subtest estimates]
\label{lem:intro-subtest-estimates}
\uses{def:inconsistency,def:state-distance,def:cl-function}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.CLFun.SupportedOn.proj_eval_cons,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.SupportedOn.outputPrefix_eval,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.ExactlyOn.outputPrefix_self,
  MIPRE.CL.CLFun.ExactlyOn.outputPrefix_univ,
  MIPRE.Introspection.agreement_subtest_average,
  MIPRE.Introspection.sampling_pauli_estimate,
  MIPRE.Introspection.sampling_prefix_estimate,
  MIPRE.Introspection.mapped_operator_zero_of_not_range,
  MIPRE.Introspection.off_range_weight_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.same_side_via_common_other,
  MIPRE.Introspection.hiding_same_side_estimate,
  MIPRE.Introspection.operator_chain_telescope,
  MIPRE.Introspection.stateSqNorm_hiding_chain,
  MIPRE.Introspection.hiding_chain_average,
  MIPRE.Introspection.hiding_chain_uniform}
\leanok
Construct a CL output-prefix map on every claimed full output; on honest outputs
it agrees with the truncated CL evaluation. For any finite game, a subtest of
weight $c>0$ whose accepted answers force equality of processed outcomes gives
averaged squared cross-party distance at most $2\eps/c$, where the game's
failure is at most $\eps$. The probability hypothesis is a pushforward bound,
so the sampled question map need not be injective. Replacing one side by ideal
Pauli readouts of squared error $\eta$ costs at most $2\eta+4\eps/c$.
All output values, including impossible claims, are controlled. Two such tests
with a common opposite-party measurement give a same-side bound
$4\eps/c_1+4\eps/c_2$. A chain of $k$ squared edge errors bounded by $\delta$
costs at most $k^2\delta$, without an outcome-cardinality factor.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Coarse-grain the accepted-answer relations before converting consistency to
distance. The opposite-party triangle inequality then gives the same-side
estimate. Telescope the operator chain and use Cauchy--Schwarz. These are
checked subtest lemmas; their acceptance implications and probability bounds
must still be instantiated for the actual introspective decision procedure.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Concrete typed sampling and terminal hiding estimates]
\label{lem:intro-typed-estimates}
\uses{lem:intro-parsed-predicate,lem:intro-type-presentation,lem:intro-subtest-estimates}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check_directed,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check_directed_reversed,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check_hiding_read,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedPredicate.check_hiding_read_reversed,

  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.hiding_read_estimate,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.hiding_read_same_side_estimate}
\leanok
For the concrete parsed introspection game of failure at most $\eps$, the
sampling-prefix and terminal hiding/Read cross-party squared distances are
at most $2|E|\eps$, where $E$ is the set of ordered type edges. Comparing the
terminal hiding and Read measurements on the same side costs at most
$8|E|\eps$. The predicates, question distribution, and Read consistency loop
are those of the constructed game; no separate acceptance or selection-weight
premise is assumed. Wrong answer constructors have a distinct coarse outcome,
so the estimates include the whole answer alphabet. The Pauli subpredicate
receives the actual Pauli question contents. The adjacent hiding induction
and product-form rigidity remain separate.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Bound each fixed auxiliary edge's conditional failure using its exact mass
$1/|E|$. The parsed acceptance checks imply the appropriate equality of
coarse outcomes, so consistency gives the cross-party bounds. Use the Read
consistency loop as the common opposite-party measurement for the same-side
comparison. Both hiding orientations are proved directly from the predicate.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The sampling test forces the question distribution]
\label{lem:intro-sampling-test}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.5}
\uses{lem:intro-pauli-extract,def:cl-function,def:inconsistency}
For the strategy of Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-pauli-extract} and each level
$k \in \{1, \ldots, \ell\}$, the player's reported question agrees with the $Z$-measurement
of the conditionally linear map at that level up to $\delta$, and the two players' reports
agree with each other up to $\delta$. Consequently the reported $y$ is distributed as the
conditionally linear distribution of $\verifier_{2^n}$, up to $\delta$.
\end{lemma}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[The hiding test]
\label{lem:intro-hiding-test}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.6}
\uses{lem:intro-sampling-test,lem:conditional-measurement,lem:pauli-mixing,def:state-distance}
For each level $k$, a player's answer on the $\mathtt{Hide}_k$ type is $\delta$-close to one
computed from the level-$k$ complement alone, and the chain of these relations across levels
$j \leq k$ is stable: knowing the answer at level $j$ tells a player nothing about the
conditionally sampled part at level $k$ beyond what it is entitled to. The rigidity form
states this as closeness of the hiding measurement to an explicit product of $Z$- and
$X$-measurements on the complements.
\end{lemma}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[The soundness induction]
\label{lem:intro-induction}
\ledgernode{1.2.3.7}
\uses{lem:intro-sampling-test,lem:intro-hiding-test,lem:conditional-measurement,lem:close-submeasurement,def:ent,lem:intro-coarse-product-stage,lem:intro-adjacent-hiding,lem:intro-conditional-dilation,lem:intro-typed-final-extraction}
For each level $k \in \{1, \ldots, \ell + 1\}$ there are ancilla states and projective
measurements, acting on the player's space together with the ancilla, which reproduce the
strategy's behaviour on the first $k$ levels while acting as the identity on the subspace the
first $k$ levels have already fixed. Running the induction to $k = \ell + 1$ converts a
strategy for $\vint_n$ of value $1 - \eps$ into a strategy for $\verifier_{2^n}$ of value
$1 - \delta(\eps, n)$ on a state of comparable entanglement, which is the soundness and
entanglement content of Theorem~\ref{thm:introspection}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Exact final strategy extraction]
\label{lem:intro-final-extraction}
\uses{def:cl-function,def:tensor-strategy}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.readout_isPVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.bornProb_registerEPR_readout,

\leanok
For arbitrary finite question maps, computational readout of a shared EPR seed
has exactly their common uniform-seed distribution. Tensor each question
projector with a projective auxiliary measurement depending on that question.
These form a joint PVM, and their accepted mass is exactly the value of the
strategy using the auxiliary state and auxiliary measurements in the original
game. Discarding the question EPR register incurs no further error or dimension
factor. The identity is specialized to the actual CL distribution of a verifier.
This assumes the terminal tensor-product measurement form, which the soundness
induction below derives from arbitrary high-value introspection play.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Evaluate the diagonal projectors on the normalized EPR state; the result is the
normalized size of the common question fibre. Born probabilities factor over
the EPR and auxiliary systems. Rearranging the finite acceptance sum gives
the original strategy value. Package the auxiliary PVMs as a legal tensor
strategy and compare with the supremum defining quantum value.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Absorbing fixed introspection error losses]
\label{lem:intro-error-absorption}
\uses{def:state-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.errorProfile_power,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_errorProfile_power,
  MIPRE.Introspection.iteratedRoot_eq_rpow,
  MIPRE.Introspection.errorProfile_iteratedRoot,
  MIPRE.Introspection.errorProfile_power_of_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.errorProfile_scale_input,
  MIPRE.Introspection.errorProfile_scaled_power,
  MIPRE.Introspection.state_transfer_loss_le_three_sqrt}
\leanok
Write $E(a,b,x,\eps)=a(x^a\eps^b+x^{-b})$. For $x\geq1$, $\eps\geq0$,
$C,a\geq0$, $0<b\leq1$ and $0<r\leq1$, there are explicit $A\geq1$ and
$0<B\leq1$ such that $C E(a,b,x,\eps)^r\leq E(A,B,x,\eps)$.
One can take $A=\max\{1,Ca^r,ar\}$ and $B=br$. This also absorbs any fixed
number of square roots and a fixed rescaling of $\eps$, including detyping's
factor. For $0\leq\delta\leq1$, the state-transfer loss satisfies
$\delta+2\sqrt\delta\leq3\sqrt\delta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Use subadditivity of the $r$-power on nonnegative sums and monotonicity of powers
with base at least one. Each square root multiplies the exponent by $1/2$.
Absorb the fixed error rescaling into the coefficient before applying the
power bound. These are quantitative closure results; they do not replace the
soundness induction that must supply the error estimates.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Executable growing clock and original-index simulation]
\label{lem:intro-growing-clock}
\uses{lem:intro-ambient-runtime,lem:detype-total-clock}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.ClockArithmetic.uniformProg_closed,
  MIPRE.Introspection.ClockArithmetic.uniformProg_runs,
  MIPRE.Introspection.growingClock_budget,
  MIPRE.Introspection.growingClock_runs,
  MIPRE.Introspection.growingClock_size,
  MIPRE.Introspection.growingClockCompiler_apply,
  MIPRE.Introspection.growingClockCompiler_runs,
  MIPRE.Introspection.growingClock_polynomial_time,
  MIPRE.Introspection.growingClock_ansBound_time,
  MIPRE.Introspection.ClockSimulation.prog_closed,
  MIPRE.Introspection.ClockSimulation.prog_halts,
  MIPRE.Introspection.ClockSimulation.decider_accepts_iff,
  MIPRE.Introspection.ClockSimulation.original_decider_preserved,
  MIPRE.Introspection.ClockSimulation.compiler_apply,
  MIPRE.Introspection.ClockSimulation.compiler_binary_bounds,
  MIPRE.Introspection.ClockSimulation.decider_haltsWithin,
  MIPRE.Introspection.ClockSimulation.original_decider_ansBound_time}
\leanok
For each fixed exponent $k$, an actual closed program generates the unary budget
$2^{(\lambda n+1)^k}$. Hardwiring $\lambda$ adds only its binary encoding size
and a fixed overhead; the parameter-to-program compiler is polynomial time.
For $k,\lambda,n\geq1$, generation time is polynomial in that budget and is
bounded by a uniformly enlarged introspection budget. A total clocked simulator
reads the clock at index $n$ and runs the original program at $2^n$.
For a bounded source verifier and legal original questions and answers,
the exponent-five clock preserves acceptance exactly.
Its actual compiler has polynomial cost and output size in the source
description size and binary parameter length. For a bounded source and
positive index, the complete clock generation, reindexing, universal
simulation and result check fit a uniformly enlarged introspection budget.
This is the original-program simulation component, not the complete
introspection predicate or its cutoff parser.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Compose the checked unary arithmetic and exponentiation programs, hardwire
the binary parameter using the actual specialization compiler, and bound the
cost by a fixed polynomial in the output budget. The reindexing program
changes only the source index. Determinism and the legal-query runtime bound
show that clocking preserves the source's accepting result.
Compose the actual program constructors for compilation, and absorb the
complete execution cost into a fixed polynomial of the generated budget.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Executable answer parsing and guarded source calls]
\label{lem:intro-answer-parser}
\uses{lem:intro-growing-clock,lem:intro-parsed-predicate}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.AnswerParser.field_append_injective,
  MIPRE.Introspection.AnswerParser.pairCheck_iff,

\leanok
For fixed register length $Q$ and original-answer bound $R$, actual polynomial
time programs parse the paper's pair and triple answer encodings. They check
all register lengths and the separate original-answer cutoff. The encoding
uses $00,01$ for bits and $10$ for component termination; exact re-encoding
rejects extra fields, malformed delimiters and trailing bits. Under $Q\geq4$
and $3R\leq Q$, honest encodings fit strictly below the outer $8Q$ bound.
A guarded source-call program rejects invalid pairs before evaluating the
source. On valid pairs, acceptance is exactly source acceptance on the parsed
fields, with halting and polynomial runtime transfer for total bounded sources.
This component retains the input index and the full register questions.
Index-dependent parameter generation and projection to the original question
space are supplied by Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-dynamic-source-compiler}.
The complete auxiliary-test dispatch remains a separate obligation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Implement doubling and candidate decoding with list folds, then compare the
input against the re-encoded candidates. Injectivity follows from the first
delimiter. The checked one-call routing program tests the format and inner
cutoffs first, and calls the source only on its accepting route. Determinism
gives the acceptance equivalence, and the routing cost theorem bounds the
complete execution.
\end{proof}

The pair and triple parsers now take binary $Q,R$ as inputs; requested slices
are capped by the actual input length before unary scanning. A closed program

zero padding, projects both questions to the source dimension, and runs the

every raw input even for arbitrary source program texts. For a bounded source

Compose the routing and clocked simulation programs, use determinism for
acceptance equivalence, and compose their cost bounds. The AST constructors
and specialization compiler establish the binary description bound.

\begin{lemma}[Honest sampling core and first hiding test]
\label{lem:intro-honest-core}
\uses{lem:intro-parsed-predicate,lem:intro-final-extraction}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.Honest.coreOp_isPVM,

A perfect PCC strategy for the source game gives an actual perfect PCC
strategy for the induced four-type Introspect/Sample game, including its loops,
sampling edges and cross-introspection edge. Its dimension is the source
dimension times the seed alphabet size. Honest Pauli-$Z$/Sample and
Pauli-$X$/first-Hide effects commute and their products vanish whenever the
full parsed test rejects. All outcome labels are retained.
The extension to all adaptive Read and Hide types is proved in
Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-honest-full-auxiliary}. The complete Pauli subgame
remains a separate construction.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Tensor the source measurements with diagonal seed-fibre projectors.
Disjoint fibres annihilate, so source PCC is needed only for question pairs
arising from a common seed. The first hiding PVM is the coarse-graining
of the full Pauli-$X$ measurement that reports the first dual readout and
the untouched tail. Check the actual parsed predicate on every constructor.
\end{proof}

\label{lem:intro-honest-hiding}
\uses{lem:intro-honest-adaptive,lem:intro-parsed-predicate}

  MIPRE.Introspection.Honest.hideOp_reject_read_zero}
\leanok
The first marginal of honest Hide at index $k$ is exactly the readout of the
CL presentation truncated at $k$. Nonzero Read and Hide effects force their
reported vectors to be supported on the active register. The full Pauli-$X$
measurement factors under the actual local/tail coordinate split, and the
stopping Hide measurement has the corresponding dual-linear-X/tail form.
Neighboring hiding effects commute; for $k+1<\ell$, every answer pair rejected
by the actual adjacent-hiding check has zero product. For $k+1=\ell$, the
terminal Hide and Read effects commute and every rejected terminal comparison
has zero product. These statements include all outcome labels.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Induct on the CL tree. The common local Z/dual-X PVM annihilates unequal local
labels before the tail relation is used. At the first stage, the exact product
selects the common dual output and the exact stopping-tail answer. At later
stages, nonzero tail effects supply their own support conditions; the actual
verifier relations lift through the common local label. Both tail projections
in the adjacent comparison use the later reported question. The terminal
stage identifies the common prefix and complete dual output.
\end{proof}

The actual core, full Read, and Hide measurements extend to PVMs on the common
parsed-answer alphabet, acting on one register/source space. Wrong constructors
have zero effect. In both orientations of the Introspect/Read edge, every
pair of effects commutes and every rejected answer pair has zero product.
The same all-constructor guarantees hold in both orientations of every
adjacent Hide edge and the terminal Hide/Read edge, with their actual index
conditions and parsed verifier predicates.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Embed the proper pair, Read, or Hide labels and extend by zero. For Hide,
tensor the adaptive register PVM with the identity on the source register.
Summing over all constructors recovers the original PVM normalization.
Constructor case analysis reduces the reading edge to its exact refinement
identity; all other products vanish.
For hiding edges, identify the parsed check exactly with the corresponding
CL relation and apply the honest hiding product theorem. Tensoring with the
source identity preserves both commutation and rejected-product vanishing.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Adaptive honest Read and Hide measurements]
\label{lem:intro-honest-adaptive}
\uses{lem:intro-honest-core,lem:intro-linear-readout-projectivity}

\leanok
Recursion on the original CL presentation constructs the honest register
PVMs for Read and every Hide level, including adaptive branch-dependent
registers. The Read first marginal is exactly the original CL question
readout. Attaching the original question-dependent answer measurement gives
the full Read PVM, whose Introspect marginal is exact. Its effects commute
with Introspect, and every rejection by the actual parsed Introspect/Read
test has zero operator product. The hiding edges are proved in
Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-honest-hiding}; assembly with the full Pauli strategy
remains an additional obligation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
At a CL node, measure the commuting linear-Z and dual-linear-X outcomes on
the selected register, and use the Z outcome to select the continuation on
the complement. These factors are disjoint, so their controlled tensor
product is a PVM. Nonzero matrix rows force the reported CL question;
summing the dual outcome gives its exact computational readout projector.
Then tensor the original answer PVM and use its orthogonality.
\end{proof}

dimension. The recursive first Hide is exactly the full-register Pauli-$X$
coarse readout. The concrete parsed $X$/first-Hide and $Z$/Sample edges commute
and have zero rejected products in both orientations. Construction of the
honest strategy for all 26 Pauli types is still required for full typed

on one register/source space. Analyze every edge of the induced auxiliary

\begin{lemma}[Guarded maps on arbitrary hiding answers]
\label{lem:intro-guarded-hiding-maps}
\uses{lem:intro-parsed-predicate}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.CLChecks.prefixRegister_mono,
  MIPRE.Introspection.CLChecks.outputPrefix_outputPrefix,

\leanok
The next hiding comparison factors through the preceding fine hiding label:
its preceding question prefix, current dual output and remaining tail.
The later question prefix is retained as the key. A dummy label handles
malformed answers and disagreeing prefixes, so every keyed coarse family
is complete. Success in the actual parsed adjacent-Hide test gives conditional
squared error at most $2|E|\eps$. With preceding fine error $\eps_f$ and
prefix-normalizer error $\eta$, replacement by a commuting ideal prefix
costs at most $6|E|\eps+3\eta+3\eps_f$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Claimed prefixes nest even outside the image of the CL map. Equal preceding
prefixes select the same next register, and the next dual readout and tail
depend only on the previously retained tail. The actual accepted-answer
relation therefore supplies the guarded equality. Extract its probability
from the uniform ordered-edge law and apply conditional consistency and
the normalizer replacement of Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-conditional-normalizer}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Actual hiding-prefix propagation to Introspect]
\label{lem:intro-actual-prefix-chain}
\uses{lem:intro-guarded-hiding-maps,lem:intro-parsed-predicate}

For any hiding index $j$ and $m\leq j$, the actual Hide-$j$ and Introspect
measurements of the reported $m$-prefix on Alice's side have squared distance
at most $8|E|(\ell-j+1)^2\eps$. Every wrong answer constructor contributes
to a separate dummy outcome. No honest-image or support assumption is
imposed on the strategy's answers.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Construct the actual path from Hide-$j$ through the remaining Hide vertices
and Read to Introspect. Each accepted edge preserves the reported prefix.
Combine its cross-party estimate with the consistency loop at the next
vertex to get same-party error $8|E|\eps$. Telescope the chain with the
squared Cauchy--Schwarz inequality.
\end{proof}

\label{lem:intro-actual-normalizer-prefix}

The same prefix-chain estimate holds on Bob's side without assuming that
the Pauli predicate is symmetric. The actual conditional marginal in the
adjacent hiding test is exactly the later answer's reported-prefix

prefix has squared error $\delta$ from its ideal, then the later Hide
normalizer has error at most
$16|E|(\ell-j+1)^2\eps+2\delta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Use each forward path edge and the loop at its earlier endpoint to obtain
Bob's estimate via the common Alice measurement. Sum the joint coarse
outcomes to identify the actual prefix marginal exactly, then combine the
chain and Introspect-prefix distances by the squared triangle inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Dimension-independent conditional normalizer replacement]
\label{lem:intro-conditional-normalizer}
\uses{def:state-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.conditionalIdeal_isPVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_coarse_overlap,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_coarse_ideal_distance,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_normalizer_change,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_coarse_ideal_replacement,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_coarse_ideal_of_test,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditionalIdeal_mirror,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_coarse_ideal_replacement_mirror}
\leanok
Let $M_i,P_i$ be complete projective families on opposite parties and
$Q_y$ a complete projective prefix family commuting with every $P_i$.
For arbitrary keyed maps $f_y$, define
$T_{y,z}=Q_y\sum_{i:f_y(i)=z}P_i$.
This is a PVM. If the fine cross-party squared error is at most $\eps_f$,
the actual marginal $R_y$ has squared error at most $\eta$ from $Q_y$,
and the conditional comparison has squared error at most $\alpha$, then
the later measurement has squared error at most
$3\alpha+3\eta+3\eps_f$ from $T$. There is no alphabet-size factor.
Exact mirrors of the primitive prefix and fine families transfer this
conclusion to the opposite-party ideal family.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
All keyed off-diagonal Born terms are nonnegative because the ideal prefix
and fine projectors commute. The diagonal terms recover the original fine
overlap after summing the complete prefix family. Convert overlap to squared
distance with the prefix attached as a normalizer. Completeness removes
Alice's coarse projectors from the summed normalizer-change error. Apply
the three-term squared triangle inequality. Exact mirrors reverse products;
commutation restores the order in the ideal family.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The concrete ideal hiding step]
\label{lem:intro-concrete-ideal-step}
\uses{lem:intro-conditional-normalizer,lem:intro-honest-hiding,lem:intro-guarded-hiding-maps}

\leanok
The computational readout of the next truncated CL prefix is a complete
PVM with zero malformed outcome and commutes with the preceding honest
Hide family. Its product with the guarded coarse preceding family is exactly
the next honest hiding measurement under the actual test's retained map.
Discarding the redundant key bookkeeping gives exactly the next fine hiding
coarse family. Only CL support and the adjacent-level index condition are
assumed; the operator identities are proved from the constructed measurements.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The next prefix is a marginal of the next honest Hide PVM, whose effects
commute with the preceding Hide effects. Matrix support forces every nonzero
effect's claimed prefix to be fixed by prefix extraction. Expand the ideal
conditional product in the next PVM. Rejected products vanish; summing the
complete earlier family leaves exactly the indicated next outcome. Finally
compose the retained outcome maps.
\end{proof}

\label{lem:intro-actual-hiding-rigidity-step}

malformed outcomes. No conditional estimate, ideal commutation, mirror or

consistency. The retained map on parsed answers and the concrete ideal
product identity identify both sides with the next fine hiding families.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Product-form stage from coarse tests]
\label{lem:intro-coarse-product-stage}
\uses{lem:pauli-mixing,lem:intro-subtest-estimates}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.coarse_joint_commutator_bound,
  MIPRE.Introspection.productStage_mixing_premises,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_product_stage_of_coarse_tests}
\leanok
For a current projective measurement on register $V$, two actual
opposite-party coarse joint PVMs provide the tested $Z$ and $X$ marginals.
A common sampling marginal provides the remaining linear-readout consistency.
If the sum of these six averaged errors is at most $\eps$ and
$16\eps\leq1$, the stage constructs actual POVMs on the residual register
obtained by removing $U$ from $V$, with product-form squared-distance error at most
$224\sqrt{\eps}$. Commutator estimates are conclusions of the coarse
tests, rather than hypotheses. The prefix-conditioned identification and
iteration across all CL levels remain separate obligations.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Apply commutation analysis to each coarse joint PVM, and combine the two
sampling comparisons on the same side. The resulting mixing premises are
bounded by $16\eps$. Apply ambient Pauli mixing on the actual nested
registers and simplify $56\sqrt{16\eps}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Conditional projectivization on a common ancilla]
\label{lem:intro-conditional-dilation}
\uses{lem:intro-coarse-product-stage,def:state-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.bornProb_extVecA,
  MIPRE.Introspection.stateSqNorm_extVecA_aOp,
  MIPRE.Introspection.dilated_pvm_distance,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditionalDilationOp_isPVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditionalDilationOp_compress,
  MIPRE.Introspection.conditionalDilationOp_born,
  MIPRE.Introspection.extVecA_registerState,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_conditional_projective_dilation,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_varying_conditional_projective_dilation}
\leanok
A family of residual POVMs admits simultaneous projective dilations with
one fixed ancilla state for every question and prefix. Prefix projectors are
left intact. Compression and Born probabilities are exact, and an original
PVM at averaged squared distance $\delta$ from the product POVM is at averaged
squared distance at most $2\sqrt{\delta}$ from the dilated PVM on the enlarged
state. A dependent-family version allows all local register carriers and
transported states to vary with the prefix, while retaining the same ancilla
alphabet and state. Explicit reassociation preserves the original EPR factor.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Apply simultaneous Naimark dilation only to the residual factor. Compression
identifies the mixed quadratic forms, and PVM normalization plus
Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the resulting distance. Jensen combines the local
bounds. For varying carriers, choose each local dilation and apply the same
weighted estimate. Reassembly via the adaptive register permutations remains
an obligation of the global induction.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Conditional estimate for the actual adjacent hiding test]
\label{lem:intro-adjacent-hiding}
\uses{lem:intro-parsed-predicate,lem:intro-type-presentation,lem:conditional-measurement}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.conditional_coarse_consistency,
  MIPRE.Introspection.CLChecks.prefixRegister_outputPrefix_succ,
  MIPRE.Introspection.CLChecks.dualReadout_outputPrefix,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.hiding_next_accepts_conditional,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.hiding_next_conditional_estimate}
\leanok
The next visited register and dual readout depend only on the claimed
output prefix. In the actual parsed adjacent-Hide game, the earlier player's
coarse answer is therefore conditioned on precisely the later player's
retained prefix. If the later measurement is projective, the conditional
squared-distance estimate is at most $2|E|\eps$. The conditioning marginal
is retained as an operator; it is not replaced by an unconditional distance
or an assumed ideal hiding measurement.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Prove prefix invariance by recursion on the CL presentation. The four
accepted-answer comparisons imply the conditional equality of coarse labels.
Use the later joint PVM and its marginal to convert conditional consistency
to squared distance, and the actual edge probability $1/|E|$ to bound failure.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Quantitative measurement and state replacement]
\label{lem:intro-value-stability}
\uses{def:state-distance,def:tensor-strategy}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.pvm_event_stability,
  MIPRE.Introspection.joint_distance_left,
  MIPRE.Introspection.testAcceptance_stability_left,
  MIPRE.Introspection.testAcceptance_stability_right,
  MIPRE.Introspection.testAcceptance_stability,
  MIPRE.Introspection.qform_state_stability,
  MIPRE.Introspection.povmValue_state_stability,
  MIPRE.Introspection.povmValue_failure_transfer}
\leanok
Replacing projective measurements at summed squared state distances
$\delta_A,\delta_B$ changes any test acceptance by at most
$2\sqrt{\delta_A}+2\sqrt{\delta_B}$. Replacing normalized state vectors at
squared distance $\eta$ changes the full game value by at most $2\sqrt{\eta}$.
These bounds have no answer-cardinality factor. The measurement-replacement
statements require projectivity, so the mixing output must first undergo a
quantitatively controlled common-ancilla dilation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Apply two Cauchy--Schwarz bounds to the accepted outcomes of the joint PVM.
Summing the unchanged party's outcomes removes it exactly from the distance.
For state replacement, the accepted effect is a contraction; expand its
quadratic-form difference and average with the normalized question law.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Quantitative extraction from the concrete typed game]
\label{lem:intro-typed-final-extraction}
\uses{lem:intro-final-extraction,lem:intro-parsed-predicate,lem:intro-type-presentation,lem:intro-value-stability}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.TypedExtraction.cross_check,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedExtraction.condWin_cross_eq,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedExtraction.cross_failure_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedExtraction.pairReadout_isPVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedExtraction.condWin_cross_readout,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedExtraction.exists_strategy_of_terminal_form,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedExtraction.exists_strategy_of_approx_terminal_form,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedExtraction.exists_strategy_of_state_and_terminal_approx}
\leanok
The actual cross-introspection test rejects every non-pair constructor and
has probability $1/|E|$. Terminal product-form measurements therefore
construct an original-game strategy on exactly the auxiliary spaces with
failure at most $|E|\eps$. With squared state error $\eta$ and terminal
measurement errors $\delta_A,\delta_B$, the proved bound is
\[
 |E|(\eps+2\sqrt{\eta})+2\sqrt{\delta_A}+2\sqrt{\delta_B}.
\]
No separate conditional-acceptance premise is assumed. The Pauli extraction
and hiding induction must still produce the state and terminal approximations.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Restrict the acceptance sum to the pair constructors using the parsed
predicate, identify their Born law with the original CL distribution, and
apply the exact auxiliary-strategy extraction. Combine the actual edge
failure bound with the proved state and measurement replacement bounds.
\end{proof}

The actual CL continuation acts only on the complement of the visited registers. Its next factor and linear map are computed from the original presentation. The prefix weights are the uniform seed fibre probabilities, agree with the Born law on the EPR register with arbitrary normalized auxiliary state, and sum to one. Full-carrier distances and commutators are exactly the weighted residual errors; impossible prefixes have weight zero.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Split the actual visited coordinates from their complement. Factor the diagonal prefix projector by fibre locality, evaluate its EPR mass, and apply the tensor squared-norm identity. The continuation support follows by recursion on the CL presentation.
\end{proof}

\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Projective reassembly across adaptive prefixes]
\label{lem:intro-adaptive-reassembly}
\uses{lem:intro-adaptive-prefix}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.prefixResidual_isPVM,

\leanok
Residual projective measurements assemble into one ambient PVM even though their coordinate spaces depend on the prefix. Prefix branches are orthogonal, so reassembly preserves squared errors exactly and global commutators reduce to the corresponding weighted local commutators. A common ancillary extension commutes with this concrete embedding.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Each branch is supported by its actual honest prefix projector. Distinct projectors multiply to zero; summing the branch normalizers gives identity. The same orthogonality eliminates all cross terms in squared distances and commutators.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Adaptive extraction and common-ancilla projectivization]
\label{lem:intro-adaptive-extraction}

Suppose the current residual measurements are projective and their three full-carrier marginal and commutator errors are at most $\eps$, with $0\leq\eps\leq1$. Using the actual prefix law and selected registers, mixing constructs residual POVMs with squared error at most $56\sqrt{\eps}$. A single fixed ancillary alphabet suffices to produce residual PVMs and one ambient replacement measurement with squared error at most $2\sqrt{56\sqrt{\eps}}$. Its residual support is exactly the next unvisited register.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Apply the exact conditioning identities to ambient Pauli mixing. Dilate the constructed residual POVMs with one common fixed ancilla, apply the quantitative compression estimate, average square roots by concavity, and use the exact prefix reassembly and register reassociation.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A concrete strategy after one adaptive replacement]

If the selected measurement has the stated current prefix form and the three stage errors are bounded as above, an actual finite-dimensional tensor-product strategy is constructed. The new state retains the original EPR factor, all other measurements are extended by identity, and the selected measurement retains the prefix and newly extracted Z factor. The absolute game-value loss is at most $2\sqrt{2\sqrt{56\sqrt{\eps}}}$. Arbitrary answer relabelling is allowed. These are single-stage hypotheses, not a completed induction from game success.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Construct the residual Naimark projectors and the ambient joint PVM, replace the selected question, and reassociate the shared EPR-plus-auxiliary state exactly. Pull the acceptance predicate back through answer relabelling before applying value stability; no same-party coarse-distance contraction is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[One stage budget from the actual game tests]
\label{lem:intro-game-stage}
\uses{lem:intro-adaptive-read-error,lem:intro-adaptive-marginal,lem:intro-answer-refinement}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.introspect_prefix_register_rigidity_alice,
  MIPRE.Introspection.stageAnswerRefinement_coarse_marginal,
  MIPRE.Introspection.stageAnswerRefinement_marginal_le_reported,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.introspect_adaptive_marginal,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.introspect_refined_stage_bounds,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.exists_game_adaptive_prefix_dilation,
  MIPRE.Introspection.adaptiveStepLoss_le_root,
  MIPRE.Introspection.adaptiveFailureBudget_mono}
\leanok
Write $e=|E|\eps$, let $\eta_Z$ be the primitive Bob-Z error and $\delta_H$
the fixed Alice hiding error, and let $r$ be the remaining depth. For the
current projective prefix product form with its valid-answer support, all three
refined stage errors are at most
\[
  B_r(e,\eta_Z,\delta_H)
   =64\eta_Z+(512r^2+224)e+64\delta_H.
\]
If this budget is at most one, the actual tests construct the common-ancilla
residual projectors. The replacement value loss is at most $8B_r^{1/8}$.
An explicit monotone numerical failure recurrence records this loss.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The Introspect consistency loop transfers Bob's prefix rigidity to Alice
with bound $8\eta_Z+28e$. Refining the dummy answer adds its old-prefix
block to the valid reported marginal. Orthogonality charges the sum of
these squared norms to the single dummy outcome, giving marginal error
at most $16\eta_Z+56e$. Combine this with the actual Z and dual-X
commutator estimates and apply mixing and dilation. Three square roots
give the stated universal coefficient and exponent. The numerical
recurrence does not itself construct a sequence of strategies.
\end{proof}

this extension. In particular the primitive Pauli, hiding, and Read errors
are unchanged when an Alice Introspect question is replaced.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The deviation vectors are extended by an isometry. Register reassociation
preserves their norms, and register ideals remain tensored with the enlarged

\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Initializing and advancing the actual prefix invariant]
\label{lem:intro-actual-successor}
\uses{lem:intro-answer-refinement,lem:intro-next-invariant,lem:intro-game-stage,lem:intro-fixed-errors}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.exists_initial_residual,

  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.exists_intro_successor}
\leanok
Every projective full-answer Introspect measurement has the initial prefix
invariant on its original auxiliary space. At each subsequent stage, the
actual game tests construct a projective replacement with the next prefix
form and valid-answer support. Its failure increases by at most the proved
stage loss. Every other question is its original identity extension, and
all fixed Alice hiding errors are preserved exactly. The raw selected
measurement identity follows from canonicalization and decoder recovery.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
At stage zero the visited register is empty; only prefix zero survives.
Transport the original measurement to its remaining-register carrier and
use a deterministic malformed answer on other branches. At a successor,
decode each refined residual before reassembly. Canonicalization preserves
game value and is overwritten at the selected question by replacement.
Attainable next prefixes satisfy the decoder's coordinate identity; all
other residuals report the malformed answer. Apply the actual stage bounds
and the quantitative replacement theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[An explicit admissible error threshold for finite iteration]
\label{lem:intro-iteration-threshold}
\uses{lem:intro-game-stage}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.adaptiveStageBudget_mono_depth,
  MIPRE.Introspection.adaptiveFailureBudget_nonneg,
  MIPRE.Introspection.adaptiveSmallThreshold_pos,
  MIPRE.Introspection.adaptiveFailureBudget_le_threshold,
  MIPRE.Introspection.adaptiveStageBudget_le_one_of_threshold}
\leanok
For nonnegative edge multiplier $m$, set
$C=(512r^2+224)m+128$ and define
\[
  t_0=C^{-1},\qquad
  t_{n+1}=\min\{t_n/2,(t_n/16)^8/C\}.
\]
Each $t_n$ is positive. If the initial failure and both fixed primitive
errors are at most $t_N$, the numerical failure after $n\leq N$ replacements
is at most $t_{N-n}$, and every common stage budget is at most one.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
When all three errors are at most $t$, the budget is at most $Ct$.
The eighth-power threshold and the bound $8B^{1/8}$ reserve at most
$t_n/2$ for the new loss; the other half bounds the previous failure.
Induct on the number of replacements, using the decreasing thresholds.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite iteration of the actual Alice strategy]
\label{lem:intro-actual-iteration}

  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.exists_intro_iteration}
\leanok
In the actual parsed-game setup, suppose the starting measurements are
projective, the primitive Bob-Z and every fixed Alice hiding error have the
stated bounds, and these bounds and the initial failure are at most $t_\ell$.
For every $n\leq\ell$ there is an actual projective Alice measurement family
with the prefix invariant at level $n$ and failure at most the explicit
numerical recurrence. Its auxiliary carrier and product ancilla state are
constructed recursively. Bob's measurement family is retained, all other
Alice questions have their exact iterated identity extensions, and the
primitive Bob-Z and Alice hiding errors remain unchanged.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Initialize the concrete residual measurement. At each successor apply
Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-actual-successor} to the current strategy. The explicit
threshold proves its smallness premise, and replacing remaining depth by
the full depth bounds the loss by the common recurrence. Exact preservation
of the fixed errors supplies the next stage's primitive inputs.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Terminal conditional readouts with malformed answers]
\label{lem:intro-terminal-invariant}
\uses{lem:intro-actual-iteration}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.terminalAuxPOVM_isPVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.IntroPrefixInvariant.terminal_some,
  MIPRE.Introspection.IntroPrefixInvariant.terminal_none}
\leanok
Suppose the CL presentation covers the whole register exactly. A terminal
prefix invariant gives a projective auxiliary measurement $T_y$ on
$\mathrm{Option}(A)$ for each reported question $y$. Every valid effect
is the question readout at $y$ tensored with $T_y(\mathrm{some}(a))$.
The malformed effect is the sum over $y$ of the question readout tensored
with $T_y(\mathrm{none})$; it is not assumed to vanish.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The remaining register is empty at the terminal level, so transport its
unique basis vector away. The valid-answer support invariant fixes the
reported question on each residual branch. Coarsen only that question
component; projectivity and normalization are preserved on the full
option alphabet. Reassembling the residual effects gives both formulas.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Player exchange and the actual Bob iteration]
\label{lem:intro-bob-iteration}
\uses{lem:intro-actual-iteration}

\leanok
Exchanging the players in the actual parsed game preserves its value after
transposing the Pauli predicate. No symmetry of that predicate is required.
The finite Alice construction therefore gives an actual Bob construction,
with its prefix invariant, projectivity, untouched questions, and the same
failure recurrence. The primitive errors required by the second player
are preserved by the first player's ancillary extension.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Swap the ordered type edge and the two seed coordinates. Check the actual
directed predicates and transport the register state and state distances.
Apply the proved Alice construction to the exchanged game and swap back.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Both actual terminal measurements]
\label{lem:intro-two-sided-iteration}
\uses{lem:intro-bob-iteration,lem:intro-iteration-threshold}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.adaptiveFailureBudget_add,
  MIPRE.Introspection.introTwoSidedState_unit,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.exists_intro_two_sided_iteration}
\leanok
If the initial failure, both primitive Z errors, and the required hiding
errors are at most the explicit threshold for $2\ell$ replacements, there
are concrete projective measurement families with Alice's false-role and
Bob's true-role terminal invariants. The final state uses only fixed local
ancillas, and failure is bounded by the single recurrence at $2\ell$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Run Alice's false-role block for $\ell$ steps. The threshold theorem supplies
the admissible failure at the midpoint, and the unchanged-question identities
supply Bob's primitive inputs. Run Bob's true-role block, retaining Alice's
completed measurement. Restarting the numerical recurrence is exactly
continuing it, so the two blocks have the stated combined bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Ordinary-answer extraction from actual terminal invariants]
\label{lem:intro-terminal-option-extraction}

\leanok
For a nonempty ordinary answer alphabet, both actual terminal invariants
construct an original-game strategy. If the final parsed-game failure is
at most $e$, its value is at least $1-|E|e$. Each malformed auxiliary outcome
is completed to a fixed ordinary answer; its mass need not vanish.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The valid full-option outcome has a unique parsed-answer preimage. Its
terminal effect is the actual conditional question readout. Map the malformed
auxiliary outcome to a fixed answer; coarse-graining preserves projectivity,
and newly accepted Born probabilities are nonnegative. The actual cross-edge
mass and the exact common-seed question law give the value bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A power bound for the entire finite recurrence]
\label{lem:intro-recurrence-power}
\uses{lem:intro-iteration-threshold,lem:intro-error-absorption}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.iteratedRoot_scale_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.adaptiveFailureBudget_le_power,
  MIPRE.Introspection.adaptiveSoundness_power_cases,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_adaptiveSoundness_errorProfile}
\leanok
For fixed depth and type graph, the failure after $N$ replacements is at
most a fixed coefficient times the $3N$-fold square root of a common input
error in the admissible regime. A second explicit coefficient makes the
same bound at least one outside that regime. Consequently the combined
bound has the paper's two-term error profile, with positive exponent at
most one and constants independent of the register dimension.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Bound each stage loss by a constant times an eighth root and induct over
the actual recurrence. Add the reciprocal of the iterated root of the
positive threshold to handle the large-error regime. Apply fixed-power
absorption to the common input profile.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite-game soundness from the primitive Pauli guarantees]
\label{lem:intro-primitive-soundness}
\uses{lem:intro-two-sided-iteration,lem:intro-terminal-option-extraction,
  lem:intro-recurrence-power,lem:hide-rigidity}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.introAliceZError_le_of_bob,
  MIPRE.Introspection.primitiveBudgetCoefficient_bounds,
  MIPRE.Introspection.primitiveSoundnessCoefficient_one_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.primitiveSoundness_power_bounds,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_primitiveSoundness_errorProfile,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.quantumValue_ge_of_primitive_pauli,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.quantumValue_ge_of_primitive_profile}
\leanok
Suppose the actual parsed-game strategy is projective on an EPR register
tensored with a normalized auxiliary state. Assume its failure and only
the primitive Alice-X and Bob-Z squared errors are at most $t\geq0$.
For a source CL presentation covering the whole register exactly, the
original game's quantum value is at least $1-Ct^{2^{-6\ell}}$, for an
explicit coefficient depending only on depth and type-graph size.
There is no assumed hiding estimate, future strategy, or smallness condition.
If $t$ has the paper's two-term profile, the conclusion has that profile
with uniform adjusted constants.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The tested Pauli-Z loop transfers Bob's primitive estimate to Alice.
Full hiding rigidity derives both required hiding families from the actual
game and the original two primitive estimates. A fixed coefficient bounds
all these inputs simultaneously. Apply the two-block construction and
ordinary-answer extraction in the small-error regime. Outside it the same
power bound is at least one, and quantum value is nonnegative. Absorb the
fixed coefficients and roots into the required profile.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Soundness on the approximately extracted state]
\label{lem:intro-extracted-state-soundness}
\uses{lem:intro-primitive-soundness,lem:intro-value-stability}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.sum_pvm_snorm_sq,
  MIPRE.Introspection.sum_pvm_difference_snorm_sq_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.sum_stateSqNorm_state_transfer_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.sum_bob_snorm_state_transfer_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.extractedSoundnessCoefficient_one_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.extractedSoundness_power_bound,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_extractedSoundness_errorProfile,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.quantumValue_ge_of_extracted_state}
\leanok
The preceding soundness theorem also applies when the primitive X/Z
guarantees hold on a normalized state at squared distance at most $t$ from
the ideal EPR-register state. If those errors and actual game failure are
at most $t\geq0$, the original-game loss is at most
$20C t^{2^{-(6\ell+1)}}$. The constants again admit the paper's two-term
profile uniformly in the register dimension.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
For two PVMs the summed squared difference on a nearby state is bounded
by twice its old value plus eight times the squared vector distance.
Projective normalization sums over all outcomes before estimating, so
there is no answer-cardinality factor. Transfer game failure using value
stability. For $t\leq1$, all three inputs are at most $20\sqrt t$;
apply the primitive soundness theorem and absorb this extra root.
For $t>1$ the bound follows from nonnegative quantum value.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Local-isometry transport and image-complement completion]
\label{lem:intro-isometric-strategy}
\uses{def:tensor-strategy,lem:intro-extracted-state-soundness}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.isometricPOVM_isPVM,
  MIPRE.Introspection.isometricEffect_intertwine,
  MIPRE.Introspection.isometricState_unit,
  MIPRE.Introspection.povmValue_isometricState,
  MIPRE.Introspection.isometricState_map_alice_deviation,
  MIPRE.Introspection.isometricState_map_bob_deviation,
  MIPRE.Introspection.projector_mass_le_state_distance,
  MIPRE.Introspection.isometricComplement_alice_mass,
  MIPRE.Introspection.isometricComplement_bob_mass,
  MIPRE.Introspection.isometricEffect_alice_error_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.isometricEffect_bob_error_le}
\leanok
Rectangular local isometries transport any projective strategy to an actual
projective strategy on the target spaces. Assigning the orthogonal image
complement to a fixed outcome normalizes the measurements and preserves
every Born probability and the whole game value on the embedded state.
The exact operator intertwining also holds after arbitrary answer maps.
On a target state at squared distance at most $\eta$ from the embedded state,
image-complement mass is at most $\eta$. Thus a summed image-measurement
error $\delta$ becomes at most $2\delta+2\eta$ after completion, on either
party and without an outcome-count factor.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The image and its orthogonal complement are orthogonal projections.
Complete one outcome, then use the isometry identity to prove compression
and intertwining exactly. A complement projection annihilates the embedded
state and is a contraction, so its mass on a nearby state is bounded by
squared vector distance. Only the chosen outcome receives this complement;
the two-term squared triangle inequality gives the error bound.
\end{proof}

Any two anticommuting self-adjoint involutions on a nonzero finite carrier
extend, with one extra qubit, to the actual Magic Square game. Explicit
binary variable measurements and three-bit constraint measurements are
projective, preserve the original binary measurements at cells zero and
four, and give a value-one PCC strategy for the doubled game. Symmetric
input matrices give symmetric grid and constraint matrices.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Construct all nine observables by tensoring the original pair with the
single-qubit Pauli pair. Verify the six commuting triples and their parity
products. Their joint spectral projectors have zero effect for wrong
parity and zero joint effect for inconsistent incidence answers. Evaluate
these explicit projectors on the EPR state to prove perfect acceptance and
cross-question commutation directly.
\end{proof}

\leanok
All twenty-six actual Pauli question maps have explicit three-stage CL
presentations: seed, truncated direction, and the remaining point and scale
coordinates. The partition covers $3m+3$ field coordinates exactly. Both
full-Pauli questions evaluate to zero. A supplied binary field basis gives
three-stage binary presentations on $(3m+3)t$ bits with the exact Pauli-game
question distribution. Canonical encoding is inverse on attained outputs,
so arbitrary strategies for the binary game pull back to the actual Pauli
game with the same state, dimensions, value, and full-Pauli measurements.
This is mathematical sampler content; the uniform program and its runtime
are additional obligations.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Construct each register map explicitly and decode its full evaluation into
the original sampled question, including canonical line representatives.
Use the content-to-coordinate equivalence to identify the uniform laws.
Downsize through the given binary basis and number the coordinates. Encode
each attained question canonically and pull back measurements along this
encoding; the pointwise sample identity proves exact value preservation.
\end{proof}

The honest field-register Pauli strategy, with one extra qubit, has explicit
PVMs for all twenty-six question types and zero effects for malformed
answers. Its full-Pauli measurements are the required register readouts.
The actual trace probes are exactly the corresponding Weyl observables,
and the verifier phase is their commutation phase. For degree bound at
least one, the sampled low-degree, Pauli-point, pair, Magic Square, and
distinguished-variable edges have commuting effects and zero product on
every rejected answer pair.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Coarse-grain a common Pauli PVM for all same-basis questions. Its
multilinear encoding restricts to an affine polynomial on an axis line and
degree at most $m$ on any diagonal; evaluation at the sampled line parameter
recovers the point value, even for zero direction. For zero phase use the
commuting joint probe PVM, and for nonzero phase use the concrete Magic
Square extension. Its distinguished cells preserve the original probes.
Coarse-graining preserves commutation and zero rejected products.
\end{proof}

\label{lem:intro-honest-full-pauli}

for the actual question distribution and actual verifier predicate.

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Positive question mass yields one common content sample and an actual
ordered graph edge. A finite graph classification reduces forward edges
to the previously checked identities. Reverse edges follow by symmetry
of the predicate and commutation; loops use orthogonality of the same PVM.
Number the register basis and package the projective families as a
synchronous strategy for the doubled game. Zero rejected products give
value one under normalized trace, and the tested commutations give PCC.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite soundness from the valid-answer extraction interface]
\label{lem:intro-valid-isometric-soundness}
\uses{lem:intro-isometric-strategy,lem:intro-primitive-soundness}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.isometricPOVM_map_mats_eq,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.quantumValue_ge_of_isometric_images,
  MIPRE.Introspection.submeasurement_extension_mass,
  MIPRE.Introspection.submeasurement_extension_dist,
  MIPRE.Introspection.valid_outcome_error_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.isometric_valid_outcome_alice_error_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.isometric_valid_outcome_bob_error_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.validSoundnessCoefficient_one_le,
  MIPRE.Introspection.exists_validSoundness_errorProfile,
  MIPRE.Introspection.TypedEstimates.quantumValue_ge_of_valid_isometric_images}
\leanok
Suppose an actual projective parsed-game strategy has failure at most $t$,
and supplied local isometries put its state within vector distance $t$ of
the ideal register state. Suppose also that the image measurements have
summed squared error at most $t$ on valid full-register Alice-X and Bob-Z
answers. The original game has quantum value at least
$1-C\,\mathrm{root}_{6\ell+2}(t)$, for an explicit coefficient depending
only on depth and graph size. The estimate holds for every $t\geq0$ and
retains all malformed answers and image-complement mass. The local
isometries and their estimates are inputs here; Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-pauli-extraction}
supplies them for the actual game from Theorem~\ref{thm:qld}.
The final loss preserves the paper's two-term error profile with uniform
constants and a positive exponent at most one.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
A positive submeasurement within squared distance $\delta$ of a PVM has
missing mass at most $2\sqrt\delta$. Extending it inside the identity
therefore changes the error to at most $2\delta+4\sqrt\delta$.
Apply this to the valid raw effects inside the complete coarse images,
including their malformed outcome. Complete the local isometry images,
preserve the actual embedded game value, and apply finite soundness.
For $t\leq1$ the common input is at most $6\sqrt t$; for $t>1$ use
nonnegative quantum value. No outcome-cardinality factor appears.
\end{proof}

Scan for the first nonzero direction coordinate, compute the scalar with

canonical-line representative. Compose the polynomial runtime bounds.
The coordinate numbering is a sum and product of explicit finite
equivalences; its value equations identify every block and bit.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pauli extraction for the introspection game]
\label{lem:intro-pauli-extraction}

players' spaces onto the Pauli register tensored with auxiliary spaces, and a unit state
$\ket{\xi}$, such that the image of the strategy's state is within $T$ of
$\ket{\EPR} \ot \ket{\xi}$, and for both players and both bases the image of the strategy's own
$(\pauli, W)$ measurement, summed over the valid full-register answers, is within $T$ of the
honest Pauli readouts in the state-dependent distance. On the binary game the register is
$\F_2^{M \times t}$, the state is $\ket{\EPR_2}^{\ot Mt} \ot \ket{\xi}$ and the readouts are
qubit Paulis, for a self-dual basis; on the field-register game the register is $\F_q^M$ itself.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Restrict the strategy to the actual Pauli basis game (Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-pauli-restriction}):
same state, failure at most $N\eps$, and the same operators at valid answers. Apply
Corollary~\ref{cor:qld-valid} to the restriction, and for the binary game relabel the register as
in Corollary~\ref{cor:qld-binary}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} This is the paper's use of \texttt{cor:pauli-binary} in the proof of
\texttt{lem:intro-pauli-strat} (Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-pauli-extract}): the conditioning on the
Pauli block is the factor $N$, and the estimates are kept as distances, the form
Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-valid-isometric-soundness} consumes, rather than used to replace the strategy
by an exact one. All four Pauli estimates are proved, as the paper states them; the consumer uses
Alice's $X$ and Bob's $Z$. \texttt{MIPRE/Background/Introspection/PauliExtraction.lean}.

\label{lem:intro-extracted-soundness}

  MIPRE.Introspection.PauliExtraction.exists_quantumValue_ge_of_field}
\leanok
Suppose the original game's CL functions are exact on the whole Pauli register. If the binary
(respectively field-register) parsed introspection game has a projective strategy failing with
probability at most $\eps$, then the original game has quantum value at least
$1 - C\,\mathrm{root}_{6\ell+2}(T)$, with $T$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-pauli-extraction} and
$C$ the coefficient of Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-valid-isometric-soundness}.
\end{lemma}

\leanok
Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-valid-isometric-soundness} applied to the data of
Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-pauli-extraction}; the Pauli question's sampler is constant, and the valid
answer at a register label projects back to that label.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The exactness hypothesis is the consumer's and covers all $M t = M \log q$
coordinates of the register, whereas the paper's original questions have length $s(N) \leq Q$.
Reaching an arbitrary original game therefore still needs the padding of the source register to
$Q$ coordinates to be composed with this lemma; that step is not part of it.

computes the direction selector from canonical field bits; every index

space to compare with the old field enumeration; all decoded question
fields then agree literally. Transport the uniform distribution through
that permutation.

\label{rem:intro-padded-dual-format}

and dual data. Its honest Read and Hide dual labels can carry unused
coordinates. The new executable Read check implements this full-register
variant, and the Sample program implements the padded sampling relation.
The executable Hide checks use a row-space membership relation on the full
register. A question-local quotient decoder converts accepted dual labels
to the semantic local dual readouts, without identifying the two coordinate
orders. Honest semantic labels satisfy the relaxed relation unchanged.
Prefix guards and attained Introspect outputs supply the support needed by
the clocked source calls. This bridge is proved in
Lemma~\ref{lem:intro-quotient-kernel}; it does not require the full-register
dual labels to lie in the original source subspace.

\subsubsection{The theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Introspection]
\label{thm:introspection}
\ledgernode{1.2, 1.2.3.8}
\uses{def:normal-verifier,def:lambda-bounded,def:pcc,thm:qld,def:cl-function,def:intro-verifier,lem:intro-complexity,lem:intro-completeness,lem:intro-induction}
In the source model, a Turing machine $\ComputeIntroVerifier$ runs in time
$\poly(\abs{\verifier},\log\lambda,\ell)$, polynomial in its input length for each
fixed $\ell$. On input $(\verifier, \lambda, \ell)$ it returns a $5$-level normal form verifier $\vint =
(\sint, \dint)$ with
$\TIME_{\sint}(n) = \poly(n, \lambda, \ell)$,
$\TIME_{\dint}(n) = \poly(2^{\lambda n}, \ell)$ and $\abs{\dint} = \poly(\lambda,
\ell)$, with its decider in standard timeout-counter form, such that for every
$\ell$-level verifier $\verifier$ satisfying the source absolute $\lambda$-bound there
is a function $\delta(\eps, n) = a\bigl( (\lambda n)^a \eps^b + (\lambda n)^{-b}
\bigr)$ (constants $a, b$ depending only on $\ell$) with, for all $n$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item (\textbf{Completeness}) If $\verifier_{2^n}$ has a value-$1$ PCC strategy, so
  does $\vint_n$.
\item (\textbf{Soundness}) If $\valstar(\vint_n) > 1 - \eps$ then
  $\valstar(\verifier_{2^n}) \geq 1 - \delta(\eps, n)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{definition}[Ambient introspection contract]
\label{def:introspection-contract}
\uses{def:normal-verifier,def:lambda-bounded,def:pcc}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection}
\leanok
The structure \texttt{MIPRE.Introspection} is an ambient contract,
parametrized by $\ell$: the introspective sampler as a function of $\lambda$ alone with its
program computable in polynomial time, the introspective decider's program as a
polynomial-time function of the input programs and $\lambda$, the packaging as a $5$-level
normal form verifier on every input, the complexity clauses for every input and index ---
sampler and decider runtime coefficients at most $(\lambda n + 1)^C$ and
$2^{(\lambda n + 1)^C}$, respectively, both at input-size degree $C$, and
$\abs{\dint} \leq C(\lambda + 1)^C$ for a constant $C$, the ambient analogue of
$C_{\mathrm{intro}}$, with
the decider rejecting answers longer than $2^{(\lambda n+1)^C}$. For ambient
$\lambda$-bounded inputs, completeness transfers \texttt{HasPerfectPCC} from index
$2^n$ at answer cut $(2^n)^\lambda$ to index $n$ at cut $2^{(\lambda n+1)^C}$.
For $n\geq1$, soundness transfers a value greater than $1-\eps$, for $\eps>0$, at the output cut
to a value at least $1-\delta(\eps,n)$ at the input cut, where
$\delta(\eps,n)=a((\lambda n)^a\eps^b+(\lambda n)^{-b})$, $a\geq1$ and $0<b\leq1$.
The complexity clauses are stated in the
vocabulary of resource budgets (\texttt{MIPRE.Budget}, \texttt{Verifier.Within}: the running-time
coefficients of sampler and decider and their degree, the question dimension, and the answer
length beyond which the decider rejects), which lets the exterior transformations be
chained. The composition \texttt{MIPRE.GapCompression.ofPipeline} consumes an instance at
$\ell = 7$. Here $\lambda$-boundedness is the ambient definition, not the source
absolute-time hypothesis. This definition specifies the contract, without constructing
an inhabitant. Since $\ell$ is fixed by the structure, its polynomial-time construction
field does not assert a bound polynomial in binary $\ell$.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[The positive-index soundness domain]
\label{rem:introspection-positive-index}
\uses{def:introspection-contract,def:lambda-bounded}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.delta_zero_index, MIPRE.Introspection.two_le_exp_index_iff}
\leanok
The ambient soundness contract requires $n\geq1$. For nonzero $a,b$, Lean's real
power convention makes $\delta(\eps,0)=0$; retaining soundness at zero would force
an unintended exact conclusion at original index $1$. The paper's overview starts
with $n\geq1$, although its later theorem display quantifies over all naturals.
Moreover $2\leq2^n$ holds exactly when $n\geq1$, matching the bounded-input domain.
The compression consumer already has $n\geq2$ and supplies this hypothesis.
\end{remark}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Evaluate the two zero-base powers using their nonzero exponents. The index
equivalence follows from monotonicity of $2^n$ and its value at zero.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Supplying ambient introspection]
\label{lem:introspection-supply}
\uses{def:introspection-contract,lem:intro-ambient-kernel,lem:intro-numbered-transport,rem:runtime-normalization,rem:pcc-representation-bridge}
\lean{MIPRE.Introspection.exists_seven,
  MIPRE.Introspection.seven,
  MIPRE.Introspection.CanonicalComplete.output_hasPerfectPCC,
  MIPRE.Introspection.CompiledSoundness.output_soundness}
\leanok
The ambient contract \texttt{MIPRE.Introspection 7} is inhabited.
The fixed instance \texttt{MIPRE.Introspection.seven} supplies the introspection
input to the compression pipeline. It has one common all-index resource
exponent and the required soundness profile, and handles the exceptional
zero cases. Its scope is the ambient contract at $\ell=7$;
Theorem~\ref{thm:introspection} separately states the source-model result for
arbitrary fixed depth.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Choose one even canonical parameter constant that absorbs the QLD field-size
tails, and use the proved clocked universal machine. Combine the sampler,
decider, cutoff and description estimates into one resource exponent.
Encode the supported honest finite strategy to obtain ambient perfect PCC
completeness, using the zero-index branch separately. For positive-index
soundness, restrict to the enforced answer cutoff, detype, decode finite
answers and apply actual QLD extraction followed by the source-game bound.
Absorb the fixed detyping loss into the profile constants. These constructions
fill every field of the ambient contract; their axiom audit contains only
\texttt{propext}, \texttt{Classical.choice} and \texttt{Quot.sound}.
\end{proof}

The checked soundness analysis follows the rigidity argument of
\cite[Section 8]{JNVWY20} and techniques introduced in~\cite{NW19}: the Pauli
basis test and the twirling, commutation and sandwiching toolkit. These lemmas
use the bipartite setting, as in the sources; the pipeline carries $\valstar$
throughout (Remark~\ref{rem:bipartite-route}).

\subsection{Oracularization}
\label{sec:ps-oracularization}

Oracularization converts a normal form verifier into one where a single player (the
``oracle'') receives both original questions and answers both, while the other player
receives one of the two questions and must answer consistently. This makes the game's
predicate a pointwise function of one player's answer --- the format required by answer
reduction. This is probably the easiest transformation in the paper and could be attempted first to fix the semantics. 

\begin{theorem}[Oracularization]
\label{thm:oracularization}
\ledgernode{1.3.5}
\uses{def:normal-verifier,def:pcc,def:sync-strategy}
\lean{MIPRE.Oracularization.construction,
  MIPRE.Oracularization.typed_soundness, MIPRE.Oracularization.typed_completeness,
  MIPRE.Oracularization.detyped, MIPRE.Oracularization.detyped_completeness,
  MIPRE.Oracularization.detyped_soundness}
\leanok
The source theorem supplies a polynomial-time Turing machine $\ComputeOracleVerifier$
mapping an $\ell$-level normal form verifier $\verifier = (\sampler, \decider)$,
whose decider is in standard timeout-counter form, to a \emph{typed} verifier $\vora$
such that, for some $\delta(\eps) = O(\sqrt{\eps})$ and all $n$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item (\textbf{Completeness}) If $\verifier_n$ has a value-$1$ PCC strategy then
  $\vora_n$ has a value-$1$ PCC strategy, in which moreover both players measure with the
  same family of operators.
\item (\textbf{Soundness}) If $\valstar(\vora_n) > 1 - \eps$ then
  $\valstar(\verifier_n) \geq 1 - \delta(\eps)$.
\item (\textbf{Complexity}) The sampler of $\vora$ depends only on $\sampler$, with
  $\TIME_{\sampler^{\mathrm{ora}}}(n) = \poly(\TIME_\sampler(n))$ and $\ell$ typed levels.
  The decider has time $\poly(\TIME_\sampler(n),B_\decider(n))$.
\end{enumerate}
The oracularized decider's preamble performs a type check, a question-length check, and a
bounded parse of each answer component against the decider's timeout bound $B_\decider(n)$;
answers failing the parse are grouped into one distinguished outcome, so the oracle
families are projective measurements and not sub-measurements.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\uses{def:oracularization-contract,lem:oracle-typed-sampler,lem:oracle-typed-decider,%
lem:oracle-decider-time,lem:oracular-typed-transfers,lem:detype-compiler}
\leanok
The typed oracularized verifier is the pair of Lemma~\ref{lem:oracle-typed-sampler} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:oracle-typed-decider}, and it inhabits the specification of
Definition~\ref{def:oracularization-contract}, with the running times of
Lemma~\ref{lem:oracle-decider-time}. Completeness and soundness of its typed game are the
transfers of Lemma~\ref{lem:oracular-typed-transfers}, read through the decider's acceptance
law, with $\delta(\eps) = 24\sqrt{\eps}$. Through the detyping compiler of
Lemma~\ref{lem:detype-compiler}, the verifier with $\ell + 3$ levels has the same completeness and
soundness with $\delta(\eps) = 1536\sqrt{\eps}$: the factor $16^3$ of the three types, under the
square root, times $24$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Formalized form.} The ambient model has no timeout-counter form. The formalization
replaces it with an index routine that supplies, at each index $n$, a parse cut $B(n)$ and a
simulation budget $K(n)$; the decider runs its core under the clocked universal machine for $K(n)$
steps, which makes it total, as the detyping compiler requires. Soundness holds at every budget.
Completeness holds once $K(n)$ covers the running time of the core, which is polynomial in the
running times of $\verifier$ at $n$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:oracle-decider-time}): this is the hypothesis
that the timeout bound supplies in the source. The typed sampler has the levels of the input
sampler, at least one.

\begin{definition}[The oracularized game]
\label{def:oracular-game}
\uses{def:game,def:sync-game}
\lean{MIPRE.Role, MIPRE.SeededGame, MIPRE.SeededGame.toGame, MIPRE.OAns,
  MIPRE.SeededGame.oquestion, MIPRE.SeededGame.shapeOk, MIPRE.SeededGame.gameCheck,
  MIPRE.SeededGame.oracleVsPlayer, MIPRE.SeededGame.oaccepts, MIPRE.SeededGame.oDist,
  MIPRE.SeededGame.oracular}
\leanok
A \emph{seeded game} is a finite seed space $V$, two question maps $L^\alice, L^\bob : V \to
V$ and a decision predicate $D$; the game it presents is the law of $(L^\alice(z),
L^\bob(z))$ for a uniform seed $z$, with predicate $D$. Its \emph{oracularization} is the
synchronous game on questions $\type^\ora \times V$, $\type^\ora = \{\oracle, \alice, \bob\}$,
and answers either a pair (from an oracle) or a single answer (from an isolated player), in
which the verifier draws the ordered pair of roles and the seed uniformly, gives an oracle the
seed $z$ itself and an isolated player of role $v$ the question $L^v(z)$, and accepts when:
each answer's shape matches its player's role; each oracle's pair of answers is accepted by
$D$ at the pair of questions its seed determines; equal roles carry equal answers; and each
oracle's component for the other player's role agrees with that player's answer.
\end{definition}

\paragraph{Source.} \texttt{oracularization.tex}, \texttt{sec:orac-def} and
\texttt{fig:oracle-decider}, with the samplers, deciders and typed question strings stripped
away: all oracularization uses of a sampler is the pair of maps $L^\alice, L^\bob$ on the seed
space.

\paragraph{Comments.} The oracularized game is synchronous with no extra hypothesis, which is
what makes the PCC vocabulary of Definition~\ref{def:pcc} available on it: equal questions
carry equal roles, and the third check then rejects unequal answers.

Two of the four repairs of Remark~\ref{rem:oracularization-repairs} are about the verifier and
are \emph{not} addressed here, and it is worth being explicit rather than appearing to have
discharged them. Grouping the decider's failed bounded parses into one distinguished outcome
(repair 3) has no content at a finite game-level answer alphabet, where there is no parse to
fail; what survives of it is the shape check, which rejects an answer whose shape does not
match its role. The truncation of answers to $B_\decider(n)$ symbols (repair 4) is a statement
about a decider and does not appear at this level at all. Repair 2, that the completeness
witness has identical measurement operators rather than a symmetric state, and repair 1, the
single square root in the soundness loss, belong to the two clauses, which are separate
obligations.

\begin{lemma}[Completeness of oracularization, at the level of games]
\label{lem:oracular-completeness}
\uses{def:oracular-game,def:pcc,def:sync-strategy,def:sync-value,rem:identical-operators}
\lean{MIPRE.SeededGame.oracleStrategy, MIPRE.SeededGame.isPCC_oracleStrategy,
  MIPRE.SeededGame.oracleStrategy_value_eq_one}
\leanok
Let $S = (L^\alice, L^\bob, D)$ be a seeded game and let $T$ be a PCC synchronous strategy for
the doubled game of $S$, whose questions are $(\mathsf{false}, x)$ for Alice and
$(\mathsf{true}, y)$ for Bob. Define a synchronous strategy $\Sigma^\ora(T)$ for the
oracularization of $S$, on the same Hilbert space, by
\begin{equation*}
  N^{(\oracle,\, z)}_{(a,b)} = M^{(\mathsf{false},\, L^\alice(z))}_a \cdot
    M^{(\mathsf{true},\, L^\bob(z))}_b, \qquad
  N^{(\alice,\, x)}_{a} = M^{(\mathsf{false},\, x)}_a, \qquad
  N^{(\bob,\, y)}_{b} = M^{(\mathsf{true},\, y)}_b,
\end{equation*}
and $N^{(v,\, x)}_u = 0$ when the shape of $u$ does not match the role $v$. Then
$\Sigma^\ora(T)$ is a synchronous strategy, it is PCC, and if $T$ has value $1$ then so does
$\Sigma^\ora(T)$. Both players measure with this one family of operators.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
That the oracle's operators are projections is where PCC is used: the seed $z$ always lands on
a question pair of positive weight, so $M^{(\mathsf{false}, L^\alice(z))}_a$ and
$M^{(\mathsf{true}, L^\bob(z))}_b$ commute, and a product of two commuting projections is a
projection. Their sum over $(a,b)$ is the product of the two sums, hence the identity. The
family is PCC because the four operators
$M^{(\mathsf{false}, L^\alice(z))}_a, M^{(\mathsf{true}, L^\bob(z))}_b$ at one seed commute
pairwise --- two at one question because distinct outcomes of a projective measurement are
orthogonal, one of each by the hypothesis --- so every product of them commutes with every
other.

For value $1$ it suffices, by Lemma~\ref{lem:perfect-rejects-nothing} read in the converse
direction, that every rejected outcome pair at a question pair of positive weight has
probability zero, and in fact each of the decider's checks that can fail makes one of the two
operators, or their product, vanish. A shape mismatch gives the zero operator by definition. A
pair the input predicate rejects gives the zero operator as well: its probability is zero by
Lemma~\ref{lem:perfect-rejects-nothing}, and the normalized trace on matrices is faithful, so
a self-adjoint idempotent of vanishing trace is $0$. Check~2(a) is orthogonality of distinct
outcomes at a repeated question, which is available because two questions of positive weight
with equal roles are equal. Check~2(b) is orthogonality again, after the oracle's other factor
has been commuted out of the way.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Soundness of oracularization, at the level of games]
\label{lem:oracular-soundness}
\uses{def:oracular-game,def:sync-strategy,def:sync-value,def:distance,lem:hs-closeness,%
