lem:close-measurements-close-values}
\lean{MIPRE.SeededGame.soundStrategy, MIPRE.SeededGame.soundStrategy_value_ge}
\leanok
Let $S$ be a seeded game with nonempty seed space and nonempty answer alphabet, let
$0 \leq \eps \leq 1$, and let $N$ be a synchronous strategy for the oracularization of $S$ with
value at least $1 - \eps$. Let $\Sigma(N)$ be the strategy for the doubled game of $S$ in which
Alice measures $\{N^{(\alice,\, x)}_a\}$ and Bob measures $\{N^{(\bob,\, y)}_b\}$, each
relabelled so that the oracle-shaped answers are grouped into one fixed answer. Then
$\Sigma(N)$ has value at least $1 - 24\sqrt{\eps}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Fix a seed $z$ and write $O_{(a,b)}$ for the oracle's measurement at $(\oracle, z)$ relabelled
onto answer pairs, and $C_a$, $D_b$ for Alice's and Bob's at $(\alice, L^\alice(z))$ and
$(\bob, L^\bob(z))$; the relabellings group the shape failures into one distinguished outcome,
so all three are measurements and not sub-measurements. Each of the nine ordered role pairs
carries a ninth of the question weight, so at any one of them a set of outcome pairs the
decider rejects carries mass at most $9\eps$ on average over the seed. Applying this at
$(\oracle, \oracle)$, $(\oracle, \alice)$ and $(\oracle, \bob)$ gives, on average over $z$,
\begin{equation*}
  \gamma = \sum_{(a,b) \text{ rejected}} \tau(O_{(a,b)}) \leq 9\eps, \quad
  \beta = \sum_{(a,b)} \tau\bigl(O_{(a,b)}(1 - C_a)\bigr) \leq 9\eps, \quad
  \alpha = \sum_{(a,b)} \tau\bigl(O_{(a,b)}(1 - D_b)\bigr) \leq 9\eps.
\end{equation*}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:hs-closeness}, $\sum_{(a,b)} \norm{C_aD_b - O_{(a,b)}}_{hs}^2 \leq
3(\alpha + \beta)$ at each seed, so on average at most $54\eps$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:close-measurements-close-values} applied at each seed with $\mathrm{acc}$ the
accepted pairs, the value of $\Sigma(N)$ at that seed is at least $1 - \gamma$ minus twice the
square root of that closeness; averaging and using Cauchy--Schwarz once more to take the
average inside the square root gives
$\valstar \geq 1 - 9\eps - 2\sqrt{54\eps} \geq 1 - 24\sqrt{\eps}$, the last step because
$\eps \leq \sqrt\eps$ for $\eps \leq 1$ and $2\sqrt{54} < 15$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Soundness of oracularization, for bipartite strategies]
\label{lem:oracular-soundness-tensor}
\uses{def:oracular-game,def:tensor-strategy,def:q-value,def:cross-distance,%
lem:close-measurements-close-values}
\lean{MIPRE.SeededGame.tensorSound, MIPRE.SeededGame.tensorSound_value_ge,
  MIPRE.SeededGame.quantumValue_ge_of_oracular, MIPRE.sum_snorm_sq_ge_of_close,
  MIPRE.sum_snorm_sq_chain, MIPRE.SeededGame.sum_snorm_sq_joint_le,
  MIPRE.SeededGame.succAt_oracle_le, MIPRE.SeededGame.succAt_tensorSound_ge,
  MIPRE.SeededGame.sum_failAt_four_le}
\leanok
Let $S$ be a seeded game with nonempty seed space and nonempty answer alphabet, let
$0 \leq \eps \leq 1$, and let $N$ be a tensor-product strategy for the oracularization of $S$
with value at least $1 - \eps$. Let $\Sigma(N)$ be the strategy for the game of $S$, on the same
state, in which Alice measures the first player's $\{N^{(\alice,\, x)}_a\}$ and Bob the second
player's $\{N^{(\bob,\, y)}_b\}$, each relabelled so that the oracle-shaped answers are grouped
into one fixed answer. Then $\Sigma(N)$ has value at least $1 - 24\sqrt{\eps}$. Consequently, for
every $\eps > 0$, if $\valstar$ of the oracularization of $S$ exceeds $1 - \eps$, then
$\valstar$ of the game of $S$ is at least $1 - 24\sqrt{\eps}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
A tensor-product strategy is projective, so no dilation is needed. Fix a seed $z$ and write, on
the joint space, $O$ for the first player's measurement at $(\oracle, z)$, $O^\alice_u$ and
$O^\bob_v$ for its sums over the answers whose Alice, respectively Bob, component is $u$,
respectively $v$, $C$ for the first player's measurement at $(\alice, L^\alice(z))$, and $P$,
$Q$ for the second player's at $(\alice, L^\alice(z))$ and $(\bob, L^\bob(z))$. Then
$C_u \ot Q_v - O^\alice_u O^\bob_v \ot \Id = X_1 + X_2 + X_3$, where
\begin{equation*}
  X_1 = (\Id \ot Q_v)(C_u \ot \Id - \Id \ot P_u), \quad
  X_2 = (\Id \ot Q_v)(\Id \ot P_u - O^\alice_u \ot \Id), \quad
  X_3 = (O^\alice_u \ot \Id)(\Id \ot Q_v - O^\bob_v \ot \Id).
\end{equation*}
The front factors are projective measurements, so summed over their outcome they cost nothing,
and each of the three deviations is the cross-party inconsistency of one role pair ---
$(\alice, \alice)$, $(\oracle, \alice)$, $(\oracle, \bob)$ --- at which acceptance forces the two
answers compared to agree; by the estimate of Definition~\ref{def:cross-distance} each is at most
twice that pair's conditional failure. So
$\delta_z = \sum_{u,v} \norm{(C_u \ot Q_v - O^\alice_u O^\bob_v \ot \Id)\ket{\psi}}^2$ is at most
$6$ times the sum of the three conditional failures, with no square root. Both
$\{C_u \ot Q_v\}$ and $\{O^\alice_u O^\bob_v \ot \Id\}$ are projective measurements indexed by
pairs, and on the pairs of isolated answers the input predicate accepts at
$(L^\alice(z), L^\bob(z))$ the second carries at least the oracle's acceptance probability at
$(\oracle, \oracle)$, since $O^\alice_u O^\bob_v$ is the sum of the oracle's outcomes with those
two components. The measurement-level form of
Lemma~\ref{lem:close-measurements-close-values} (the paper's
\texttt{fact:approx-implies-close-value}) then puts the conditional value of $\Sigma(N)$ at the
seed at least one minus the conditional failure at $(\oracle, \oracle)$ minus $2\sqrt{\delta_z}$.
The four role pairs used are distinct and each carries a ninth of the question weight, so their
conditional failures average to at most $9\eps$ in total, and with Cauchy--Schwarz taking the
average inside the square root, $\valstar \geq 1 - 9\eps - 2\sqrt{54\eps} \geq 1 - 24\sqrt\eps$.
The paper's chain \texttt{eq:oracle-1} to \texttt{eq:oracle-3} is not needed: the oracle's
measurement enters only through its joint marginal, so there is no marginalization step inside a
pair-indexed family, and the second player's two isolated measurements are multiplied in one
order only, so there is no commutation step. For the second statement, when $\eps \leq 1$ a
strategy of value above $1 - \eps$ exists and the first applies; when $\eps > 1$ the bound is
negative.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What the game-level clauses leave to the verifier level]
\label{rem:oracular-game-level}
\uses{thm:oracularization,lem:oracular-completeness,lem:oracular-soundness,%
lem:oracular-soundness-tensor,def:oracularization-contract,rem:oracularization-repairs}
Lemmas~\ref{lem:oracular-completeness} and~\ref{lem:oracular-soundness} are the two clauses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:oracularization} with the samplers, deciders and typed question strings
stripped away, and they settle the two repairs that are about the strategies: repair~2, the
identical measurement operators, is structural, because a synchronous strategy is one family
played by both players; and repair~1, the single square root, is where the constant $24$ comes
from. What they do \emph{not} do is inhabit
Definition~\ref{def:oracularization-contract}: the complexity clause, the bounded parse and its
grouping (repair~3), the truncation to $B_\decider(n)$ (repair~4) and the level accounting of
Remark~\ref{rem:oracularization-contract} are all statements about a verifier and none of them
appears at this level. The soundness constant here is $24$ rather than an unspecified $c$; the
ambient contract asks only for some fixed $c>0$, so the game-level lemma is stronger than the
contract needs on that point and silent on every other.

Lemma~\ref{lem:oracular-soundness} is stated for synchronous strategies, and a verifier-level
statement cannot use that form: $\valstar$ is a supremum over tensor-product strategies, and so is
the strategy transport of detyping, while the bridge from synchronous to general values
(Theorem~\ref{thm:almost-sync}) is off the critical path. The paper's own soundness theorem is
bipartite. Lemma~\ref{lem:oracular-soundness-tensor} is that form, with the same constant.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[The typed oracularized game]
\label{def:oracular-typed-game}
\uses{def:oracular-game,def:normal-verifier,def:typed-sampler-interface}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.CLFun.ident, MIPRE.roleFamily, MIPRE.roleGraph, MIPRE.SeededGame.ofCL,
  MIPRE.Verifier.seeded, MIPRE.pairEnc, MIPRE.pairDec, MIPRE.parseAns, MIPRE.oraclePred,
  MIPRE.encAns, MIPRE.Verifier.oracleTypedPred}
\leanok
Let $\verifier$ be a normal form verifier with at least one level, $n$ an index, $B$ a parse cut
and $T$ an answer cut, and let $L^\alice, L^\bob$ be the CL functions of its sampler at $n$. The
\emph{typed oracularized game} of $\verifier_n$ is the finite typed game, in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:typed-sampler-interface}, on the complete type graph with loops over
$\type^\ora = \{\oracle, \alice, \bob\}$. Its CL functions are the identity for the type
$\oracle$, as a CL function with the levels of $\verifier$, and $L^\alice$, $L^\bob$ for the types
$\alice$, $\bob$, the same for both players; its answers are the bit strings of length at most $T$.
Its predicate parses each answer by the type of the player who gave it: an oracle's must be the
serialization of a pair of strings of length at most $B$, an isolated player's a single string of
length at most $B$. It rejects when a parse fails, and otherwise decides as the oracularization
(Definition~\ref{def:oracular-game}) of the seeded game $(L^\alice, L^\bob, D)$, where $D$ is the
predicate of $\verifier_n$ on answers of length at most $B$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Value transfers of the typed oracularized game]
\label{lem:oracular-typed-transfers}
\uses{def:oracular-typed-game,lem:oracular-completeness,lem:oracular-soundness-tensor,def:pcc}
\lean{MIPRE.CL.CLFun.eval_ident, MIPRE.CL.CLFun.exactlyOn_ident, MIPRE.exactlyOn_roleFamily,
  MIPRE.SeededGame.typedGame_mu, MIPRE.SeededGame.quantumValue_typedGame_le,
  MIPRE.SyncStrategy.pushTo, MIPRE.SyncStrategy.value_le_pushTo,
  MIPRE.SyncStrategy.isPCC_pushTo, MIPRE.Verifier.valStar_ge_of_typed,
  MIPRE.Verifier.exists_typed_perfectPCC, MIPRE.pairDec_pairEnc, MIPRE.parseAns_encAns,
  MIPRE.oaccepts_of_oraclePred, MIPRE.oraclePred_encAns,
  MIPRE.Verifier.valStar_ge_of_oraclePred, MIPRE.Verifier.exists_oraclePred_perfectPCC}
\leanok
In the setting of Definition~\ref{def:oracular-typed-game}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item (\textbf{Soundness}) for every $\eps > 0$ and every answer cut $T$, if the quantum value of
  the typed oracularized game exceeds $1 - \eps$, then $\valstar(\verifier_n) \geq 1 - 24\sqrt\eps$
  with answers of length at most $B$;
\item (\textbf{Completeness}) if $\verifier_n$ has a value-$1$ PCC strategy with answers of length
  at most $B$, then for every $T \geq 8B + 3$ the doubled typed oracularized game has a value-$1$
  PCC synchronous strategy.
\end{enumerate}
The same holds for any typed predicate whose acceptance implies the oracularized predicate after a
reading of the answers, for soundness, and for any typed predicate that accepts an encoding of every
answer pair the oracularized predicate accepts, for completeness.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The typed game samples a uniform ordered pair of types and one common uniform seed, and the
identity gives the oracle the seed itself, so its question distribution is exactly that of the
oracularization. For soundness, a tensor-product strategy of the typed game, played through the
parse on the same state, is a strategy of the oracularization that loses nothing, because the
typed predicate accepts only what the oracularized one accepts after parsing; then
Lemma~\ref{lem:oracular-soundness-tensor} applies. For completeness, the strategy of
Lemma~\ref{lem:oracular-completeness}, whose two players use identical operators, is doubled and
its outcomes pushed forward along the honest encoding: the serialization for an oracle's pair, the
string itself for an isolated player's answer. Summing a projective measurement over the fibres of
a map keeps it projective and keeps commutation, and the pushed-forward strategy wins whenever the
original does, because an accepted answer has the shape its type asks for and parses back from its
encoding. The serialization of two strings of length at most $B$ has length at most $8B + 3$. In
the ambient model the input witness already answers with strings of length at most $B$; in the
source model that is what the truncation of Lemma~\ref{lem:oracle-timeout-pcc} provides.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The typed oracularized sampler]
\label{lem:oracle-typed-sampler}
\uses{def:oracular-typed-game,def:typed-sampler-interface,lem:universal-tm,lem:smn}
\lean{MIPRE.oracleSampler, MIPRE.oracleSampler_cl, MIPRE.oracleSampler_dim,
  MIPRE.OracleSampler.core, MIPRE.OracleSampler.route_dimension,
  MIPRE.OracleSampler.route_alice, MIPRE.OracleSampler.route_bob,
  MIPRE.OracleSampler.route_oracle, MIPRE.OracleSampler.core_runs_within,
  MIPRE.OracleSampler.oracleSampler_timeBound, MIPRE.OracleSampler.samplerProgFun,
  MIPRE.OracleSampler.samplerProgFun_apply}
\leanok
For every sampler $\sampler$ with at least one level there is a typed sampler over
$\type^\ora$, with the levels of $\sampler$, whose typed CL functions are those of
Definition~\ref{def:oracular-typed-game}: the identity for $\oracle$, and the CL functions
$L^\alice$, $L^\bob$ of $\sampler$ for $\alice$, $\bob$, the same for both players. It depends on
$\sampler$ alone, and its program is computed from the program of $\sampler$ in polynomial time.
There are constants $c, m, e$ such that, whenever $\sampler$ runs within $B(\abs{d} + 1)^k$ on
inputs $(n, d)$ and $B$, $\abs{\sampler}$ and $n$ are at most $W$, the typed sampler runs within
$c(W + 1)^m(\abs{d} + 1)^{e(k + 1)}$ on inputs $(n, d)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The program is a fixed core run on the program of $\sampler$ through the s-m-n construction of
Lemma~\ref{lem:smn}, as the repeated sampler is. The core answers a typed query with at most one
call to $\sampler$, through the universal machine of Lemma~\ref{lem:universal-tm}. The dimension
query is forwarded unchanged. A query at an isolated type is forwarded as the query of the
corresponding original player. A query at the type $\oracle$ is answered from the query itself,
with no call: the identity's marginals are the seed, its first stage map is the identity and its
later ones are zero, its first factor space is everything and its later ones are empty. The zero
vectors are written over the supplied vector rather than over the dimension, so the core never
allocates by a dimension it has not been given. A forwarded query is at most twice the typed
query plus a constant, and since $\abs{d} + 1 \geq 2$ the resulting constant factor is absorbed
into the degree, so no term exponential in $k$ enters the coefficient.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The typed oracularized decider]
\label{lem:oracle-typed-decider}
\uses{def:oracular-typed-game,lem:oracular-typed-transfers,lem:oracle-typed-sampler,%
lem:universal-tm,lem:smn,lem:detype-compiler}
\lean{MIPRE.OracleDecider.oracleDecider, MIPRE.OracleDecider.oracleDecider_total,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.oracleDecider_accepts_iff, MIPRE.OracleDecider.gameProg_accepts_iff,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.core_accepts_iff, MIPRE.OracleDecider.accepts_sound,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.accepts_complete, MIPRE.OracleDecider.deciderProgFun,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.deciderProgFun_apply, MIPRE.OracleDecider.typedPredicate_sound,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.typedPredicate_complete,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.valStar_ge_of_typedPredicate,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.exists_typedPredicate_perfectPCC}
\leanok
Let $\verifier$ be a normal form verifier whose sampler has $\ell + 1$ levels, and let an index
routine supply at each index $n$ a parse cut $B(n)$ and a simulation budget $K(n)$. There is a
typed decider over $\type^\ora$ that halts on every input, whatever the programs of $\verifier$
do, whose program is computed from those of $\verifier$ and the routine in polynomial time, and
which accepts $(n, t, x, u, y, a, b)$ exactly when a fixed core accepts it within $K(n)$ steps.
For $x, y$ of length $s(n)$ the core accepts exactly the answers that the bit-string predicate of
Definition~\ref{def:oracular-typed-game} accepts at the cut $B(n)$: both answers parse by role
against $B(n)$, the checks of \texttt{fig:oracle-decider} hold, and for each oracle the decider of
$\verifier$ accepts $(n, L^\alice z, L^\bob z, a_1, a_2)$. Consequently, for the typed predicate
the detyping compiler reads off this decider, at an inner cut of at least $8B(n) + 3$: if its
typed game has value above $1 - \eps$ then $\valstar(\verifier_n) \geq 1 - 24\sqrt{\eps}$ at the
cut $B(n)$, whatever the budget; and a value-$1$ PCC strategy of $\verifier_n$ at the cut $B(n)$
gives one of the doubled typed game, when the core's accepting runs on answers within the inner
cut take at most $K(n)$ steps.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The decider is a fixed shell run on the three programs through the s-m-n construction of
Lemma~\ref{lem:smn}. The shell asks the index routine for $(K(n), B(n))$ and runs the core under
the clocked universal machine of Lemma~\ref{lem:universal-tm} for $K(n)$ steps. This replaces the
paper's timeout-counter form, which the ambient model does not have, and it makes the decider
total, which the detyping compiler of Lemma~\ref{lem:detype-compiler} requires. The core parses
each answer by role and runs the checks that need no call. For each oracle it runs the game
check: two marginal queries to the sampler and one call to the decider of $\verifier$, all
through the universal machine. The decider of $\verifier$ need not halt on the inputs it
rejects, so both directions of the core's acceptance law go through inversion lemmas of the
programs rather than through determinism. The two value statements are those of
Lemma~\ref{lem:oracular-typed-transfers}, read through the acceptance law.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Running times of the typed oracularized decider]
\label{lem:oracle-decider-time}
\uses{lem:oracle-typed-decider,lem:universal-tm}
\lean{MIPRE.OracleDecider.gameProg_runs_within, MIPRE.OracleDecider.core_runs_within,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.core_timeBound, MIPRE.OracleDecider.core_accepting_le,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.budget_sufficient,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.exists_typedPredicate_perfectPCC_within,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.oracleDecider_runs_within, MIPRE.OracleDecider.oracleDecider_timeBound}
\leanok
In the setting of Lemma~\ref{lem:oracle-typed-decider}, let the sampler and the decider of
$\verifier$ run within $T_S(\abs{d} + 1)^k$ and $T_D(\abs{d} + 1)^k$ on inputs $(n, d)$, with
$T_S, T_D \leq W$. There are constants depending only on $\ell$ such that:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the core runs on every well-formed input $c$ within $c_0(W + 1)^m(\abs{c} + 1)^{e(k + 1)}$;
\item hence, if the budget $K(n)$ is at least that bound at the largest core input whose answers
  lie within the inner cut, the completeness statement of Lemma~\ref{lem:oracle-typed-decider}
  holds with no further hypothesis;
\item the decider runs on every input $(n, d)$ within $c_1(W + 1)^{m_1}(\abs{d} + 1)^{e_1}$ whenever
  $K(n)$, $B(n)$, the index routine's running time at $n$, the description lengths of the three
  programs and $n$ are at most $W$, and the degree $e_1$ does not depend on $k$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The core's cost is the sum of the costs of its stages, each bounded through the cost lemmas of
the routing combinators and the overhead of the universal machine. The answers of the sampler of
$\verifier$ to the marginal queries are sized by its correctness clause, as vectors of length
$s(n)$, and not by its running time; otherwise the time of the decider of $\verifier$, of degree
$k$ in its input, would compound to degree $k^2$. Affine size bounds are absorbed into powers of
$\abs{c} + 1 \geq 2$, so that no constant depends on $k$. The decider's own cost is that of the
index routine and of the clocked universal machine on the budget and the input, so the running
time of $\verifier$ enters only through the budget.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Oracularization specification]
\label{def:oracularization-contract}
\uses{def:oracular-typed-game,lem:oracle-typed-sampler,def:pcc}
\lean{MIPRE.Oracularization, MIPRE.Oracularization.inputSize}
\leanok
The structure \texttt{MIPRE.Oracularization}, parametrized by $\ell$, specifies the typed
oracularized verifier of an $(\ell + 1)$-level normal form verifier. Its sampler is the typed
sampler of Lemma~\ref{lem:oracle-typed-sampler}, so the oracle form of the sampler is
definitional. Its fields specify the decider: a typed decider over $\type^\ora$ of the programs
of the input sampler and decider and of an index routine supplying a parse cut $B(n)$ and a
simulation budget $K(n)$. The decider halts on every input, and its program is a polynomial-time
function of the three programs. It accepts only answers that the bit-string predicate of
Definition~\ref{def:oracular-typed-game} accepts at the cut $B(n)$: this is the oracle form of the
decider. It accepts every such answer within an answer cut $T$ once
$K(n) \geq c(W + 1)^m(Z + 1)^{e(k + 1)}$, where $W$ bounds the running-time coefficients of the
input verifier at $n$, $k$ is their degree, and $Z$ is the sum of the description lengths of the
input programs, the sizes of $B(n)$ and $n$, the dimension $s(n)$ and $T$. It runs within
$c'(W' + 1)^{m'}(\abs{d} + 1)^{e'}$ on inputs $(n, d)$, where $W'$ bounds $K(n)$, $B(n)$, the index
routine's running time at $n$, the description lengths of the three programs and $n$.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[The specification and the earlier contract]
\label{rem:oracularization-contract}
\uses{thm:oracularization,def:oracularization-contract,rem:typed-detyping,rem:runtime-normalization}
An earlier version of \texttt{MIPRE.Oracularization} was untyped and level-preserving, with value
transfers as fields. It was satisfied by a transformation that does not oracularize at all: keep
the input sampler, and let the output decider reject any answer longer than the parse bound and
otherwise run the input decider. At the parse bound its game is $\verifier_n$ itself, so
completeness is the identity and soundness holds with $c = 1$. No field saw the \emph{oracle
form} of the output --- that one player's answer alone decides the game check, and the other
player's answer must equal one of its components --- and that form is the only property of
oracularization that answer reduction uses: the probabilistically checkable proof replaces
exactly that game check (Lemma~\ref{lem:ar-construction}), and the decoding step returns to
exactly that form (Lemma~\ref{lem:ar-decoding}).

The specification of Definition~\ref{def:oracularization-contract} sees the oracle form in both
halves: the sampler is fixed to the oracle's, and the decider's acceptance implies the
oracularized predicate. It is inhabited by the construction, and the value transfers are theorems
about every inhabitant, both for the typed game and for the verifier with $\ell + 3$ levels that
the detyping compiler produces. In the source, oracularization is internal to the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:answer-reduction}, whose analysis uses the typed verifier and detypes only the
final answer-reduced verifier. The checked composition \texttt{MIPRE.GapCompression.ofPipeline}
does not consume \texttt{MIPRE.Oracularization}, and the detyped verifier is not a stage of
compression.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[What the audit changed in oracularization]
\label{rem:oracularization-repairs}
\ledgernode{1.3.5}
\uses{thm:oracularization,def:pcc,def:sync-strategy,rem:identical-operators}
Node $1.3.5$ was added late --- round 12 of the campaign found that
\texttt{oracularization.tex} was covered by no ledger node at all, although both of its
theorems are consumed by answer reduction --- and it carries four repairs that the
statement above has been written to respect.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The soundness constant $\delta_\ora$ carries \emph{one square root}: the step from a
  $1 - O(\eps)$ measurement-closeness to a $1 - O(\sqrt{\eps})$ statement about the value
  is an application of \cite[Fact 4.31]{NW19}, not an identity. An earlier revision of this
  blueprint wrote $\delta(\eps) = \poly(\eps)$, which is not merely imprecise but false as
  stated: $\sqrt{\eps}$ is not a polynomial in $\eps$.
\item The completeness witness's extra property is \emph{identical measurement operators},
  not symmetry of the state. The campaign found the claim that the constructed strategy is
  symmetric ``by construction'' unjustified --- the hypothesis supplies a value-$1$ PCC
  strategy whose state is reused unchanged, and no lemma in the paper symmetrizes such a
  strategy while preserving projectivity, consistency and value $1$. SPCC was withdrawn
  from the completeness clause in consequence. The ambient interface starts from a
  different strategy representation; transferring this source witness requires
  Remark~\ref{rem:pcc-representation-bridge}.
\item The oracle families are genuine projective measurements. Outcomes failing the
  decider's bounded parse are grouped into a distinguished outcome; leaving that implicit
  made them sub-measurements, which the later sandwiching argument may not use.
\item The truncation of answers to $B_\decider(n)$ symbols is justified by a corollary of
  the timeout-counter form, not by the assertion that a timeout-form decider inspects at
  most $B_\decider(n)$ symbols. Nothing forces $B_\decider(n)$ to exceed the time bound
  $T$ against which the PCP decodes, so the truncation is load-bearing and a formalization
  must carry it explicitly.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}

\subsection{Answer reduction}
\label{sec:ps-answer-reduction}

After introspection the decider still runs in time exponential in $n$ (it simulates
$\decider$ at index $2^n$), and the players' answers are correspondingly long. Answer
reduction composes the game with a PCP: by Theorem~\ref{thm:succinct-sat} the
statement ``the original decider would have accepted our answers'' is a succinct 3SAT
instance; the players commit to a low-degree encoding of a satisfying assignment
(their original answers plus the computation tableau) and the new decider spot-checks
it, using the low individual degree test to certify the encoding and
Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel} for the arithmetization. Answers and decider time
become polylogarithmic in the original.

\begin{lemma}[Succinct decoupled 5SAT]
\label{lem:decoupled-5sat}
\ledgernode{1.3.2, 1.3.2.1, 1.3.2.2, 1.3.2.3}
\uses{thm:succinct-sat,def:succinct-3sat,def:decider}
\lean{MIPRE.SAT.DecoupledDescriber, MIPRE.SAT.decoupledDescriber}
\leanok
There is a polynomial-time algorithm that, on the inputs of
Theorem~\ref{thm:succinct-sat} under the same hypotheses, outputs a circuit with two inputs
of length $\ell_0 = \lceil \log 2T \rceil$, three of length $r_0 = O(\log T + \log
\sigma)$ and five single-bit inputs, with at most $s_0 = \poly(\log T, \log n, Q, \sigma)$
gates, which describes the decider $\decider$ on $(n, x, y)$ at time $T$ in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:succinct-3sat}: the variables of the decoupled 5SAT formula fall into
five blocks, the first two being the players' answer strings, and every clause touches
each block once. The parameters $\ell_0, r_0, s_0$ are computable in time polynomial in
the bit lengths of $n, T, Q, \sigma$. (The padded description, with the outer variable
count a power of two, is the padding step of the PCP, Remark~\ref{rem:pcp-power-of-two}.)
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{thm:succinct-sat}
Run the describer of Theorem~\ref{thm:succinct-sat} to get a circuit $C_3$ on
$3 r_0 + 3$ inputs, $r_0$ being that theorem's index width, and set
$\ell_0 = \lceil \log 2T \rceil$. Since $2^{r_0} \geq 4T$ and $2^{\ell_0} < 4T$ we have
$\ell_0 \leq r_0$, so an $\ell_0$-bit index compared with an $r_0$-bit one is zero-padded
and $i_2 + 2T < 4T \leq 2^{r_0}$ does not overflow. The output circuit accepts
$(i_1, \ldots, i_5, o_1, \ldots, o_5)$ when any of nine conditions holds: the first is
$C_3(i_3, i_4, i_5, o_3, o_4, o_5) = 1$, and the other eight are
$i_1 < 2T \land i_1 = i_3 \land o_1 \neq o_3$; \;
$i_2 < 2T \land i_3 = i_2 + 2T \land o_2 \neq o_3$; \;
$i_1 \geq 2T$ with $i_1$ even and $o_1 = 1$, or $i_1$ odd and $o_1 = 0$; \; the same two for
$i_2, o_2$; \; $i_3 = i_4 \land o_3 \neq o_4$; and $i_4 = i_5 \land o_4 \neq o_5$.

For a fixed triple of indices the signs of the blocks a condition does not constrain range
over all of $\{0,1\}^3$, so a decoupled clause is satisfied exactly when the literals of the
blocks it does constrain are. The last two conditions therefore say $w_1 = w_2 = w_3$, the
first two that the common assignment agrees with $a$ below $2T$ and with $b$ on $[2T, 4T)$,
and the middle four that $a$ and $b$ carry the blank padding above $2T$. So a satisfying
five-tuple is exactly a satisfying assignment of $\varphi_{3}$ extending the answer blocks,
and item~1 of Theorem~\ref{thm:succinct-sat} finishes it. Sizes and running times are those
of $C_3$ plus $O(r_0)$ gates for the eight conditions.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Two repairs this proof carries]
\label{rem:decoupled-5sat-repairs}
\uses{lem:decoupled-5sat}
An earlier sketch here described decoupling as a generic syntactic transformation --- split a
clause that touches a block twice by adding a fresh variable and an equality clause --- which
is not the construction the source gives and does not produce the promised index lengths:
$\ell_0$ is pinned to $\lceil \log 2T \rceil$ by the answer blocks, and a generic splitting
fixes neither that nor $r_0$. The proof above is the source's.

The parities of the four padding conditions are a repair of the verification campaign, and
the one place where a careless transcription breaks completeness rather than soundness.
Blocks are $0$-indexed and the blank encodes as $10$, so the honest padding begins at the
even index $2T$ and puts $1$ at even indices and $0$ at odd ones; the pre-repair text had it
the other way round, which the honest assignment violates. The formalization's tape encoding
agrees with the repaired parity, so nothing had to be adapted --- but nothing was taken on
trust either.
\end{remark}

\effortMathlib

\begin{definition}[The low-degree code]
\label{def:ld-encoding}
\lean{MIPRE.LowDegree.ofBool, MIPRE.LowDegree.pt, MIPRE.LowDegree.ind,
  MIPRE.LowDegree.eval_ind, MIPRE.LowDegree.degreeOf_ind_le, MIPRE.LowDegree.ldEnc,
  MIPRE.LowDegree.eval_ldEnc, MIPRE.LowDegree.degreeOf_ldEnc_le, MIPRE.LowDegree.codedOn,
  MIPRE.LowDegree.codedOn_ldEnc, MIPRE.LowDegree.coded, MIPRE.LowDegree.coded_eq,
  MIPRE.LowDegree.totalDegree_ind_le, MIPRE.LowDegree.totalDegree_ldEnc_le}
\leanok
For $y \in \{0,1\}^m$ let $\ind_{m,y}(x) = \prod_{i : y_i = 1} x_i \prod_{i : y_i = 0}
(1 - x_i)$, a multilinear polynomial over $\F_q$ which on the subcube $\{0,1\}^m$ is $1$ at
$y$ and $0$ elsewhere. For $a \in \F_q^M$, $M = 2^m$, with coordinates indexed by
$\{0,1\}^m$, the \emph{low-degree encoding} of $a$ is $g_a = \sum_y a_y \ind_{m,y}$; it is
multilinear and $g_a(y) = a_y$. For $H \subseteq \F_q$ the \emph{decoding} map $\coded_H$
sends a polynomial $g$ to the vector with $y$-coordinate $g(y)$ if $g(y) \in H$ and $0$
otherwise, so that $\coded_H(g_a) = a$ for $a \in H^M$; $\coded = \coded_{\{0,1\}}$ is the
Boolean decoding map, and $\coded(g) \in \{0,1\}^M$ for every $g$. Being multilinear, $g_a$ has
individual degree at most $1$ and total degree at most $m$.
\end{definition}

\paragraph{Source.} The paper states this in \texttt{preliminaries.tex},
Section~\texttt{sec:ld-encoding}, alongside Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel}; it is recorded
here, with its consumer, because Theorem~\ref{thm:pcp-decider} is the only place the blueprint
uses it.

\paragraph{Comments.} The identification of $\{0,1\}^m$ with $\{1, \ldots, M\}$ is any fixed
bijection --- the paper says ``lexicographic''; the formalization uses the repository's
existing little-endian \texttt{bitsVal} convention, so that the encoding of an answer block
agrees with the tape indexing of Theorem~\ref{thm:succinct-sat}. The choice is invisible to
the statements below, the same bijection appearing in the encoding and in the decoding.

\begin{definition}[Restriction to a line]
\label{def:line-restriction}
\uses{def:ld-encoding}
\lean{MIPRE.LowDegree.lineRestrict, MIPRE.LowDegree.lineRestrict_zero,
  MIPRE.LowDegree.eval_lineRestrict, MIPRE.LowDegree.natDegree_lineRestrict_le,
  MIPRE.LowDegree.sum_coeff_lineRestrict}
\leanok
For $p \in \F_q[x_1, \ldots, x_m]$ and a line $t \mapsto u_0 + t w$, the \emph{restriction of $p$
to the line} is the univariate polynomial $p|_\ell(t) = p(u_0 + t w)$, obtained by substituting the
affine parametrization into $p$. Its degree is at most the total degree of $p$, and its
coefficients, read at any degree bound $n$ that the degree does not exceed, evaluate back to
$p(u_0 + t w)$. So the low-degree encoding $g_h$, being multilinear, restricts to a polynomial of
degree at most $m$ on any line, which fits the degree-$md$ answer format of
Definition~\ref{def:lidt-cl} whenever $d \geq 1$.
\end{definition}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Padding preserves the complete answer tapes]
\label{lem:pcp-padding-semantics}
\uses{lem:decoupled-5sat}
\lean{MIPRE.SAT.Cnf5.sat_pad_iff, MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.DescribesDecider.of_pad}
\leanok
Embed the five blocks of a decoupled formula into larger blocks. Add every clause
whose first or second literal is beyond its original answer block and has the
blank-tape sign: positive at even indices, negative at odd indices. The enlarged
formula is satisfied exactly when the restrictions satisfy the original formula
and both complete answer tails equal the blank tape. Thus a circuit implementing
this clause set still describes the same decider, provided the original answer
block has length at least $2T$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Restriction proves the original clauses. To isolate an answer-tail bit, choose
the signs in the other four blocks so that their literals are false. Conversely,
each added clause contains a true blank-tail literal. Extend the three auxiliary
assignments arbitrarily, and use that every accepted answer has length at most
$T$, so its tape is blank beyond $2T$. This is the semantic part of the paper's
\texttt{prop:exciting-padding-prop}; the circuit algorithm and its cost remain
separate obligations.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Effective indexing and padding arithmetic]
\label{lem:pcp-padding-programs}
\lean{MIPRE.SAT.ArrayProg.eqBits, MIPRE.SAT.ArrayProg.eqNat,
  MIPRE.SAT.ArrayProg.getD, MIPRE.SAT.Fml.renameBy, MIPRE.SAT.Fml.eval_rename,
  MIPRE.SAT.ceilPowerProg, MIPRE.SAT.le_ceilPower, MIPRE.SAT.ceilPower_le_twice,
  MIPRE.Cost.PolyTimeFun.bitsValue, MIPRE.Cost.PolyTimeFun.predN,
  MIPRE.Cost.PolyTimeFun.addUnary, MIPRE.Cost.PolyTimeFun.subUnary}
\leanok
Binary equality, array lookup by a binary index, and formula renaming have
ambient polynomial-time programs. The least power of two above a unary number
can be produced in unary in polynomial time. Binary normalization, predecessor,
and addition or subtraction of a unary offset are polynomial-time as well.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Scan the supplied array instead of expanding its index. Rename the formula's
postorder input nodes. For power-of-two rounding, double a unary accumulator
once per binary digit, capping every intermediate state by twice the input
length plus one. Normalize binary numbers by a fold of the binary constructor;
the existing borrow machine gives predecessor, including a separate zero case.
The offset loops have explicit bounds on every intermediate encoding.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Effective padded descriptions with an explicit output budget]
\label{prop:pcp-padded-describer}
\uses{lem:decoupled-5sat,lem:pcp-padding-semantics,lem:pcp-padding-programs}
\lean{MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.formula5_circuit,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.describe, MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.describe_wellFormed,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.describe_describes, MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.innerDim_isPow,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.innerDim_le, MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.two_mul_le_innerDim,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.padGatesProg, MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.wellFormed_padGates,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.eval_padGates, MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.describeExact,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.describeExact_wellFormed,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.describeExact_describes,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.describeExact_gateCount,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.describeExact_variables,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.outerDim_isPow,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.outerDim_polynomial,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.innerDim_add_bound_le_gateCount,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.paddingParams, MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.paddingParams_apply}
\leanok
There are effectively computed parameters $m(T,\sigma)$ and $s(n,T,Q,\sigma)$,
with $m$ a power of two, $m=O(\log T+\log\sigma)$, $2^m\geq 2T$, $s\geq m$,
and $5m+5+s$ a power of two polynomially bounded in
$\log n+\log T+Q+\sigma$. On a valid specification and a unary budget of
length $s$, an ambient polynomial-time program constructs a well-formed circuit
with five $m$-bit blocks, five signs, and exactly $s$ gates, describing exactly
the same accepting answer prefixes. The parameter program is polynomial-time
in the binary parameter lengths.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Implement the padded clause predicate by a renamed formula and comparisons
with the original block bounds. Round the common width up to a power of two.
From the circuit program's proved output bound $s_1$, set
$m'=2^{\lceil\log_2(6m+5+s_1)\rceil}$ and $s=m'-5m-5$.
Append copying OR gates to reach exactly $s$ gates; this preserves the last-gate
output convention and fan-out two. The unary budget prevents an invalid short
input from demanding exponentially many output gates. In the PCP verifier the
formatted view will supply this budget; connecting that caller remains part
of Theorem~\ref{thm:pcp-decider}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Effective classical PCP parameters]
\label{lem:pcp-effective-parameters}
\uses{prop:pcp-padded-describer}
\lean{MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.pcpParamsProg,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.pcpParamsProg_apply,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.pcpParams_odd,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.pcpParams_field_large,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.pcpParams_inner_dvd,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.pcpParams_outer_dvd}
\leanok
The padded dimensions extend to ambient polynomial-time classical PCP parameters
$(k,m,s)$ with $k$ odd, $q=2^k\geq 82m'$, and both $m\mid q$ and $m'\mid q$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Choose $k=2\mathrm{size}(m')+7$. Then
$q=128(2^{\mathrm{size}(m')})^2\geq82m'$. Both dimensions are powers of
two at most $q$, hence divide it. Compute the degree from the binary length of
$m'$ and combine it with the already verified $(m,s)$ program. This supplies
the field-size requirement for classical majority soundness; stronger
low-degree-test field-size choices remain with their consumers.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Circuit arithmetization with bounded input use]
\label{lem:pcp-circuit-arithmetization}
\lean{MIPRE.LowDegree.eval_killCompl, MIPRE.LowDegree.degreeOf_killCompl_le,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.inputRef_injective, MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.routedConsistent_iff,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.eval_iff_routedConsistent,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.degreeOf_routedArith_le, MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.eval_routedArith_iff,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.eval_routedArith_bool, MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.degreeOf_finiteArith_le,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.eval_finiteArith_bool, MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.eval_iff_exists_finiteArith}
\leanok
Over a field of characteristic two, every well-formed circuit with $n$ inputs
and $s$ gates has a Boolean-valued arithmetization on exactly $n+s$ coordinates,
of individual degree at most five, hence at most six. An input is accepted
exactly when it extends to a Boolean assignment at which this polynomial is one.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
The current circuit interface bounds gate fan-out, but permits repeated reads of
one external input. Route the first read through the external variable and each
later read through its preceding input gate. These copy links use each variable
at most once and preserve all Boolean gate values. In characteristic two,
$1+w_g+\mathrm{rhs}_g$ indicates gate consistency. Multiply these factors and
the output literal. A variable contributes at most one degree from its own
factor, two from ordinary fan-out, one from a copy link, and one from the output
literal. Restrict to the exact input and gate coordinates by setting all other
variables to zero; this preserves degree and evaluation. The polynomial-time
field evaluator for this expression is supplied by Lemma~\ref{lem:pcp-circuit-evaluator}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Effective circuit polynomial evaluation]
\label{lem:pcp-circuit-evaluator}
\uses{lem:pcp-circuit-arithmetization,lem:pcp-padding-programs}
\lean{MIPRE.SAT.PolyTimeFun.casesGate,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.lastInputValueProg, MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.lastInputValue_range,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.gateBitsProg, MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.evalBits_gateBits,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.circuitBitsProg, MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.circuitBitsProg_apply,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.length_circuitBits,
  MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.evalBits_circuitBits, MIPRE.SAT.Circuit.evalBits_circuitBits_finite,
  MIPRE.SAT.shoupBinField_charP, MIPRE.SAT.shoupBinField_toBits_ofBits}
\leanok
There is an ambient polynomial-time evaluator for the routed circuit polynomial
on binary coefficient vectors, uniform in the supplied monic binary modulus.
Over a characteristic-two field containing a root of that modulus, correctly
sized inputs evaluate to the circuit polynomial at the corresponding field point,
including its restriction to the exact finite set of input and gate coordinates.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Dispatch the five gate constructors, scan preceding input copies, and use the
verified coefficient-vector addition and modular multiplication programs.
Multiply the consistency factors and output coordinate directly. The scan stores
only the supplied coordinates and preceding gate/value pairs; the accumulator
width never grows, even on malformed inputs. Its ambient cost therefore has a
global polynomial bound. Induction over the gate prefix identifies its accumulator
with the product of the polynomial factors. The Shoup field uses the same quotient
carrier and canonical coefficient representation after recording its exact degree.
This proves circuit evaluation; assembling the PCP verifier and its prescribed
runtime bound remains part of Theorem~\ref{thm:pcp-decider}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[PCP semantics for a circuit describer]
\label{lem:pcp-circuit-semantics}
\uses{lem:pcp-circuit-arithmetization,lem:pcp-proof-algebra,prop:pcp-padded-describer}
\lean{MIPRE.SAT.cubeIndexEquiv, MIPRE.SAT.clauseInput5_injective,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpParams.clauseInput5_pointClause,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpParams.pointClause_pointFromClause,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpParams.degreeOf_circuitArith,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpParams.eval_circuitArith_bool,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpParams.eval_iff_exists_circuitArith,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpParams.exists_proof_of_formula5_sat,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpParams.formula5_sat_of_majority,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpParams.completeness_of_describes,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpParams.soundness_of_describes}
\leanok
For a well-formed circuit with $5m+5$ inputs and exactly $m'$ input and gate
variables, the typed algebraic PCP is complete and majority-sound for the actual
five-block formula. If the circuit describes a decider, completeness uses its
accepted answer prefixes, and soundness recovers accepted prefixes and both
entire decoded answer tapes.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Identify little-endian indices with Boolean cube points by an explicit bijection.
The circuit's input order is exactly the five PCP blocks followed by their signs,
with inverse encodings preserving every literal. Transport the finite circuit
polynomial to the advertised PCP dimension. Its Boolean witnesses correspond
to precisely the circuit's clauses. Apply the honest-proof construction and
majority decoding, then the describer's exact accepting-tape relation. These
are typed algebraic tests; the raw verifier program is a separate obligation.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Encoded PCP view size and source runtime]
\label{lem:pcp-view-size}
\uses{lem:pcp-effective-parameters,prop:pcp-padded-describer}
\lean{MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.esize_bitBlocks_le,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.esize_view_le,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.pcpInput_size_polynomial,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.pcpProgram_time_le}
\leanok
Valid formatted PCP inputs for the constructed parameters have encoded size
polynomial in $L=\log n+\log T+Q+\sigma$. Consequently every ambient
polynomial-time program on these inputs has runtime polynomial in $L$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
A list of $r$ field blocks of width $k$ has encoded size at most $(4k+2)r+1$.
The two view lists therefore have total size at most
$(4k+2)(2m'+6)+3$. The padded outer dimension is polynomial in $L$, and
$k=2\mathrm{size}(m')+7\leq 2m'+7$. Add the valid specification's size bound
and compose the resulting polynomial with the program's ambient time bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The executable PCP tests]
\label{lem:pcp-executable-tests}
\uses{lem:pcp-circuit-evaluator,lem:pcp-circuit-semantics,lem:pcp-view-size,
  lem:effective-binary-field-arithmetic}
\lean{MIPRE.SAT.validProg_true_iff, MIPRE.SAT.viewFormatProg_true_iff,
  MIPRE.SAT.shoupRoot_equation, MIPRE.SAT.shoupRoot_eval_toBits,
  MIPRE.SAT.shoupRoot_eval_eq_iff,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpFieldTests.checksProg_true_iff,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpViewTests.checks_typed_iff,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.verifyPcp_reject_invalid,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.verifyPcp_time_le,
  MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.verifyPcp_rawView_iff}
\leanok
A uniform ambient program checks specification validity and all view dimensions,
constructs the padded circuit within the view's unary output budget, and performs
both polynomial tests. On a represented low-degree proof it accepts exactly when
the typed algebraic tests hold. Invalid specifications and malformed views are
rejected. Its runtime on valid formatted inputs is polynomial in
$\log n+\log T+Q+\sigma$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Use binary comparisons for the parameters and list scans for the shape checks.
Evaluate the circuit and both field identities using coefficient-vector arithmetic.
Fixed-width evaluation at the Shoup quotient's root is injective, so raw bit equality
is field equality. The clause coordinates and answer blocks agree with the typed
proof view. Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:pcp-view-size} to the assembled program.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Formula arithmetization and occurrence bounds]
\label{lem:pcp-formula-arithmetization}
\ledgernode{1.3.3}
\lean{MIPRE.SAT.Fml.eval_arith, MIPRE.SAT.Fml.degreeOf_arith_le}
\leanok
A Boolean formula has a polynomial arithmetization over any nontrivial commutative
ring, agreeing with its Boolean value on Boolean inputs. The degree in each variable
is at most the number of occurrences of that variable, including identifications
made by the assignment of formula inputs to polynomial variables.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Replace conjunction by multiplication, negation by subtraction from one, and
disjunction of $f,g$ by $1-(1-f)(1-g)$. Induction on the formula proves both claims.
The circuit application must bound external input occurrences as well as gate fan-out;
the current circuit well-formedness predicate only bounds the latter.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Boolean-cube zero certificates]
\label{lem:pcp-zero-basis}
\ledgernode{1.3.3}
\uses{def:ld-encoding}
\lean{MIPRE.LowDegree.exists_cubeZero_division, MIPRE.LowDegree.exists_zero_basis}
\leanok
Let $f$ be a polynomial over a field, of individual degree at most $d$, vanishing
on $\{0,1\}^m$. There are polynomials $c_i$, each of individual degree at most $d$,
such that the polynomial identity $f=\sum_i c_i X_i(1-X_i)$ holds.
Division in a single coordinate can be performed without increasing the degree in
any coordinate, leaving degree at most one in the selected coordinate.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Repeatedly replace the part divisible by $X_i^2$ with the corresponding multiple
of $X_i$. This decreases the degree in coordinate $i$ while preserving all other
coordinate bounds. Eliminate each coordinate in turn. The remaining multilinear
polynomial vanishes on the Boolean cube and is therefore zero. The general
Nullstellensatz total-degree bound alone does not give the individual-degree
bounds required here.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The algebraic PCP tests]
\label{lem:pcp-algebra-tests}
\ledgernode{1.3.3}
\uses{lem:pcp-zero-basis,lem:schwartz-zippel}
\lean{MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.completeness,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.degreeOf_constraint_le,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.degreeOf_zeroCombination_le,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.identities_of_majority,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.clause_satisfied_of_identities,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.clause_decoded_of_identities,
  MIPRE.LowDegree.eq_of_majority_agree, MIPRE.LowDegree.eq_of_majority_subset}
\leanok
Write $p=\varphi\prod_{i=1}^5(g_i-X_{s_i})$ and
$z_c=\sum_j c_jX_j(1-X_j)$. The algebraic verifier tests $c_0=p$ and $c_0=z_c$
at its sampled point. If $p$ vanishes on the Boolean cube and has individual
degree at most $d$, certificates of individual degree at most $d$ pass both tests
everywhere. Conversely, if $\varphi$ has individual degree at most six, all
$g_i,c_j,c_0$ have individual degree at most $d$, and
$|\F|\geq 2m(6+5\max\{d,1\})$, acceptance on more than half of $\F^m$ forces
both polynomial identities. On each Boolean point with $\varphi=1$, one of the
five answer evaluations equals its sign.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Completeness uses Lemma~\ref{lem:pcp-zero-basis}. For soundness, the two tested
polynomials have individual degree at most $6+5\max\{d,1\}$ and $d+2$,
respectively. Schwartz--Zippel forces equality from strict majority agreement.
Evaluation on the Boolean cube annihilates every zero-certificate term, so the
product of the five literal factors is zero on a described clause.
This is the algebraic component only: padding, answer-block embeddings, decoded
answer strings and the ambient verifier program remain separate obligations.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[PCP answer blocks, honest proof and decoded clauses]
\label{lem:pcp-proof-algebra}
\ledgernode{1.3.3}
\uses{lem:pcp-algebra-tests,def:ld-encoding}
\lean{MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.degreeOf_liftAnswer_le,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.literal_product_degree_le,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.honest_constraint_degree_le,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.typedAccepts_ev_iff,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.exists_proof_of_satisfying_assignment,
  MIPRE.SAT.PcpAlgebra.decoded_clause_of_majority}
\leanok
Embed each of the five answer polynomials in its own coordinate block of the
$m'$-variate point, with distinct sign coordinates outside those blocks.
This preserves individual degree, and for multilinear answers the product of
the five literal factors is multilinear. Consequently a degree-six Boolean
clause-description polynomial and a satisfying five-tuple of Boolean assignments
give a degree-seven PCP proof passing both algebraic tests at every point.
Conversely, for $q\geq82m'$, strict majority acceptance of a degree-seven proof
implies that the Boolean decoding satisfies every described Boolean clause.
These are statements about the existing PCP proof type and its typed evaluations.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Injectivity of each coordinate embedding preserves degrees; the images of
different blocks, and their sign coordinates, are disjoint. A coordinate
therefore contributes degree at most one to the literal product. Encode the
five assignments multilinearly and apply the zero-basis decomposition to the
degree-seven constraint. For soundness, transport the degree bounds to the
ambient point and use Lemma~\ref{lem:pcp-algebra-tests}. Any answer evaluation
equal to its Boolean sign survives the decoding map.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Scope.} Lemma~\ref{lem:pcp-circuit-semantics} supplies the
clause-description polynomial and its relation to the input decider.
Lemma~\ref{lem:pcp-executable-tests} supplies the raw verifier and its runtime.

\begin{theorem}[The bespoke PCP for deciders]
\label{thm:pcp-decider}
\ledgernode{1.3.3}
\uses{lem:decoupled-5sat,lem:schwartz-zippel,def:decider,def:ld-encoding,
  lem:pcp-formula-arithmetization,lem:pcp-circuit-arithmetization,lem:pcp-circuit-evaluator,
  lem:pcp-zero-basis,lem:pcp-algebra-tests,
  lem:pcp-proof-algebra,prop:pcp-padded-describer,lem:pcp-effective-parameters,
  lem:pcp-circuit-semantics,lem:pcp-view-size,lem:pcp-executable-tests,
  lem:effective-binary-field-arithmetic}
\lean{MIPRE.SAT.BinField, MIPRE.SAT.PcpParams, MIPRE.SAT.PcpProof, MIPRE.SAT.PcpProof.ev,
  MIPRE.SAT.ViewFormat, MIPRE.SAT.PcpDecider, MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.classicalPcpDecider}
\leanok
There is a verifier $\pcpverifier$ which, given a valid decider specification $(\decider,
n, T, Q, \sigma, x, y)$ --- validity requiring $\abs{\decider} \leq \sigma$, $\max\{Q,
2\lceil \log n \rceil\} \leq T$ \emph{and} $\abs{x}, \abs{y} \leq Q$, as exact inequalities
--- together with a PCP view $(z, \Xi)$, rejects any invalid specification or misformatted
view, runs in time $\poly(\log T, \log n, Q, \sigma)$ and satisfies:
\begin{enumerate}
\item (\textbf{Completeness}) if $\abs{a},\abs{b}\leq T$ and $\decider$ accepts
  $(n, x, y, a, b)$ within $T$ steps,
  then the low-degree proof with $g_1, g_2$ the low-degree encodings
  (Definition~\ref{def:ld-encoding}) of the padded answers
  is accepted with probability $1$ over uniform $z$;
\item (\textbf{Soundness}) any low-degree proof accepted with probability greater than
  $\frac12$ has $\coded(g_1), \coded(g_2)$ equal to the padded encodings of two strings whose
  prefixes, of length at most $T$, $\decider$ accepts within $T$ steps.
\end{enumerate}
Here the parameters are $(q, m, d, m', s) = \pcpparams(n, T, Q, \sigma)$ with $q = 2^k$ for an
odd $k$, $d = 7$, $m' = 5m + 5 + s$ and $2T \leq 2^m$, computable in time polynomial in the
bit lengths of $(n, T, Q, \sigma)$; and \emph{both} variable counts $m$ and $m'$ of the
arithmetization must be powers of two dividing $q$ (Remark~\ref{rem:pcp-power-of-two}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The verifier checks the arithmetized formula and a certificate that its constraint
polynomial vanishes on the Boolean cube. Soundness against the low-degree proofs in the
statement follows from Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel}. The PCP theorem itself does not run
a quantum low-degree test or consume Pauli-test soundness; those belong to its later
composition with a nonlocal game. Four obligations are separate in the source and stay separate here: the
proof format and query structure, completeness, the zero-testing argument with its error
bounds, and the complexity bound the composition consumes.
\end{proof}

The Lean field family is indexed by odd extension degrees, as required by the parameter
choice. Requiring a field of cardinality $2^k$ for every natural $k$ would include $k=0$
and make the contract uninhabitable. The constructed verifier uses the canonical
polynomial-basis representation and uniform field programs from the proved binary
irreducible-polynomial constructor (Lemma~\ref{lem:binary-uniform-irreducible}).
Its exact axiom guard contains only Lean's standard axioms. Effective self-dual
normal coordinates are supplied by Lemma~\ref{lem:self-dual-basis}; integrating
those coordinates into the downsizing consumers remains a separate obligation.

The theorem has four obligations, and they are separate in the source because they fail
separately.

\effortMedium

\begin{lemma}[The PCP's proof format and query structure]
\label{lem:pcp-format}
\ledgernode{1.3.3.1}
\uses{thm:pcp-decider,lem:decoupled-5sat}
The proof $\Pi$ that $\pcpverifier$ reads is well defined: it consists of the low-degree
encodings $g_1, g_2$ of the two padded answer prefixes, together with the auxiliary
polynomials the zero-test needs, in the stated input formats; and $\pcpverifier$'s queries and
decision rule are determined by them.
\end{lemma}

\effortMedium

\begin{lemma}[PCP completeness]
\label{lem:pcp-completeness}
\ledgernode{1.3.3.2}
\uses{lem:pcp-format,thm:succinct-sat}
\lean{MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.classicalPcp_completeness}
\leanok
If $\abs{a},\abs{b}\leq T$ and the decider accepts $(n, x, y, a, b)$ within $T$ steps,
then the honestly generated proof
$\Pi$ --- the low-degree encodings of the actual answers, with the auxiliary polynomials
computed from them --- makes $\pcpverifier$ accept with certainty.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\leanok
Combine the padded describer's accepting witness, the degree-seven zero certificate,
and the equivalence of raw and typed tests.
\end{proof}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[PCP soundness]
\label{lem:pcp-soundness}
\ledgernode{1.3.3.3}
\uses{lem:pcp-format,lem:schwartz-zippel}
\lean{MIPRE.TM.CookLevin.Pad.classicalPcp_soundness}
\leanok
If $\pcpverifier$ accepts with probability greater than $\tfrac12$ on views of a fixed
proof whose answer and certificate polynomials all have individual degree at most seven,
then the decider accepts the answers the first two polynomials decode to. The entire
decoded answer vectors equal the padded tape encodings of the accepted prefixes.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:schwartz-zippel}
Zero-testing of the constraint polynomial against a zero basis, with
Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel} bounding the probability that a nonzero polynomial vanishes
on a random point. The stated error bounds are what the composition consumes, so a
formalization should carry them rather than an asymptotic form.
\end{proof}

\effortMedium

\begin{lemma}[PCP complexity]
\label{lem:pcp-complexity}
\ledgernode{1.3.3.4}
\uses{lem:pcp-format}
$\pcpverifier$ runs in time $\poly(\log T, \log n, Q, \sigma)$, with circuit-size and
randomness bounds of the same form. These are the bounds the composition of
Remark~\ref{rem:ar-composition} consumes; in particular the randomness bound is what makes the
answer-reduced sampler conditionally linear.
\end{lemma}

\begin{remark}[Why $m$ is a power of two]
\label{rem:pcp-power-of-two}
\uses{thm:pcp-decider,thm:qld}
The hypothesis is not cosmetic and was a confirmed defect in the source. The $m$-variate
low-degree test needs $m \mid q$, and $q$ is a power of two, so $m$ must be one as well.
The padded description guarantees this only for the \emph{outer} variable count $m' = 5m +
5 + s$; the inner $m$, inherited from the succinct-SAT reduction, carries no such
guarantee, which leaves the conditionally linear functions of the axis-parallel and
diagonal lines ill defined and the applications of low-degree soundness with an unverified
hypothesis. The repair is to round $m$ up to a power of two in the padding step, which
costs nothing asymptotically. A formalization must state it as a hypothesis, as
Theorem~\ref{thm:pcp-decider} does.
\end{remark}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Low-degree sandwich support]
\label{lem:ar-sandwich-support}
\ledgernode{1.1.1.3}
\uses{def:cross-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.one_sub_sum_bornProb_ldSandwich_le, MIPRE.sandK, MIPRE.sandK_zero,
  MIPRE.sandK_succ, MIPRE.sandK_snoc, MIPRE.sandK_nonneg, MIPRE.sum_sandK,
  MIPRE.sqrt_sum_xSqNorm_sandK_le, MIPRE.sqrt_sum_xSqNorm_sandStep_le,
  MIPRE.sum_snorm_sq_sandStep_le, MIPRE.snorm_sandStep_le, MIPRE.sandStep_identity,
  MIPRE.snorm_add3_le, MIPRE.sqrt_sum_weighted_sq_add_le, MIPRE.sqrt_sum_weighted_sq_le_add3,
  MIPRE.sum_mul_sum_eq_sum_prod, MIPRE.sqrt_wsum_le_add3, MIPRE.IsPVM.le_one,
  MIPRE.IsPVM.aOp_contraction, MIPRE.IsPVM.bOp_contraction,
  MIPRE.sum_bOp_conjTranspose_mul_self_le_one_of_nonneg, MIPRE.fibSum_comp_equiv,
  MIPRE.fibSum_marg_left, MIPRE.fibSum_snd, MIPRE.fibSum_fibSum,
  MIPRE.sum_sum_bornProb_eq_one, MIPRE.one_sub_sum_bornProb_le_sqrt,
  MIPRE.sum_bornProb_le_fibSum_of_nonneg, MIPRE.one_sub_sum_bornProb_le_eval}
\leanok
Let $k\geq 0$. Let $x$ be drawn from a distribution $\mu$ on a finite set $\mX$ and, independently
of $x$, let $y$ be drawn from a distribution $\nu$ on a finite set $Y$ (uniform in the source).
For each $i\leq k$ let $\cal{G}_i$ be a finite family of functions on $Y$, any two distinct
elements of which agree at $y\sim\nu$ with probability at most $\eps$. Let $\ket{\psi}\in
\C^{d_\alice}\ot\C^{d_\bob}$ be a unit vector, let $\{A^x_{g_1,\ldots,g_k}\}$ be a projective
measurement on $\C^{d_\alice}$ with outcomes in $\cal{G}_1\times\ldots\times\cal{G}_k$, and for
each $i$ let $\{G^{i,x}_g\}_{g\in\cal{G}_i}$ be a projective measurement on $\C^{d_\bob}$. Suppose
that for every $i$ the evaluated $i$th coordinate of $A$ and the evaluated $G^{i,x}$ disagree with
probability at most $\delta$:
\[
  1 - \E_{x,y} \sum_{a} \bra{\psi} A^x_{[g_i(y)=a]} \ot G^{i,x}_{[g(y)=a]} \ket{\psi}
  \;\leq\; \delta\;.
\]
Define the POVM $C^x_{g_1,\ldots,g_k}=G^{k,x}_{g_k}\ldots G^{2,x}_{g_2}G^{1,x}_{g_1}
G^{2,x}_{g_2}\ldots G^{k,x}_{g_k}$, the last index outermost. Then the evaluated tuples disagree
with probability at most $2\sqrt{2}\,k\,(\delta+\eps)^{1/2}$:
\[
  1 - \E_{x,y} \sum_{a_1,\ldots,a_k} \bra{\psi}
    A^x_{[(g_1(y),\ldots,g_k(y))=(a_1,\ldots,a_k)]} \ot
    C^x_{[(g_1(y),\ldots,g_k(y))=(a_1,\ldots,a_k)]} \ket{\psi}
  \;\leq\; 2\sqrt{2}\,k\,(\delta+\eps)^{1/2}\;.
\]
Combined with~\eqref{eq:cross-from-agreement}, the evaluated tuples are
$4\sqrt{2}\,k\,(\delta+\eps)^{1/2}$-close across the parties in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:cross-distance}.
\end{lemma}

\paragraph{Comments.} Both relations are the paper's consistency relation of \texttt{games.tex}
--- a disagreement probability --- and not the squared distance that $\simeq_\delta$ denotes here;
this entry previously stated the hypothesis and the conclusion as squared distances with an
unspecified constant, which is equivalent up to the factor of~\eqref{eq:cross-from-agreement} but
is not how the source states it or how its consumer, Lemma~\ref{lem:ar-giant-sandwich}, uses it.
The source defers the proof to NW19's Fact~4.34, whose import was audited \cite{AuditSync26};
the formalization re-derives it, and the constant $2\sqrt{2}$ it produces is the universal
constant the source leaves implicit. Two generalizations cost nothing and are kept: $y$ may be
drawn from any distribution independent of $x$, with the collision bound stated against it; and
the families are arbitrary finite sets with evaluation maps, so the consumer's alphabets extended
by zero projectors are covered without comment. The collision bound is a hypothesis: for
polynomial families it is Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel}, applied by the consumer.

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{def:cross-distance,lem:commutation-analysis,lem:cool-closeness-fact,lem:pasting-updated,lem:qld-combined-points}
Three steps, of which only the middle one is about sandwiches.

\emph{From evaluations to outcomes.} Fix $i$ and write $\eta$ for the probability that the $i$th
coordinate of $A$ and $G^{i,x}$ return different functions. The Born probabilities do not depend
on $y$, and two different functions are separated at $y$ with probability at least $1-\eps$, so
the evaluated disagreement is at least $(1-\eps)\eta$; since $\eta\leq 1$ this gives
$\eta\leq\delta+\eps$ with no case split. Both measurements are projective, so their squared
distance across the parties is exactly $2\eta$.

\emph{One layer at a time.} Split the outcome as $(o, g)$ with $g$ the last coordinate. Alice's
element is the palindrome $A_g A_o A_g$ of her two marginals, and three replacements move it to
Bob's $G_g C_o G_g$: the right $A_g$, then $A_o$, then the left $A_g$. Each multiplies a known
deviation by a family $F$ with $\sum F^\dagger F\leq\Id$ --- the joint projections, the product of
the contraction $A_g$ with the projective $G_g$, and $G_g C_o$ with $\sum_o C_o^2\leq\Id$ --- so each
costs exactly the deviation it multiplies (the paper's \texttt{fact:add-a-proj}). The three costs
are added as square roots, by Minkowski's inequality in $\ell^2$ over the index and the outcomes:
each layer adds twice the square root of its coordinate's squared distance, and by induction on
$k$ the square root of the outcome-level squared distance between $A$ and $C$ is at most
$2\sum_i (2(\delta+\eps))^{1/2}$. The cruder $(a+b+c)^2\leq 3(a^2+b^2+c^2)$ would lose a factor
$3$ at every layer, exponential in $k$.

\emph{Back to agreement, then evaluation.} Against a projective measurement,
$1-\sum_g\bra{\psi}A_g\ot C_g\ket{\psi}$ is a sum of cross terms against the deviations and is at
most the square root of the squared distance, whatever $C$ is; Jensen moves the average over $x$
inside the root. Evaluating both sides at $y$ merges outcomes, and since every Born probability
involved is nonnegative, merging can only add agreement.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Truncation at the timeout counter]
\label{lem:timeout-truncation-interface}
\ledgernode{1.4.1.4}
\uses{rem:tm-conventions}
For a source decider in standard timeout-counter form with $B=B_D(n)$,
simultaneously replacing its two answers by their length-$B$ prefixes
preserves the result: accept, reject, or timeout. The counter depends only
on $n$ and the underlying machine is simulated for at most $B$ steps, so
none of the changed cells can be read. This is not the false assertion
$\TIME_D(n)\leq B$; wrapper runtime may be polynomial in $B$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Effective typed oracle construction]
\label{lem:oracle-effective-interface}
\ledgernode{1.3.5.1}
\uses{thm:oracularization,lem:oracle-typed-sampler,lem:oracle-decider-time}
\lean{MIPRE.OracleSampler.samplerProgFun, MIPRE.OracleSampler.oracleSampler_timeBound,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.deciderProgFun, MIPRE.OracleDecider.oracleDecider_timeBound,
  MIPRE.OracleDecider.core_timeBound}
\leanok
The typed oracle verifier is compiled in time polynomial in the input
description. Its sampler preserves $\ell$ levels, depends only on the
input sampler, and runs in $\poly(\TIME_S(n))$. Its decider runs in
$\poly(\TIME_S(n),B_D(n))$, including the bounded format checks.
This is the typed construction used inside answer reduction.
In the ambient model the decider's running time is polynomial in the budget of its index
routine, and a budget polynomial in the running times of the input verifier covers the core
(Lemma~\ref{lem:oracle-decider-time}).
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Timeout-compatible oracle PCC completeness]
\label{lem:oracle-timeout-pcc}
\ledgernode{1.3.5.2}
\uses{lem:timeout-truncation-interface,thm:oracularization,def:pcc}
Coarse-grain a perfect input PCC witness by length-$B_D(n)$ prefix
truncation. Orthogonal projector sums preserve completeness and projectivity;
commutation and consistency are preserved by the common deterministic map.
The preceding truncation lemma preserves value one on the same state.
These measurements supply the honest isolated-player and joint-oracle
measurements with legal answer lengths and the same combined operator rule
for both players.
\end{lemma}

The following answer-reduction obligations use the parameter routine and
input hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:answer-reduction}. In measurement
relations, ``consistent'' means close in the cross-party squared distance
of Definition~\ref{def:cross-distance}; bounds on game failure are stated
separately. Put $\theta=16^{|T^{\mathrm{ar}}|}\eps_{\mathrm{out}}$,
$E=\delta_{\mathrm{CL}}(\theta,q,m',d,m'+6)$ and $\rho=m'd/q$.
The common upper bound $\kappa$ below absorbs both the individual and
simultaneous errors; it is not a renaming of the source's individual
$\delta_1$ or simultaneous $\delta_2$.

\begin{lemma}[Answer-reduction construction]
\label{lem:ar-construction}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.1}
\uses{lem:oracle-effective-interface,thm:pcp-decider,lem:typed-downsize,lem:detype-compiler,lem:lidt-effective}
With the parameter and timeout hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:answer-reduction},
construct the typed oracle verifier and the PCP verifier. Downsize the PCP
sampler using its explicit field routine before taking the direct sum with
the binary oracle sampler. This yields a common binary ambient space and
$\max\{\ell,3\}$ typed levels, hence $\max\{\ell+2,5\}$ levels after detyping.
The compiled timeout and actual runtimes have the claimed polynomial bounds.
Format checks include all proof coefficients and answer components.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Answer-reduction completeness]
\label{lem:ar-completeness}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.2}
\uses{lem:ar-construction,lem:oracle-timeout-pcc,lem:detype-completeness}
A perfect PCC input witness gives a perfect typed answer-reduction witness,
using the complete honest proof tuple and one combined measurement rule on
both players. All queried formats are satisfied; mismatched proof branches
use a whole legal zero tuple. Apply detyping completeness, including its
identical-operator property, to obtain the exterior PCC witness.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Typed soundness setup]
\label{lem:ar-soundness-setup}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.3}
\uses{lem:ar-construction,lem:detype-soundness}
An exterior strategy with deficit $\eps_{\mathrm{out}}$ gives a typed strategy
with deficit $\theta=16^{|T^{\mathrm{ar}}|}\eps_{\mathrm{out}}$ on the same
state. Symmetrize the typed strategy for its symmetric game; do not assume
the detyped question law is symmetric. The soundness proof then works with
projective, well-formatted measurements and a swap-invariant state.
All type-pair conditioning costs are constants since $T^{\mathrm{ar}}$ is fixed.
\end{lemma}

\begin{definition}[Full-seed indexing for answer reduction]
\label{def:ar-seed-index}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.3.1}
\uses{lem:ar-soundness-setup}
The common index $\xi$ is the full uniform oracle seed. Both questions
$(x_A,x_B)$ and every auxiliary oracle coordinate are functions of $\xi$.
Indexed measurements and all subsequent averages retain $\xi$.
The image pair $(x_A,x_B)$ is not substituted as an index without proving
injectivity or specifying its conditional seed distribution.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Global PCP consistency tests]
\label{lem:ar-global-tests}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.4}
\uses{def:ar-seed-index,def:cross-distance}
Conditioning the typed success estimate on each checked type pair gives the
global PCP subtests with $O(\theta)$ failure, averaged over uniform $\xi$
and the prescribed PCP seed. Retain the joint proof-tuple format, individual
proof-line formats, and all identical-question equality subtests. No
low-degree theorem is applied to line--point success alone.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Input-codeword consistency tests]
\label{lem:ar-input-tests}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.5}
\uses{lem:ar-global-tests}
The two input-codeword tests, conditioned on their respective typed questions,
have $O(\theta)$ failure on the required seeded point/line laws. The
distributions are conditioned before averaging over $\xi$, and include
answer formats and equality tests. These supply both input evaluation
relations consumed by the individual-polynomial extraction.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Common individual polynomial measurements]
\label{lem:ar-common-polynomials}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.6}
\uses{lem:ar-input-tests,thm:lidt-cl-soundness-one,lem:schwartz-zippel}
For each seed, extract the input and proof polynomial PVMs, averaged with
the source's conditional question laws. One common family $G_i$ can be used
on both copies, for $i=1,\ldots,5$. To derive this, choose one side's LDT
output, use swap invariance of the state to transport its point relation,
then point self-consistency and polynomial separation to obtain cross-copy
consistency; average the resulting errors using Jensen. Identical point
operators alone do not make the two existential LDT outputs identical.
The resulting evaluation and full-polynomial consistency error is denoted
$\kappa=O(\sqrt E+\rho)$ with $E,\rho$ as below.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[A simultaneous complete proof tuple]
\label{lem:ar-simultaneous-proof}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.7}
\uses{lem:ar-common-polynomials,thm:lidt-cl-soundness}
Use the simultaneous theorem at $(q,m',d,m'+6)$ to obtain a projective
measurement $J$ of the entire tuple $(f_1,\ldots,f_5,c_0,\ldots,c_{m'})$.
Write $M_i$ for individual point measurements, and $M_6$ for the complete
proof-tuple point measurement. With seed averaging understood, retain all
five relations: $M_i(y_i)$ is cross-party consistent with the $i$th
evaluation coordinate of $M_6(z)$ for $i=3,4,5$ with error $O(\theta)$;
$M_i(y)$ is consistent with $G_i$ evaluated at $y$; the two copies of $G_i$
are consistent; $M_6(z)$ is consistent with $J$ evaluated at $z$; and the
two copies of the complete $J$ are consistent. The last four errors are
$O(\kappa)$. Include player mirrors of the first, second and fourth.
Individual evaluations without a projective complete-tuple output do not
suffice for the next lemma.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[The answer-reduction giant sandwich]
\label{lem:ar-giant-sandwich}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.8}
\uses{lem:ar-simultaneous-proof,lem:ar-sandwich-support,lem:schwartz-zippel}
Embed each of the five scalar polynomial alphabets into the full
individual-degree-$d$ alphabet on $m'$ variables by zero-projector extension.
Use the $(m'+1)$-tuple of constraint polynomials as a sixth alphabet; the
complete $J$ now has literally the product of the six alphabets as outcomes.
For either scalars or tuples, unequal outcomes agree at a uniform point
with probability at most $\rho=m'd/q$: one unequal component witnesses
the tuple bound, with no tuple-length factor.
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:ar-sandwich-support} with the full seed as index and
the evaluation point uniform independently of it. Reverse the six-index
order so the constraint-tuple marginal of $J$ is innermost. The resulting
giant sandwich has error
$\delta_3=O(\sqrt{\kappa+\rho})=O((\sqrt E+\rho)^{1/2})$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Transferring to the polynomial strategy]
\label{lem:ar-polynomial-strategy}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.9}
\uses{lem:ar-giant-sandwich}
The sandwiched polynomial measurements define the intermediate strategy on
the same state. Transfer acceptance through the complete tuple $J$,
including its constraint polynomials and tested formats, to obtain success
$1-O(\sqrt{\delta_3})$. The proof uses the full-tuple relations of
Lemma~\ref{lem:ar-simultaneous-proof}, not independently chosen evaluations.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Decoding with the original answer cutoff]
\label{lem:ar-decoding}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.10}
\uses{lem:ar-polynomial-strategy,lem:timeout-truncation-interface,thm:oracularization}
Decode the polynomial strategy to the oracle strategy, applying the
length-$B_D(n)$ prefix cutoff to each original answer. It preserves the
simulated decider's output both before and after the oracle construction,
and makes every decoded answer pass its bounded parser. Check all three
oracle subtests. Group malformed or failed decodings into fixed legal
outcomes so measurements remain complete. The final conversion to the
input game yields deficit $O(\delta_3^{1/4})$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Answer-reduction quantitative assembly]
\label{lem:ar-error-assembly}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.11}
\uses{lem:ar-decoding,lem:ar-soundness-setup,lem:ar-common-polynomials}
Put $L=(\lambda n)^\mu$, $\rho=m'd/q$ and
$E=\delta_{\mathrm{CL}}(\theta,q,m',d,m'+6)$ for the source parameter
routine. The comparison with the smaller-$m$ LDT error uses its field-error
floor $E>4^{-b'}/L$ and the bound
$a'(dm)^{a'}2^{-b'md}\leq C/L$ for sufficiently large $n$, with
$m\geq\lambda\mu n$ and $d=7$. Here $a',b'$ are the fixed seeded-LDT
constants; the polynomial prefactor is absorbed into the exponential.
It does not reverse the exponential inequality when $m'\geq m$.
The quantitative chain is
\[
 \kappa=O(\sqrt E+\rho),\qquad
 \delta_3=O((\sqrt E+\rho)^{1/2}),\qquad
 \mathrm{deficit}_{\mathrm{in}}=O(E^{1/16}+\rho^{1/8}).
\]
Choose a universal final exponent strictly below $\min\{b'/16,1/16\}$
and enlarge the polynomial coefficient and threshold to absorb every
constant. This gives the displayed error of Theorem~\ref{thm:answer-reduction}.
When that error is at least one the value conclusion is trivial; the
nontrivial regime also justifies the small-error inequalities above.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Answer-reduction sampler independence]
\label{lem:ar-sampler-independence}
\ledgernode{1.3.4.12}
\uses{lem:ar-construction,lem:oracle-effective-interface,lem:detype-compiler}
The output sampler is determined by $(S,\lambda,\ell,\mu,\sigma)$, independent
of the input decider's code. The typed oracle sampler uses only $S$; PCP
parameters use only the supplied numerical bounds; downsizing uses their
explicit field routine; and detyping's sampler uses only its sampler and
fixed graph. This supplies the source premise used by compression's
sampler-independence argument (ledger node $1.5.1$). The already checked
conditional compression theorem keeps its existing Lean proof and premises.
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Answer reduction]
\label{thm:answer-reduction}
\ledgernode{1.3, 1.3.4}
\uses{def:normal-verifier,def:pcc,lem:ar-construction,lem:ar-completeness,lem:ar-error-assembly,lem:ar-sampler-independence}
In the source model there is a polynomial-time Turing machine $\ComputeAnsVerifier$ that on input
$(\verifier, \lambda, \ell, \mu, \sigma)$, for $\verifier$ an $\ell$-level normal form
verifier whose decider is in standard timeout-counter form, with
$\abs{\decider} \leq \sigma$, $\TIME_\sampler(n) \leq (\lambda n)^\mu$
and $\TIME_\decider(n) \leq (2^{\lambda n})^\mu$ for $n \geq 2$, outputs a
$\max\{\ell + 2, 5\}$-level normal form verifier $\vans$ whose decider is in standard
timeout-counter form with $B_{\dans}(n)=\poly(\sigma,(\lambda n)^\mu)$, with
$\TIME_{\sans}(n), \TIME_{\dans}(n) = \poly\bigl( (\lambda n)^\mu, \sigma \bigr)$,
whose sampler depends only on $(\sampler, \lambda, \ell, \mu, \sigma)$, and such that for
some $\delta(\eps, n) = \sigma^a \bigl( (\lambda n)^{\mu a} \eps^b + (\lambda
n)^{-\mu b} \bigr)$ (universal constants $a, b$) and a universal constant
$C_{\mathrm{ar}}$, for all $n \geq C_{\mathrm{ar}}$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item (\textbf{Completeness}) If $\verifier_n$ has a value-$1$ PCC strategy
  then $\vans_n$ has a value-$1$ PCC strategy.
\item (\textbf{Soundness}) If $\valstar(\vans_n) > 1 - \eps$ then
  $\valstar(\verifier_n) \geq 1 - \delta(\eps, n)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{definition}[Ambient answer-reduction contract]
\label{def:answer-reduction-contract}
\uses{def:normal-verifier,def:pcc}
\lean{MIPRE.AnswerReduction}
\leanok
The structure \texttt{MIPRE.AnswerReduction} is an ambient contract,
parametrized by $\ell$, its output at $\max\{\ell + 2, 5\}$ levels. The hypotheses on the
input are the budget \texttt{AnswerReduction.inBudget}: sampler and decider runtime
coefficients at most $(\lambda n + 1)^\mu$ and $2^{(\lambda n + 1)^\mu}$, respectively,
both at input-size degree $\mu$, questions of dimension at most
the sampler's bound and answers rejected beyond the decider's, together with $\abs{\decider}
\leq \sigma$. For a universal base polynomial $P$ and base degree $d$, the
output is within $P((\lambda n+1)^\mu+\sigma)^{\mu+1}$ at input-size degree
$d(\mu+1)$, and rejects longer answers. The extra power retains the input
sampler's degree; fixing $\mu$ in compression leaves a polynomial bound. Its threshold $C_{\mathrm{ar}}$
plays the role of the source threshold (\texttt{thm:ar} in \texttt{ld\_compiler.tex}); an earlier statement here had the
value clauses for all $n$. Its soundness field also explicitly requires $n\geq2$,
$\lambda\geq1$ and $\eps>0$. Completeness transfers ambient \texttt{HasPerfectPCC}
from input answer cut $2^{(\lambda n+1)^\mu}$ to the output budget's answer cut.
Soundness transfers output value greater than $1-\eps$ to input value at least
$1-\delta(\eps,n)$ at those cuts, with
